But the two hands which in the aforesaid throne hold the seat on this side and on that ought not unfittingly to be understood as the clemency and mercy of our 514CSaviour; which wrought this, that for the redemption of the human race Christ should take flesh, and should for a time taste the death which he utterly knew not. Whence also the prophet David says: He shall drink of the torrent in the way, therefore shall he lift up the head (Psal. CIX). These, I say, are the spiritual breasts of the Bridegroom in the Canticle of Canticles, the two hands which hold the seat of the golden and ivory throne in the book of Kings, namely clemency and mercy, which brought it about that God should become man, and should wonderfully lead us back to the joys of paradise which we lost through Adam.

Let us see finally what that is which the text of the history rehearsed to us above: Beside, he says, each several hand 514Dtwo lions stood (III Reg. X). Indeed by manifest reason we understand from the preceding discourse that there were no more hands there than two, and if two lions were assigned to each several hand, we hold for certain that there were not more than four lions only attending there. What therefore do the four lions signify, which are set beside each several hand, but the four gospels which everywhere preach the clemency and mercy of God? For thus thou hast it written in the Gospel: He that believeth and is baptized shall be saved (Marc. XVI). And in another place: Do penance, the kingdom of heaven is at hand (Matth. IV). The twelve little lions which the sacred history relates to have been upon the six steps signify the fulness and perfection 515Aof the spiritual gifts, which no one doubts to have rested and remained upon Christ. In Christ, as the Apostle says, dwelleth all the fulness of the divinity corporeally (Coloss. II). And of his fulness, says John, we have all received (Joan. I). Meanwhile, if by the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit which Isaias relates to have rested upon Christ, and by the virtues which holy Church chiefly commends, we wish to understand these twelve little lions, our understanding, even among the enemies of the truth, will be judged worthy of worthy praise. For thus says Isaias: There shall come forth a rod out of the root of Jesse, and a flower shall rise up out of his root, and the spirit of wisdom and of understanding shall rest upon him, the spirit of counsel and of fortitude, the spirit of knowledge and of godliness, and the spirit of the fear of the Lord shall fill him 515B(Isa. XI) [Vulg. Isa. 11:2 has spiritus Domini before spiritus sapientiae et intellectus]. If therefore to these seven gifts of the Holy Spirit thou shalt add five virtues, namely faith, hope, charity, good work, and chastity, thou wilt beyond doubt fill up the number of twelve. For seven and five, when they come together into one, undoubtedly make up the number of twelve. These spiritual little lions therefore, as I judge, are of a better and worthier price than those which Solomon caused to stand upon the six steps in the midst of the golden and ivory throne.

After these things, the history relates, as it were under a certain wonder, that there was no such work made in any kingdoms (III Reg. X). And well indeed and justly enough did the history relate this. For there was no such work made in any kingdoms as is that which 515Cthe Word was made flesh, and dwelt among us (Joan. I). There was not, I say, such a work made in any kingdoms, that God should be made man, and should redeem with his blood the human race which had perished. Truly, I say, there was not so glorious a work made in any kingdoms, that our nature, already utterly despaired of as to salvation, should be drawn away from the lower regions and set in heaven at the right hand of God the Father.

301 RECAPITULATIO A SUPERIORIBUS. DE THRONO AUREO ET EBURNEO QUEM FECIT SALOMON, QUOMODO DEBEAT REFERRI AD PERSONAM SANCTAE ECCLESIAE.

Omnia fere sacramentalia verba quae in Scripturis divinitus inspiratis adumbrata reperiuntur, aliquando 515Dquidem ad caput nostrum, qui est Christus, referuntur, aliquando vero universaliter sanctae Ecclesiae quae est corpus ejusdem capitis nostri, debent assignari, aliquando singulis quibusque fidelibus, qui sunt membra praedicti capitis et corporis, non inconvenienter possunt aptari.

Quod enim ita sit, sciamus pro certo quia sicut thronus eburneus, de quo saepe jam locuti sumus, Domino Jesu Christo convenit, sic consequenter sanctae Ecclesiae, quae est corpus ipsius, congruit. Sed quia jam prout facultas ingenii nostri suppetiit, supra dictum thronum capiti nostro Christo allegorice assignavimus, restat nunc ut eumdem corpori nostro sanctae Ecclesiae allegorico sensu ascribamus. 516AFecit, inquit, Salomon thronum de ebore grandem (III Reg. X). Quia enim Salomon interpretatur pacificus, non immerito per Salomonem accipitur Dominus Jesus Christus. De quo nimirum Paulus apostolus dicit: Ipse est pax nostra qui fecit utraque unum (Ephes. II.) Iste rex ergo pacificus noster fecit thronum eburneum, quia sicut divina pagina testatur: Sapientia aedificavit sibi domum (Prov. IX), et quia sancta Ecclesia in omnibus documentis, suis castitatem maxime observandam praedicat, eleganter quoque thronum illum, qui sanctam Ecclesiam praefiguravit, ex ebore conditum esse constat. Ebur enim os est elephantis; quod animal tantae fertur esse frigiditatis ut aut raro, aut semel in anno, ut arbitramur, vix accedat ad jura persolvenda coitus 516Bnaturalis. Quod utique sanctae Ecclesiae sacramentis decenter alludit, quae nonnullis ex filiis suis conjugalia opera funditus interdicit, aliquibus vero ob reparandam successionem humanae prolis conjugales actus indulgendo concedit. Est praeterea aliud sacramentum quod de elephante et de dracone dici solet, quod sine dubio sanctae Ecclesiae specialiter assignari debet: draeo quippe, ut ait beatus Augustinus, serpens vel animal est duodecim cubitorum longitudinis, qui idcirco elephantem prosequitur, quia illius sanguis est ei potus valde delectabilis. Quapropter quando vult eum interficere ut sanguinem ejus quem desiderat, delectabiliter valeat epotare, longitudine corporis et caudae, elephantis pedes illaqueat, ne elephas usquam aut divertere, 516Caut procedere queat. Sicque miro modo fit, ut dum elephas illaqueatis pedibus super draconem constrictus obruitur, ipsum draconem mole sui corporis deprimat et occidat qui ei mortem inferre moliebatur. Quid ergo significat iste elephas, nisi sanctam Ecclesiam? Et quid draco ille, nisi diabolum qui assiduis machinationibus persequitur eamdem sanctam Ecclesiam? Unde et in Apocalypsi sua Joannes apostolus dicit: Et draco persecutus est mulierem, et mulier fugit in solitudinem. Et misit draco flumen post mulierem ut faceret eam trahi a flumine (Apoc. XII). Haec est autem illa mulier, scilicet sancta Ecclesia, de qua per Salomonem dicitur: Mulierem fortem quis inveniet? (Prov. XXXI.) Sciendum praeterea quia sancta Ecclesia quae appellatur Sponsa et columba et 516Damica in Canticis canticorum (cap. II), ipsa dicitur thronus eburneus in libro Regum. Et quam Salomon vocat mulierem fortem, illam Joannes in Apocalypsi nominat mulierem in solitudine fugientem. Ipsa est denique domus quam aedificavit sapientia super septem columnas subsistentem. Quod autem dicitur, quia thronus ille quem fecit Salomon de ebore, grandis exstitit; nullatenus hoc sanctae Ecclesiae contrarium existit. Ipsa est enim quae in capite suo scilicet Christo coeli altitudinem penetravit, et pedibus suis videlicet apostolis, subvecta usque ad extrema totius mundi pervenit. Quod utique bene in Daniele propheta praefiguratur, quando impiissimo regi Nabuchodonosor sub specie cujusdam magnae 517Aarboris per visionem sancta Ecclesia demonstratur. Sicut ibi scriptum est: Vidi, et ecce quaedam magna arbor erat in medio terrae, cujus proceritas pertingebat usque ad coelum, et rami illius dilatabantur usque ad terminos terrae (Dan. IV.) Subter eam habitabant bestiae agri, et in ramis ejus morabantur volucres coeli, et cibus omnium erat in arbore illa, et ex illa vescebatur omnis caro. Arbor igitur in medio terrae sancta Ecclesia est, de qua dicitur quia operatus est Deus salutem in medio terrae (Psal. LXXIII). Proceritas ejus quae usque ad coelum pertingit, caput sanctae Ecclesiae est Christus, de quo Paulus dicit: Habemus pontificem magnum Christum Jesum qui penetravit coelos (Hebr. IV). Rami vero illius sunt Apostoli quibus Dominus aiebat: Ego sum vitis, vos 517Bpalmites estis (Joan. XV). Unde bene dicitur quod rami illius usque ad terminos terrae dilatabantur, quia in omnem terram exivit sonus apostolorum, et in fines orbis terrae verba eorum (Psal. XVIII). Quod autem Propheta subjunxit, quia sub terra habitant bestiae agri, et in ramis ejus morabantur 302 volucres coeli, per bestias, homines bestiales possunt accipi, et per volucres, spirituales viri debent intelligi. Illi enim utpote animales, infimum in sancta Ecclesia obtinent locum isti vero. veluti spirituales ad sublimia seipsos provehunt pennis virtutum. Cibus vero omnium qui in illa arbore fuisse refertur evangelica praedicatio est, videlicet refectio animarum fidelium, quae in Ecclesia praedicatur. Et quoniam quidem in fine hujus visionis subnectitur quod 517Comnis caro ex illa arbore vescebatur, manifeste datur intelligi quia omnis caro quae debet in die judicii salvari, necesse est ut secundum Evangelium Christi vivat, et a sancta Ecclesia nullatenus recedat. Quod autem Regum historia subintulit, quia Salomon thronum quem fecerat auro radiante vestierit, nequaquam a sacramentis ecclesiasticis nos repellit: Sancta enim Ecclesia auro clarissimo est vestita, quia omni sapientiae ornatu est decorata. Per aurum quippe sapientia designatur, sicut per quemdam sapientem dicitur: Sapientia occulta et thesaurus absconditus, quae utilitas est in utrisque? (Eccli. XL.) Praeterea si per aurum, spiritualium virtutum ornamenta intelligere volumus, quomodo Psalmista 517Dnostro intellectui occurrat, mente devota audiamus. Omnis gloria, inquit, filiae regis ab intus in fimbriis aureis circumamicta varietatibus (Psal. XXXXIV). Sex autem gradus qui in supradicto throno erant, sex gradus ecclesiasticos qui in sancta Ecclesia sunt, significant; scilicet lectores, ostiarios, acolythos, subdiaconos, levitas, presbyteros. Sicut enim per illos sex gradus usque ad sedile throni ascendebatur, sic nimirum his sex gradibus ecclesiasticis ad sacrosanctum altare pertingitur. Quod vero liber Regum refert quia thronus ille rotundus fuerit, hoc significat, quoniam sancta Ecclesia ascendendo de virtute in virtutem semper ad perfectionem tendit. Unde in Canticis canticorum dicitur: Venter tuus sicut crater tornatilis (cap. VII). Quidquid 518Aenim torno efficitur, liquido constat quia perfectius humano visui exhibetur. Duae autem manus quae tenent sedile. divinitas et humanitas Christi sunt, quae in sancta Ecclesia nobis sacrosanctum Altare construunt. Nisi enim Deus homo fieret, nequaquam caro ejus in altari sancto quotidie immolaretur. Et nisi ipse Deus homo fieret verus Deus, nullatenus cum Patre et Spiritu sancto pari honore adoraretur. Quatuor leones qui ibi juxta manus stabant, quatuor virtutes significant, scilicet justitiam, fortitudinem, prudentiam, temperantiam, quae in sancta Ecclesia triumphale signum obtinent. Duodecim leunculi qui stabant super sex gradus hinc atque inde, duodecim sunt apostoli, videlicet praedicatores sanctae Ecclesiae. Quia enim leones fortia 518Banimalia sunt et regiae dignitatis, et auctoritatis typum gerunt, non immerito per leones Apostoli intelliguntur, quorum regia dignitas, et regalis auctoritas quatuor per climata mundi praedicatur. In conclusione autem sententiae hujus, Regum historia subjunxit, quia tale opus non est factum in universis regnis. Verum equidem dicit Regum historia quoniam opus tam admirabile alicubi non est factum sicuti est sancta Ecclesia, quae filiis suis per fidem et operationem quodidie patefacit regna coelestia.

301 RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING. ON THE GOLDEN AND IVORY THRONE WHICH SOLOMON MADE, HOW IT OUGHT TO BE REFERRED TO THE PERSON OF HOLY CHURCH.

Almost all the sacramental words which are found overshadowed in the divinely inspired Scriptures are sometimes 515Dindeed referred to our head, who is Christ; sometimes, however, they ought to be assigned universally to holy Church, which is the body of that same head of ours; sometimes they can not unfittingly be fitted to each several one of the faithful, who are the members of the aforesaid head and body.

That this is so, let us know for certain, that as the ivory throne, of which we have already often spoken, befits the Lord Jesus Christ, so consequently it agrees with holy Church, which is his body. But since already, so far as the faculty of our wit sufficed, we have allegorically assigned the aforesaid throne to our head, Christ, it remains now that we ascribe the same, in an allegorical sense, to our body, holy Church. 516ASolomon made, he says, a great throne of ivory (III Reg. X). For because Solomon is interpreted peaceable, not undeservedly is the Lord Jesus Christ taken by Solomon. Of whom indeed Paul the apostle says: He is our peace, who hath made both one (Ephes. II.) This our peaceable king therefore made an ivory throne, because, as the divine page testifies: Wisdom hath built herself a house (Prov. IX); and because holy Church in all her teachings preaches that chastity is chiefly to be kept, it is elegantly established also that that throne, which prefigured holy Church, was built of ivory. For ivory is the bone of the elephant, which beast is said to be of so great coldness that either rarely, or once in the year, as we judge, it scarcely comes to pay the dues of natural coupling. 516BWhich indeed becomingly alludes to the sacraments of holy Church, which utterly forbids conjugal works to not a few of her sons, but to some grants conjugal acts by indulgence, for the repairing of the succession of human offspring. There is besides another sacrament which is wont to be told of the elephant and of the dragon, which without doubt ought specially to be assigned to holy Church: for the draeo, as blessed Augustine says, is a serpent or beast of twelve cubits in length, which pursues the elephant for this cause, that its blood is a drink very delightful to him. Wherefore, when he wills to kill him, that he may delightfully drink up the blood which he desires, with the length of his body and tail he ensnares the feet of the elephant, that the elephant may not be able anywhere to turn aside 516Cor to go forward. And so it comes about in a wondrous manner that, while the elephant, his feet ensnared, is overthrown and pressed down upon the dragon, he crushes and slays with the mass of his own body that very dragon which was contriving to bring death upon him. What then does this elephant signify, but holy Church? And what that dragon, but the devil, who with assiduous machinations persecutes that same holy Church? Whence also in his Apocalypse John the apostle says: And the dragon persecuted the woman, and the woman fled into the wilderness. And the dragon sent a river after the woman, that he might cause her to be carried away by the river (Apoc. XII) [Vulg. Apoc. 12:15 et misit serpens ex ore suo post mulierem aquam tamquam flumen, "the serpent... water as it were a river"]. And this is that woman, namely holy Church, of whom it is said through Solomon: Who shall find a valiant woman? (Prov. XXXI.) It is besides to be known that holy Church, which is called the Bride and the dove and the 516Dbeloved in the Canticle of Canticles (cap. II), is herself called the ivory throne in the book of Kings. And her whom Solomon calls a valiant woman, John in the Apocalypse names a woman fleeing into the wilderness. She is finally the house which wisdom built, standing upon seven pillars. But as to what is said, that that throne which Solomon made of ivory was great, this is in no wise contrary to holy Church. For she it is who in her head, namely Christ, has penetrated the height of heaven, and, borne up by her feet, namely the apostles, has come even to the utmost bounds of the whole world. Which indeed is well prefigured in the prophet Daniel, when to that most impious king Nabuchodonosor holy Church is shown by a vision under the appearance of a certain great 517Atree. As it is written there: I saw, and behold a certain great tree was in the midst of the earth, whose height reached unto heaven, and its branches were spread out unto the ends of the earth (Dan. IV.) [Vulg. Dan. 4:8 aspectus illius erat usque ad terminos universae terrae, "the sight thereof was unto the ends of the whole earth"; the branches enter only at v. 9] Beneath it dwelt the beasts of the field, and in its branches abode the birds of the air, and the food of all was in that tree, and of it all flesh did eat. The tree therefore in the midst of the earth is holy Church, of which it is said that God hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth (Psal. LXXIII). Its height, which reaches unto heaven, is the head of holy Church, Christ, of whom Paul says: We have a great high priest, Christ Jesus, who hath penetrated the heavens (Hebr. IV) [Vulg. Hebr. 4:14 pontificem magnum qui penetravit caelos, Iesum Filium Dei, "Jesus the Son of God"]. But its branches are the Apostles, to whom the Lord said: I am the vine, you 517Bare the branches (Joan. XV). Whence well is it said that its branches were spread out unto the ends of the earth, because into all the earth hath gone forth the sound of the apostles, and their words unto the ends of the world (Psal. XVIII). But as to what the Prophet subjoined, that under the earth dwell the beasts of the field, and in its branches abode 302 the birds of the air: by the beasts, bestial men can be taken, and by the birds, spiritual men ought to be understood. For the former, as being animal, obtain the lowest place in holy Church the latter however. as being spiritual, bear themselves up to lofty things on the wings of the virtues. But the food of all which is related to have been in that tree is the evangelical preaching, namely the refreshment of faithful souls, which is preached in the Church. And since indeed at the end of this vision it is added that 517Call flesh did eat of that tree, it is manifestly given to be understood that all flesh which is to be saved in the day of judgment must needs live according to the Gospel of Christ, and in no wise depart from holy Church. But as to what the history of Kings brought in besides, that Solomon clothed with radiant gold the throne which he had made, this in no wise drives us back from the ecclesiastical sacraments: for holy Church is clothed with the brightest gold, because she is adorned with every ornament of wisdom. For by gold wisdom is designated, as it is said through a certain wise man: Wisdom that is hid, and treasure that is not seen, what profit is there in them both? (Eccli. XL.) [Vulg. Eccli. 41:17 sapientia absconsa et thesaurus invisus] Moreover, if by gold we wish to understand the ornaments of the spiritual virtues, let us hear with devout mind how the Psalmist 517Dmeets our understanding. All the glory, he says, of the king's daughter is within, in golden borders, clothed round about with varieties (Psal. XXXXIV). But the six steps which were in the aforesaid throne signify the six ecclesiastical degrees which are in holy Church, namely readers, doorkeepers, acolytes, subdeacons, levites, priests. For as by those six steps one ascended unto the seat of the throne, so indeed by these six ecclesiastical degrees one comes to the most holy altar. But as to what the book of Kings relates, that that throne was round, this signifies that holy Church, ascending from virtue to virtue, ever tends toward perfection. Whence in the Canticle of Canticles it is said: Thy belly is like a turned bowl (cap. VII) [Vulg. Cant. 7:2 umbilicus tuus crater tornatilis... venter tuus sicut acervus tritici, "thy navel is a turned bowl... thy belly as a heap of wheat"]. For whatever 518Ais wrought upon the lathe, it plainly stands that it is presented more perfectly to human sight. But the two hands which hold the seat. are the divinity and the humanity of Christ, which in holy Church build for us the most holy Altar. For unless God were made man, his flesh would in no wise be daily offered upon the holy altar. And unless he himself, God, became man true God, he would in no wise be adored with the Father and the Holy Spirit in equal honour. The four lions which stood there beside the hands signify the four virtues, namely justice, fortitude, prudence, temperance, which in holy Church obtain the triumphal token. The twelve little lions which stood upon the six steps on this side and on that are the twelve apostles, namely the preachers of holy Church. For because lions are strong 518Banimals and bear the type of royal dignity and authority, not undeservedly are the Apostles understood by the lions, whose royal dignity and kingly authority is preached through the four quarters of the world. But in the conclusion of this sentence, the history of Kings subjoined that such a work was not made in any kingdoms. Truly indeed does the history of Kings say it, since so wonderful a work has nowhere been made as is holy Church, which quodidie lays open to her sons, through faith and working, the heavenly kingdoms.

RECAPITULATIO A SUPERIORIBUS DE THRONO EBURNEO, QUOMODO DEBEAT REFERRI AD MORALITEM.

Qui mores instruere vult in hominibus, debet 518Cipse esse instructus suis moribus. Sed quia hoc difficile reperitur ut aliquis ex omni parte bonis moribus adornetur, non tamen prorsus ab aedificatione morum cessare debemus, quia hoc ex auctoritate divinorum eloquiorum obtinemus, quod semper mens nostra debet bonis moribus et operibus intenta esse, et lingua laudes sui conditoris infatigabili devotione omnibus propalare. Quicunque scripturam illam legis vel audis quae hoc dicit quomodo Salomon thronum de ebore grandem fecerit, non attendas ad illum thronum duntaxat materialem, sed respice ad tuum corpus, et ad tuam animam, quoniam quidem quod in illo throno actum est materialiter, hoc in te ipso reperire potes spiritualiter 518Det moraliter. Thronus quippe aureus et eburneus, spiritualiter et moraliter esse poteris, si verae sapientiae quae per aurum designatur, operam dederis, et castitati quae per ebur demonstratur, mente et corpore perfecte studueris. Sunt denique in te sex illi gradus quibus ascendebatur ad perfectum sedile, scilicet sex opera misericordiae, quae, quia superius jam enarrasse nos aestimamus, hic pro fastidio lectoris devitando reticemus. Et quoniam thronus rotundus dicitur fuisse, manifeste indicat quia thronus Dei desiderat esse, omni perfectioni studeat, mentem et linguam corpusque ab omni peccato custodiat. Duae autem manus quae tenent sedile, duae res sunt; scilicet voluntas Dei, et praesens vita, quae retinent animam in hoc corpore. 519AQuod vero anima uniuscujusque fidelis sedile appellari queat, hoc divina pagina etiam nobis tacentibus clamat, dicens: Anima justi sedes est sapientiae (Sap. VII). Quatuor autem leones qui juxta supradictas manus sunt, quatuor virtutes 303 scilicet timor et amor, contemptus et dilectio recte intelligi possunt. Debemus enim voluntatem Dei et amare, et timere, vitam vero praesentem, prout expedit, et diligere, et contemnere. Duodecim vero leunculi qui super sex gradus stant hinc atque inde, duodecim sunt sacramenta sanctae Ecclesiae, scilicet trina abrenuntiatio diaboli, trinaque professio catholicae fidei. susceptio sacri baptismi, communicatio corporis et sanguinis Christi. confessio, poenitentia, in Spiritu sancto peccatorum indulgentia, 519Bet in bonis operibus usque ad mortem perseverantia. Isti igitur sunt spirituales leones, qui illum leonem, qui circuit quaerens quem devoret, ab animabus fidelium longe faciunt; et ne in eis ulterius habitare praesumat, sua virtute et sanctificatione repellunt. Postea vero historiographus istam sententiam tali fine conclusit, dicens: Non est factum tale opus in universis regnis (III Reg. X). Qui bene est morigeratus, cujus mores longe lateque suavissimum spirant odorem, qui bonis moribus assidue insistit et inhaeret, qui sacrificium quotidianum seipsum Deo offert, cujus mores approbat Deus et laudat, qui suis correctis moribus de animali efficitur spiritualis, iste procul dubio potest dicere de seipso quod non est factum tale opus in universis regnis.

RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING, CONCERNING THE IVORY THRONE, HOW IT OUGHT TO BE REFERRED TO THE MORAL SENSE.

He who wishes to instruct men in morals ought 518Chimself to be instructed in his own morals. But because it is found hard that anyone should be adorned on every side with good morals, we ought not on that account to cease altogether from the building up of morals, since we hold this on the authority of the divine sayings: that our mind ought always to be intent upon good morals and works, and our tongue to publish abroad to all the praises of its Maker with untiring devotion. Whosoever thou art that readest or hearest that scripture which tells how Solomon made a great throne of ivory, attend not to that material throne only, but look to thy body and to thy soul, since indeed what was done in that throne materially, this thou canst find in thyself spiritually 518Dand morally. For a throne of gold and of ivory thou shalt be able to be, spiritually and morally, if thou givest thy pains to the true wisdom which is signified by the gold, and if in mind and body thou strivest perfectly after the chastity which is shown by the ivory. There are, in fine, in thee those six steps by which one ascended to the perfect seat, namely the six works of mercy, which, because we reckon that we have already recounted them above, we here pass over in silence, that the reader be not wearied. And since the throne is said to have been round, it plainly shows that he desires to be the throne of God, let him strive after every perfection, let him keep mind and tongue and body from all sin. But the two hands which hold the seat are two things, namely the will of God and the present life, which keep the soul in this body. 519ABut that the soul of every faithful man may be called a seat, this the divine page cries out even while we are silent, saying: The soul of the just man is the seat of wisdom (Sap. VII). And the four lions which are beside the aforesaid hands can rightly be understood as four virtues, 303 namely fear and love, contempt and esteem. For we ought both to love and to fear the will of God, and the present life, as is expedient, both to esteem and to despise. But the twelve little lions which stand upon the six steps on this side and on that are the twelve sacraments of holy Church, namely the threefold renunciation of the devil, and the threefold profession of the catholic faith. the receiving of holy baptism, the communion of the body and blood of Christ. confession, penance, the forgiveness of sins in the Holy Spirit, 519Band perseverance in good works unto death. These, therefore, are the spiritual lions, which drive far from the souls of the faithful that lion who goes about seeking whom he may devour; and lest he presume to dwell in them any longer, they repel him by their own power and sanctification. But afterward the historian closed this saying with such an ending, saying: There was not such work made in all kingdoms (III Reg. X). He who is well-mannered, whose morals breathe far and wide a most sweet odour, who unremittingly presses upon good morals and cleaves to them, who offers himself to God as a daily sacrifice, whose morals God approves and praises, who by the correcting of his morals is made spiritual out of animal, he beyond doubt can say of himself that there was not such work made in all kingdoms.

RECAPITULATIO A SUPERIORIBUS, DE THRONO EBURNEO ET AUREO, QUALITER SUB ALLEGORICA INTERPRETATIONE REFERRI DEBEAT AD PERSONAM BEATAE, ET GLORIOSAE, ET PERPETUAE SEMPER VIRGINIS MARIAE.

  • 519C

Fecit, inquit, Salomon thronum de ebore grandem, et vestivit eum auro clarissimo nimis (ibid.). Obsecro, Domina mea clarissima, si ego loquar aliquid minus dignum quam mereatur dignitas tua, non mihi hoc proveniat ad animae detrimentum, sed obveniat ad meriti et gloriae coelestis augmentum. Scio enim, scio, quia si omnia membra mea in linguas verterentur, non valerem ut dignum est effari laudes tuas, quas sublimis gloria tuae singularis virginitatis promeretur. Scio, inquam, Domina mea, etiamsi 519Domnia esse mea possent loqui, non tamen invenirent in coelo aut in terra aliquam similem tibi. In coelo autem similis tibi nullus est, quia si de Deo agimus, Deus major te est, et idcirco similis tui non est. Si vero de sanctis, vel de angelis, vel aliquibus creaturis, quamvis a Deo creatis vult aliquid dicere lingua nostra, manifestum est quod omnes creaturas excellit et praecellit dignitas tua, quia sicut scriptum est. Exaltata es super choros angelorum ad coelestia regna. Obsecro iterum mea Domina millies plus quam charissima, servulos tuos qui te libenter honorant, et tibi devote serviunt, et de te quod dignum est decenter loquuntur, precibus et meritis tuis omnes adjuva, et sustenta, conforta ab omni peccato, conserva et perduc illos ad immortalia Filii tui gaudia. 520ATu es ergo thronus ille non quem fecit Salomon rex mortalis, filius scilicet David regis, sed potius ille quem elegit ab aeterno, et praeelegit sibi ad habitandum Deus, Dei Filius videlicet rex immortalis. Tu es ille thronus de quo Psalmista dicit: Thronus tuus, Deus, in saeculum saeculi, virga directionis, virga regni tui (Psal. XLIV). Tu es, inquam, mea Domina, omnibus charissimis charior, thronus de auro coelesti, et ebore spirituali constitutus. Per aurum intelligitur Christi Filii tui divinitas, per ebur vero quod frigidae naturae est, sacrosancta tua virginitas. Ex auro ergo divinae majestatis, et ex ebore perpetuae virginitatis es constituta, quia et divinitas Filii tui cooperante Spiritu sancto in te est ineffabiliter adumbrata, et virginitas tua permanet et permansit 520Btibi illibata. Praeterea hoc quod de natura eboris solent dicere inquisitores naturarum, nequaquam reor sub silentio praetereundum. Congruit enim beatae Mariae, dignitati, et ejus auxilii sublimi potestati. Siquidem dicunt quia si ebur dolenti capiti persaepe adhibitum fuerit, virtute naturali omnem dolorem mitigat capitis et expellit. Qui ergo in capite, hoc est in mente, vitiorum telis vulnerati dolent, isti sine dubio necesse est ut auxilium et opem sanctae Dei Genitricis Mariae implorent, et ex intimo affectu cordis dicant: « Sub tuam protectionem confugimus, ubi infirmi acceperunt virtutem, et propter hoc Dei Genitrix virgo tuae dignitati referimus gratiarum actionem. » Tu es, inquam, Domina domus quam Sapientia, id est Filius sibi aedificavit (Prov. IX), et 520Cin qua septem columnas spirituales ad decorem et gloriam aeterna stabilitate collocavit, scilicet virginitatem, castitatem, continentiam, humilitatem, fidem, spem, charitatem. Vere in beata Maria singulare privilegium incomparabilis virginitatis, et fragrantia odoriferae castitatis effulsit, quia nisi virgo et casta exstitisset, nequaquam illam ad habitandum sibi Filius Dei elegisset. Decebat namque ut virgo esset quae mundum redimebat, quia virgo exstitit illa prima parens, quae totum mundum perdiderat. Quia ergo beata Dei Genitrix Maria, virgo fuit, consequenter casta et continens exstitit. Sane de ejus inenarrabili humilitate quid possumus dignum laudibus ejus proferre? Enimvero cum haec sanctissima 520Datque millies 304 beatissima Virgo mater Filii Dei ab Angelo eligeretur, ancillam ejusdem Filii sui humili devotione se fatetur, dicens: Ecce ancilla Domini, fiat mihi secundum verbum tuum (Luc. I). Fidem vero mater Dei intemeratam habuit, quia quod mater Dei posset fieri Angelo nuntiante credidit. Spes autem in ipsa beatissima Virgine sublimem arcem posuerat, quia quamvis insolitum esset et inauditum retro saeculis et generationibus quod audiebat; tamen sic fieri oportere sicut ab Angelo evangelizabatur, certissima fide sperabat. Charitatis visceribus quomodo mater Dei super abundaverit, nullus incognitum habere potest, qui scientia divinae legis aliquantulum eruditus fuerit. Sicut enim Filius ejus maledictum legis pro redemptione generis humani 521Aincurrit. Maledictus nempe, ait Moyses (Deut. XXI), qui in ligno pendebit, sic et ipsa nimirum maluit cum observatione virginitatis maledicto legis subjacere, quam amissa virginitate, carnales filios in Israel relinquere. Sic denique lex Mosaica aiebat: maledictus, sive maledicta quae non reliquerit semen in Israel. Tu es ergo omni claritate et splendore solis clarior et splendidior, omni lapide pretioso longe incomparabiliter pretiosior, tu super omnem stellarum fulgorem utpote stella maris fulgentior inveniris, candor quippe es aeternae claritatis, et speculum sine naevo divinae majestatis. Tu es haereditas Domini, in te moratus est Flius Dei. Tu potes dicere: Creator omnium qui me creavit, in tabernaculo meo requievit (Eccli XXIV). Tu es tabernaculum in sole positum, 521Bde quo utique tabernaculo Filius Dei processit tanquam sponsus de thalamo suo (Psal. XVIII). Viscera tua sunt lecti argentei in quo malum illud aureum est, de quo Salomon ait: Mala aurea in lectis argenteis verbum prolatum in tempore suo (Prov. XXV). Non enim potuit digniori et clariori comparatione pronuntiari nativitas specialis unigeniti Filii tui. Malum quippe prolatum in lectis argenteis, Verbum est caro factum in visceribus Virginis. Sex autem gradus qui describuntur fuisse in throno eburneo, sex specialiter gradus humilitatis designant, quos habuit beata Maria in corde suo. Unde bene Spiritus sanctus loquitur in Canticis canticorum: Cum esset rex in accubitu suo, nardus mea dedit odorem suum (Cant. I). Nardus quippe aromatica, et brevis staturae arbor 521Cesse dicitur, per quam congrue humilitas beatae Mariae intelligitur. Quae nimirum virginalis humilitas regi in accubitu suo quiescenti complacuit, quando idem rex Filius scilicet aeterni regis de coelo descendens, carnem sacrosanctam assumpsit in utero beatae Mariae Virginis. Sed jam nunc videamus qui sint sex gradus humilitatis. Primus namque gradus humilitatis est ut homo creatum se de terra fateatur, et ob pravitatis suae meritum in cinerem se reversurum confiteatur. Secundus vero gradus est ut de accepta qualibet gratia homo nunquam superbiat. Tertius, ut de collatis sibi a Domino beneficiis ipsi Creatori suo indesinenter gratias referat. Quartus, ut homo omnibus se aestimet meritis inferiorem. Quintus, ut 521Dnulli se arbitretur aequalem. Sextus, ut nullatenus se reputet aliquo potiorem vel superiorem. Isti sunt sex spirituales gradus humilitatis, quibus Deus descendit ad cor sese humiliantis, et per quos ascendit homo ad cognitionem Dei ad se descendentis. Post haec autem nobis historia insinuavit, quod thronus ille rotundus in exteriori parte exstiterit. Superiori vero tractatu dixisse me memini, quia quidquid est rotundum, videtur esse perfectum; quid ergo rotunditas et perfectio throni aurei nobis innuit, nisi quod beata Maria virgo ante partum fuit, et post partum virgo inviolata permansit? Quae siquidem manus quae throni eburnei sedile tenuerunt, duo Testamenta designant quae ubique Christi humanitatem quam in beata Maria assumpsit, praedicaverunt.

522AQuatuor ergo leones qui juxta praedictas manus stant, quatuor sunt Evangelia quae itidem quod duo Testamenta, clamant. Duodecim leunculi qui super sex gradus fuisse dicuntur hinc atque inde, duodecim virtutes sunt, quas constat beatam Mariam pro certo possedisse, scilicet spiritualem vitam, gaudium in Spiritu sancto, longanimitatem, patientiam, bonitatem, benignitatem, mansuetudinem, fidem, incomparabilem modestiam, virginitatem, compassionem, in sancto proposito perseverantiam. Spiritualem siquidem vitam, beata Maria duxit, quia nisi vita ejus plena virtutibus refulsisset, nullatenus Deo placuisset. Gaudium nimirum in Spiritu sancto habuit, quoniam ipsa in hymno suo repleta spirituali jubilatione dixit: Exsultavit spiritus meus in 522BDeo salutari meo (Luc I). Longanimitas quippe illi non defuit, quia quae ab Angelo audiebat, licet inaudita et insolita essent, tamen ut in se complerentur per longanimitatem exspectabat. Virtute patientiae non modicum exstitit munita, quia contumelias quas Filio suo, imo etiam sibi a perfidis Judaeis inferri vidit, patienti animo toleravit. Bonitatem et benignitatem quis deneget illam habuisse, quam procul dubio fatemur omni abundantia floruisse? Mansuetudo autem in ea locum praecipuum obtinuit, quia nisi mansueta esset, Spiritus sanctus nullatenus illam ad habitandum elegisset. Fides vero incomparabilis, ut supra jam memoravimus, cordis ejus intima perlustravit, quia licet contra humanae naturae jura esset hoc quod Virgo Mater Dei eligebatur, tamen 522Csic fieri nutu Dei posse credidit, ut ab Angelo ei 305 evangelizabatur. Modestiam ipsius, id est religionem quibus praeconiis possumus, ut dignum est attollere, quam scimus pro certo orationi et silentio vacantem ab Angelo repertam esse. Fores vero Virginitatis et insignia integerrimae castitatis ipsius, quibus laudibus, ut decet, praedicabimus, quae in tantum dissueverat perfrui alloquiis hominum, ut etiam ingressum et affatum expavesceret angelicum? Unde Angelus: Ne timeas, inquit, Maria, invenisti gratiam apud Dominum (ibid.). Cum enim illud ave gloriosum et ineffabile a Deo missum Gabriel Angelus illi evangelizaret, quia illam timentem vidit, illico submonuit ne quidquam timeret. 522DCompassionem vero et dolorem quem Beata Maria sustinuit in morte Filii sui, non valet quisquam ad liquidum infundere humano auditui. Videbat enim Deum et Dei Filium quem de Spiritu sancto Virgo Mater conceperat, ab impiissimis Judaeis crucifixum, et, quasi alicujus facinoris reus esset, cum latronibus praedamnatum. Quomodo autem possent viscera Beatae Mariae Virginis sine doloris experientia remanere, cum ipsa cerneret carnem suam quam Dei Filius in suo utero pro salute generis humani acceperat, in cruce pendere? Perseverantiam in sancto proposito et in bonis operibus quam Beata Maria habuit, facili judicio possumus ostendere, quia illam constat remota omni ambiguitate ab ortu suo usque ad finem hujus mortalis vitae per omnia Deo 523Aplacuisse. Post haec autem liber Regnum superiorem sententiam tali fine conclusit dicens: Non est factum tale opus in universis regnis (III Reg. X). O quam admirabilis est ista conclusio! Vere, tale opus in universis regnis factum non fuit, quale est illud quod Virgo de Spiritu sancto concepit, Virgo Dei Filium genuit, Virgo inviolata post partum permansit. 524ANon est, inquam, ab initio mundi factum tale opus ut Virgo Mater lactaret Filium Dei virgineis uberibus. Igitur quoniam huic secundo throno, id est volumini ubera Sponsi dederunt exordium, dignum est ut eidem throno ubera Sponsae Virginis et Matris finem tribuant idoneum.

523Explicit secundus tomus in Cantica canticorum.

RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING, CONCERNING THE THRONE OF IVORY AND GOLD, IN WHAT MANNER, UNDER AN ALLEGORICAL INTERPRETATION, IT OUGHT TO BE REFERRED TO THE PERSON OF THE BLESSED AND GLORIOUS AND PERPETUAL EVER-VIRGIN MARY.

  • 519C

Solomon made, he says, a great throne of ivory, and clothed it with gold exceedingly bright (ibid.) [Vulg. III Reg. X, 18 fecit etiam rex Salomon thronum de ebore grandem, et vestivit eum auro fulvo nimis, "with exceeding tawny gold"]. I beseech thee, my most bright Lady, if I speak anything less worthy than thy dignity deserves, let this not turn out to me for the loss of my soul, but let it fall out for the increase of merit and of heavenly glory. For I know, I know, that if all my members were turned into tongues, I should not be strong enough to utter as is worthy thy praises, which the sublime glory of thy singular virginity deserves. I know, I say, my Lady, that even if 519Dall that is mine could speak, yet they would not find in heaven or on earth anyone like to thee. In heaven, moreover, there is none like to thee, because if we treat of God, God is greater than thou, and therefore he is not thy like. But if our tongue wishes to say anything of the saints, or of the angels, or of any creatures, though created by God, it is manifest that thy dignity excels and surpasses all creatures, for as it is written. Thou art exalted above the choirs of angels to the heavenly kingdoms. Again I beseech thee, my Lady a thousand times more than most dear: thy little servants who gladly honour thee, and devoutly serve thee, and becomingly speak of thee what is worthy — help them all by thy prayers and merits, and sustain them, comfort them, keep them from all sin, and lead them through to the immortal joys of thy Son. 520AThou art therefore that throne — not the one which Solomon the mortal king made, the son, that is, of David the king, but rather that one which God chose from eternity and fore-chose for himself to dwell in, namely the Son of God, the immortal King. Thou art that throne of which the Psalmist says: Thy throne, O God, is for ever and ever; the rod of direction is the rod of thy kingdom (Psal. XLIV) [Vulg. Psal. XLIV, 7 sedes tua, Deus, in saeculum saeculi, "thy seat"]. Thou art, I say, my Lady, dearer than all dear ones, a throne framed of heavenly gold and of spiritual ivory. By the gold is understood the divinity of Christ thy Son; but by the ivory, which is of a cold nature, thy most holy virginity. Of the gold, therefore, of divine majesty, and of the ivory of perpetual virginity art thou framed, since both the divinity of thy Son was ineffably overshadowed in thee, the Holy Spirit working with him, and thy virginity remains and has remained 520Bunviolated to thee. Moreover, what the investigators of natures are wont to say about the nature of ivory I judge in no wise to be passed over in silence. For it agrees with blessed Mary, with her dignity, and with the sublime power of her help. For they say that if ivory be very often applied to an aching head, by its natural power it soothes and drives out all pain of the head. Those, therefore, who ache in the head — that is, in the mind — being wounded by the darts of vices, it is needful without doubt that they implore the help and aid of holy Mary the Mother of God, and say from the innermost affection of the heart: « We flee to thy protection, where the weak have received strength; and for this cause, O Virgin Mother of God, we render to thy dignity the giving of thanks. » Thou art, I say, Lady, the house which Wisdom, that is the Son, built for himself (Prov. IX), and 520Cin which he set seven spiritual pillars, for comeliness and glory, with eternal stability: namely virginity, chastity, continence, humility, faith, hope, charity. Truly in blessed Mary there shone forth the singular privilege of incomparable virginity and the fragrance of odour-bearing chastity, since unless she had been a virgin and chaste, the Son of God would in no wise have chosen her to dwell in. For it was fitting that she should be a virgin who was redeeming the world, since that first parent was a virgin who had ruined the whole world. Since therefore blessed Mary the Mother of God was a virgin, she was consequently chaste and continent. But concerning her unspeakable humility, what can we bring forward worthy of her praises? For indeed when this most holy 520Dand a thousand times 304 most blessed Virgin was chosen by the Angel to be mother of the Son of God, she confesses herself with humble devotion the handmaid of that same Son of hers, saying: Behold the handmaid of the Lord; be it done unto me according to thy word (Luc. I). But the Mother of God had faith undefiled, since she believed, at the Angel's announcing, that she could become the Mother of God. And hope had set its lofty citadel in that most blessed Virgin, for although what she heard was unwonted and unheard-of in ages and generations gone by; yet she hoped with most certain faith that it must so come to pass as it was proclaimed to her by the Angel. How the Mother of God overflowed in the bowels of charity, no one can hold unknown who has been in any degree instructed in the knowledge of the divine law. For as her Son incurred the curse of the law for the redemption of the human race. 521AFor cursed, says Moses (Deut. XXI), is he who shall hang upon a tree [Vulg. Deut. XXI, 23 maledictus a Deo est qui pendet in ligno, "cursed of God is he that hangeth on a tree"], so she too doubtless chose rather to lie under the curse of the law with the keeping of her virginity, than, virginity lost, to leave behind carnal sons in Israel. So, in fine, the Mosaic law said: cursed is he, or cursed is she, who has not left seed in Israel. Thou art therefore brighter and more splendid than all the brightness and splendour of the sun, far and incomparably more precious than every precious stone; thou art found more shining than every gleam of the stars, being as thou art the star of the sea; for thou art the brightness of eternal light, and the mirror without blemish of the divine majesty. Thou art the inheritance of the Lord; in thee the Flius of God tarried. Thou canst say: The Creator of all things, who created me, rested in my tabernacle (Eccli XXIV). Thou art the tabernacle set in the sun, 521Bfrom which tabernacle the Son of God came forth as a bridegroom out of his chamber (Psal. XVIII). Thy womb is the silver bed in which is that golden apple, of which Solomon says: Golden apples on beds of silver — a word brought forth in its own time (Prov. XXV) [Vulg. Prov. XXV, 11 mala aurea in lectis argenteis qui loquitur verbum in tempore suo, "he that speaketh a word in due season"]. For the special nativity of thine only-begotten Son could not be declared by a worthier or brighter comparison. For the apple brought forth on beds of silver is the Word made flesh in the womb of the Virgin. But the six steps which are described as having been in the ivory throne signify especially the six degrees of humility which blessed Mary had in her heart. Whence the Holy Spirit well speaks in the Song of Songs: When the king was at his repose, my spikenard gave forth its odour (Cant. I). For spikenard is said to be an aromatic tree of low stature, 521Cby which the humility of blessed Mary is fitly understood. Which virginal humility doubtless pleased the king resting at his repose, when that same king — the Son, that is, of the eternal King — descending from heaven took most holy flesh in the womb of the blessed Virgin Mary. But now let us see what the six degrees of humility are. For the first degree of humility is that a man confess himself created from earth, and acknowledge that for the desert of his own depravity he will return into ashes. But the second degree is that a man never grow proud over any grace received. The third, that for the benefits conferred upon him by the Lord he render thanks unceasingly to his Creator himself. The fourth, that a man esteem himself inferior to all in merits. The fifth, that 521Dhe judge himself equal to none. The sixth, that he in no wise reckon himself better than or superior to anyone. These are the six spiritual degrees of humility, by which God descends to the heart of him who humbles himself, and by which man ascends to the knowledge of God descending to him. But after these things the narrative intimated to us that that throne was round on its outer part. And in an earlier treatment I remember to have said that whatever is round seems to be perfect; what then does the roundness and perfection of the golden throne suggest to us, unless that blessed Mary was a virgin before childbirth, and after childbirth remained an unviolated virgin? And the hands indeed which held the seat of the ivory throne signify the two Testaments, which everywhere preached the humanity of Christ which he took in blessed Mary.

522AThe four lions therefore which stand beside the aforesaid hands are the four Gospels, which cry out the same thing as the two Testaments. The twelve little lions which are said to have been upon the six steps on this side and on that are the twelve virtues which it is agreed that blessed Mary assuredly possessed: namely spiritual life, joy in the Holy Spirit, long-suffering, patience, goodness, kindness, meekness, faith, incomparable modesty, virginity, compassion, perseverance in a holy purpose. For blessed Mary led a spiritual life, since unless her life had shone full of virtues, it would in no wise have pleased God. She doubtless had joy in the Holy Spirit, since she herself, filled with spiritual jubilation, said in her hymn: My spirit hath rejoiced in 522BGod my saviour (Luc I). Long-suffering indeed was not lacking to her, for the things which she heard from the Angel, though they were unheard-of and unwonted, she yet awaited with long-suffering that they might be fulfilled in her. She was fortified in no small measure with the virtue of patience, since the insults which she saw inflicted upon her Son — nay, even upon herself — by the faithless Jews, she bore with a patient mind. Who would deny that she had goodness and kindness, whom beyond doubt we confess to have flourished in all abundance? But meekness held the chief place in her, since unless she had been meek, the Holy Spirit would in no wise have chosen her to dwell in. But incomparable faith, as we have already recalled above, ranged through the inmost parts of her heart, since although it was against the laws of human nature that a Virgin was being chosen Mother of God, yet 522Cshe believed that it could so come to pass at the nod of God, as it was proclaimed to her 305 by the Angel. Her modesty, that is her religion, with what proclamations can we exalt as is worthy, she whom we know for certain to have been found by the Angel at leisure for prayer and silence. But the doors of her Virginity and the tokens of her most entire chastity, with what praises shall we proclaim them as is fitting, she who had so far unlearned to enjoy the converse of men that she was terrified even at the angel's entrance and address? Whence the Angel: Fear not, he says, Mary, thou hast found grace with the Lord (ibid.) [Vulg. Luc. I, 30 invenisti enim gratiam apud Deum, "with God"]. For when the Angel Gabriel proclaimed to her that glorious and ineffable Hail sent from God, because he saw her afraid, he straightway warned her not to fear anything. 522DBut the compassion and grief which Blessed Mary endured at the death of her Son, no one is able to pour out clearly into human hearing. For she saw God and the Son of God, whom as a Virgin Mother she had conceived of the Holy Spirit, crucified by the most impious Jews, and — as though he were guilty of some crime — condemned beforehand along with thieves. But how could the inward parts of the Blessed Virgin Mary remain without the experience of grief, when she beheld her own flesh, which the Son of God had taken in her womb for the salvation of the human race, hanging upon the cross? The perseverance in a holy purpose and in good works which Blessed Mary had we can show by an easy judgment, since it is agreed, all ambiguity removed, that from her birth to the end of this mortal life she pleased God 523Ain all things. But after these things the book of Kings closed the foregoing saying with such an ending, saying: There was not such work made in all kingdoms (III Reg. X). O how admirable is that conclusion! Truly, such work was not made in all kingdoms as is that which a Virgin conceived of the Holy Spirit, a Virgin bore the Son of God, a Virgin remained unviolated after childbirth. 524AThere was not, I say, from the beginning of the world such work made, that a Virgin Mother should suckle the Son of God with virginal breasts. Therefore, since to this second throne — that is, to this volume — the breasts of the Bridegroom gave the beginning, it is fitting that the breasts of the Bride, the Virgin and Mother, should grant to that same throne a suitable end.

523Here ends the second Tome on the Song of Songs.

INCIPIT TERTIUS TOMUS Super hoc quod dicitur: Oleum effusum nomen tuum, ideo adolescentulae dilexerunt te nimis.

523BCui placet ut aliquem ingrediatur hortum diversi generis arboribus consitum, vernantibus floribus decoratum, gustu quidem fructuum gratanter reficitur et suavi fragrantia vernantium florum miro modo delectatur. Quid ergo dicimus esse hunc hortum nisi amoenissimum textum divinarum Scripturarum? Quid vero arbores et flores? Utique sententias spirituales Scripturarum. Ingrediamur ergo mente et spiritu hortum istum, et videamus quid sit quod loquitur Sponsa ad Sponsum: Oleum effusum nomen tuum. Sicut aiunt qui de naturis rerum disserunt, oleum in se septem virtutes vel naturas habere perhibetur, quae nomini Christi aptissime convenire videntur, quia juxta Isaiam prophetam (cap. XI), septem dona Spiritus sancti 523Csuper ipsum requievisse dicuntur; scilicet, « spiritus sapientiae et intellectus, spiritus consilii et fortitudinis, spiritus scientiae et pietatis, spiritus timoris Domini. » Sed jam ad septem naturas olei disserendas veniamus, et eas Domino Jesu coaptemus. Omnes liquores in quibus ponitur oleum supernatare fertur. Quod bene Domino Jesu congruit, quia sicut Paulus dicit: « Dedit Deus ei nomen, quod est super omne nomen (Philip. II). » Et ipse Deus « est super omnia benedictus in saecula. Amen (Rom. IX). » Oleum nullum externum liquorem recipit, quia Christum nulla peccati macula vel contagio infecit, sicut Petrus dicit: « Qui peccatum non fecit (I Petr. II) » homines reficit, et Christus nos sanguine 523Det carne sua potat et satiat. Vulnera sanat, quia Christus sicut Isaias ait: « Vulnera nostra sanavit (Isa. LIII). » Tale est interius, quale exterius, quia nostra natura a divinitate Christi assumpta, participatione ejusdem divinitatis omnino caruit culpa. Adhibitum igni sedentibus in tenebris lumen donat. et Christus omnem hominem venientem in hunc mundum illuminat (Joan. I). Si in uno loco effunditur, terram circa se occupare videtur: Christus vero in Bethlehem natus est, sed stella radiante de coelo magis Orientalibus denuntiatus est. Compara ergo, si tibi placet, septem dona Spiritus sancti septem naturis olei, et considerabis mirabilia in lege Dei. Non 306 est major sapientia, quam ut cognoscat homo suum Creatorem, « qui est super 524Bomnia Deus benedictus in saecula. Amen. » Non potest aliquis habere digniorem intellectum, quam ut bonum quod intelligit, sciat, et intra arcanum cordis sui, diaboli opus nullatenus recipiat. Tunc vero corpus nostrum et anima sapienti et perfecto consilio utitur, quando praemissa confessione et poenitentia, corpore Christi et sanguine Christi reficitur. Mens autem nostra tunc fortiorem sanitatem consequitur, quando Christum juxta possibilitatem suam imitatur. Sane ad consortium Christi non possumus pervenire, nisi per veram scientiam, castitatem corporis, et munditiam cordis, studeamus possidere. Magno charitatis fulgore tunc resplendemus, quando pietatis viscera circa omnes homines sincero animo gestamus. Timorem Domini sanctum 524Ctunc revera nos fatemur habere, quando in uno loco bene facientes, suave redolens exemplum longe lateque a nobis dimittimus emanare. Dicat ergo Sponsa ad Sponsum: Oleum effusum nomen tuum, ac si dicat: Sicut oleum quod in uno loco effunditur, terram circa se occupare videtur, sic tu pro me quidem in Hierusalem crucifixus es, per totum vero mundum per me praedicatus es, et ideo adolescentulae, id est fideles animae dilexerunt te. Quoniam quidem pro ipsis mortuus es in cruce, ideo ipsae adolescentulae dilexerunt te in tantum etiam ut ipsi martyres morerentur pro te. Studeamus ergo, sicut Paulus dicit, compati et commori Christo (Rom. VIII), ut ad beatam vitam valeamus resurgere 524Dcum ipso, quia si socii passionum fuerimus, simul et resurrectionis et gaudii erimus (II Cor. I).

HERE BEGINS THE THIRD TOME Upon that which is said: Thy name is oil poured out; therefore the maidens have loved thee exceedingly.

523BHe who is pleased to enter some garden planted with trees of divers kinds and adorned with blossoming flowers is gladly refreshed by the taste of the fruits, and is wonderfully delighted by the sweet fragrance of the blossoming flowers. What then do we say this garden is, but the most delightful text of the Holy Scriptures? And what the trees and flowers? Assuredly the spiritual meanings of the Scriptures. Let us therefore enter this garden in mind and spirit, and see what it is that the Bride says to the Bridegroom: Thy name is oil poured out. As those say who discourse of the natures of things, oil is reported to have in itself seven powers or natures, which seem most aptly to agree with the name of Christ, since according to Isaiah the prophet (cap. XI) the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit 523Care said to have rested upon him; namely, « the spirit of wisdom and understanding, the spirit of counsel and fortitude, the spirit of knowledge and piety, the spirit of the fear of the Lord. » But now let us come to the discussing of the seven natures of oil, and fit them to the Lord Jesus. Oil is said to float above all liquids into which it is put. Which well agrees with the Lord Jesus, since as Paul says: « God gave him a name which is above every name (Philip. II). » And God himself « is over all things blessed for ever. Amen (Rom. IX). » Oil receives no external liquid, since no stain or contagion of sin infected Christ, as Peter says: « Who did no sin (I Petr. II) » it refreshes men, and Christ gives us his blood 523Dand his flesh to drink and satisfies us. It heals wounds, since Christ, as Isaiah says: « He healed our wounds (Isa. LIII). » [Vulg. Isa. LIII, 5 livore ejus sanati sumus, "by his bruises we are healed"] It is such within as it is without, since our nature, taken up by the divinity of Christ, by participation in that same divinity was wholly without fault. Applied to fire, it gives light to those sitting in darkness. and Christ enlightens every man coming into this world (Joan. I). If it is poured out in one place, it is seen to occupy the ground around it: but Christ was born in Bethlehem, yet by a star shining from heaven he was announced to the Magi of the East. Compare therefore, if it please thee, the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit with the seven natures of oil, and thou shalt consider wondrous things in the law of God. There is no 306 greater wisdom than that a man should know his Creator, « who is over 524Ball things God blessed for ever. Amen. » No one can have a worthier understanding than that he should know the good which he understands, and within the secret place of his heart in no wise receive the work of the devil. But then our body and soul use wise and perfect counsel, when, confession and penance having gone before, it is refreshed by the body of Christ and the blood of Christ. And our mind then attains a stronger health, when it imitates Christ according to its possibility. Assuredly we cannot come to fellowship with Christ, unless through true knowledge we strive to possess chastity of body and cleanness of heart. We then shine with a great blaze of charity, when with a sincere mind we bear bowels of piety toward all men. We confess that we truly have the holy fear of the Lord 524Cthen, when, doing well in one place, we let a sweetly-smelling example flow forth from us far and wide. Let the Bride therefore say to the Bridegroom: Thy name is oil poured out, as if she said: As oil which is poured out in one place is seen to occupy the ground around it, so thou indeed wast crucified for me in Jerusalem, but wast preached through the whole world by me; and therefore the maidens, that is the faithful souls, have loved thee. Since indeed thou didst die on the cross for them, therefore those maidens have loved thee so far that the martyrs themselves died for thee. Let us strive therefore, as Paul says, to suffer with Christ and to die with him (Rom. VIII), that we may be able to rise again to the blessed life 524Dwith him, since if we have been companions of his sufferings, we shall likewise be companions of his resurrection and joy (II Cor. I).

RECAPITULATIO A SUPERIORIBUS.

Aurifices qui de auro et argento vel de lapidibus pretiosis operari solent, quando aliquod vas pretiosum inter manus suas fabricant, sunt fortasse aliqui homines qui illud vas, quod aspiciunt, laudant et mirantur et mira varietate fabrilis operis delectantur, sed tamen artifices vas quod in manibus suis habent adhuc malleis supponunt, limis extenuant, gemmis adornant; et licet astantibus hominibus illud vas undique jam videatur esse perfectum, artificibus tamen videtur esse minus politum et perfectum. Eo itaque modo quando nos illam sententiam 525Ascilicet, Oleum effusum nomen tuum, superius tractavimus, quamvis nonnullis forsitan placeat quod diximus, tamen adhuc auxiliante gratia Spiritus sancti, possumus aliquid addere superiori tractatui quod legentium pariter et audientium non displiceat auditui. Oleum effusum nomen tuum, ideo adolescentulae dilexerunt te (Cant. I). Ista vox est sanctae Ecclesiae, quae laudat clementiam Sponsi sui, et charitatem ineffabilem in hac oratione bimembri. Nec mirum sane si Sponsa sui Sponsi laudat clementiam, cum etiam ipse Sponsus non taceat affectum et pietatem quam habuerit erga Sponsam suam. Sic enim ipse dicit per Jeremiam prophetam: « In charitate perpetua dilexi te, ideo attraxi te miserans (cap. XXXI). De hac nimirum ineffabili charitate 525BChristi qua ipse sanctam Ecclesiam dilexit, Joannes apostolus dicit: In hoc perfecta est charitas Dei in nobis, non quia nos dilexerimus eum, sed quoniam ipse prior dilexit nos (I Joan. IV). » Praeterea considerare libet si in Veteri Testamento vel in prophetis de oleo aliquid dictum potest reperiri, quod nomini sponsi congruat et in mysteriis Sponsae videlicet sanctae Ecclesiae decenter valeat aptari. In Exodo nempe dicit Dominus ad Moysen: « Praecipe filiis Israel ut afferant tibi oleum de arboribus olivarum purissimum, piloque contusum, ut ardeat semper lucerna coram me in tabernaculo testimonii (cap XXVII). » Isaias quoque propheta olei mentionem faciens dicit: « Vinea facta est dilecto 525Cmeo in cornu filio olei, et sepivit illam, et lapides elegit ex illa, et plantavit illam electam, et aedificavit turrim in medio ejus, torcular exstruxit in ea et exspectavit ut faceret uvas et fecit labruscas (cap. V). » In Zacharia vero propheta de oleo sic scribitur: « Isti sunt duo filii olei splendoris, qui assistunt dominatori universae terrae (cap. IV). » Quia enim in superiori tractatu ope divinitatis Christi adjuti, et rore spiritualis intelligentiae aliquantulum fecundati, non tamen ut dignum est, sed pro modulo ingenii nostri de septem naturis olei disseruimus, et easdem naturas septem donis Spiritus sancti, quae in Christo plenariae sunt, assignavimus; superest modo, ut adhuc de oleo aliquid sub allegorico sensu loquamur, quod et Christo et sanctae Ecclesiae congruat, 525Det singulis quibusque fidelibus moraliter conveniat. Videamus ergo quid sit quod Isaias egregius propheta dicit: « Vinea, inquit, facta est dilecto meo in cornu filio olei. » O tu, Domine Jesu Christe, qui es filius summi olei, revela oculos spiritales cordis mei, ut valeam considerare mirabilia sacramenta, quae sunt ab aeterno abscondita in sancta ac beatissima lege tua. Eleos siquidem Graece, misericordia Latine dicitur. Dominus quippe in Evangelio dicit: « Estote misericordes sicut et Pater vester misericors est (Luc. VI). » David quoque propheta refectus Spiritus sancti superabundanti gratia, de Deo sic loquitur: « Deus meus, misericordia mea (Psal. LVIII). » Deus ergo omnipotens, ineffabiliter pius, ac 307 specialiter clemens, qui in Isaia oleum appellatur, 526Aipse nimirum in Evangelio ac Unigenito Filio suo misericors dicitur, et a David propheta misericordia praedicatur. Hujus igitur ineffabilis atque incomparabilis olei dilectissimo Filio quaedam vinea charissima facta est in cornu excellentissimo, id est in humanitate quam de nostra peccatrice massa assumpsit, quae humanitas ita reliquam massam, de qua assumpta est, virtute assumentis divinitatis excessit, sicut solet cornu illam carnem excedere de qua oriendo videtur procedere. De hoc autem cornu, id est de incarnatione Domini Jesu Zacharias impletus Spiritus sancti gratia dixit, « Benedictus Dominus Deus Israel, quia visitavit et fecit redemptionem plebis suae (Luc. I). » Et quasi si aliquis quaereret ab eo quam misericordiam Deus Israel 526Bfecisset populo suo, protinus adjunxit, dicens: « Erexit cornu salutis in domo David pueri sui (ibid.). » Si nos aliter vellemus istud cornu interpretari quam rei veritas se haberet, ipsa veritas auctoritate subnixa, et auctoritas fideli veritate roborata nobis contradiceret. Manifestum est enim quia regia Virgo stirpis Davidicae est electa, de qua et in qua istud cornu, id est nostra humanitas a Verbo Patris est assumpta, quod si non fieret, nullatenus genus humanum, quod perierat, salvari posset. Et hoc est cornu salutis quod erectum est nobis, videlicet Verbum caro factum pro salute nostra quod habitavit in nobis (Joan. I). In hoc ergo cornu vinea est charissima dilecto Filio olei, id est per humanitatem quam 526Cassumpsit, sanctam Ecclesiam sibi acquisivit Filius omnipotentis Dei. Quod autem ipse Filius, dilectus appelletur, Sponsa ejus in Canticis canticorum protestatur: « Dilectus, inquit, meus candidus et rubicundus (Cant. V). » Rubicundus propter sanguinis in cruce effusionem, candidus ob virginitatis et castitatis praerogativam. Item in eodem libello charissima ejus Sponsa sicut de eodem Sponso suo loquitur: « Dilectus, inquit, meus inter ubera mea commorabitur (Cant. I). » Sancta ergo Ecclesia quae in Isaia appellatur vinea, ipsa in Canticis canticorum dicitur Sponsa. Ista vinea quidem effecta est in deliciis charissima dilecto Filio olei, ex quo ex ea carnem accepit, quia sicut dicit Paulus: « Nemo unquam carnem suam odio habuit (Ephes. V). » Et alio 526Din loco idem Paulus manifestius insinuat quam dilectionem Sponsus erga Sponsam suam habeat: « Viri, inquit, diligite uxores vestras sicut Christus dilexit sanctam Ecclesiam et tradidit semetipsum propter illam (Col. III). » Unde idem Sponsus in Canticis canticorum de Sponsa sua dicit: « Quam pulchra es, amica mea, quam pulchra es, oculi tui columbarum! (Cant. I.) » Pulchritudinem vero Sponsae suae, ideo Sponsus repetendo duplicat, quia virginem et castam in anima et in corpore eam praesciebat. Oculos autem ejus columbis comparavit, quia simplicitatem fidei qua haeretici carent, illam habere praevidit. Verumtamen quid esse arbitramur hoc quod propheta dicit, quia Deus vineam suam, id est sanctam Ecclesiam sepivit? vinea nempe vocatur sancta Ecclesia, 527Anon solum in Isaia, verum in libro Evangeliorum et in Canticis canticorum: in Evangelio autem ubi paterfamilias operarios in vineam suam conduxit (Matth. XX); in Canticis vero canticorum ubi scriptum est: « Vinea fuit pacifico, » et, « Vinea nostra floruit (Cant. II). » Hanc ergo Dominus vineam sepe circumdedit, quando eam eloquiis prophetarum munivit, et, ne conculcari et dilacerari queat ab haereticis, quasi forti praesidio, apostolicis erudivit documentis. Unde bene quidem sequitur in eodem propheta, quia « lapides elegit ex illa (Isa. V). » Lapides enim idcirco in vinea ab hominibus eliguntur, ut de ea vinea quasi noxii projiciantur. Haeretici itaque qui in Evangelio zizania vocantur (Matth. XIII), ipsi in Isaia propheta lapides appellantur, et 527Bin Canticis canticorum eleganti sermone sub nomine vulpium describuntur, sicut scriptum est: « Capite nobis vulpes parvulas, quae demoliuntur vineas nostras (Cant. II). » Quod autem Propheta dicit: [Et plantavit illam electam], hoc est nimirum, quod Dominus in Evangelio ait: « Ergo vos elegi de mundo ut eatis et fructum afferatis et fructus vester maneat (Joan. XV). » Et Paulus apostolus in Epistolis suis: « Ego, inquit, plantavi, » et Dei: « agricultura estis (I Cor. XIII). » De apostolis vero dicitur: « Isti viventes in carne plantaverunt Ecclesiam sanguine suo. » Turris quoque quam propheta Isaias in medio vineae aedificatam testatur fuisse, ipsa siquidem est nomen Domini, de quo per Salomonem dicitur: 527C« Turris fortissima nomen Domini, ad eam confugiet justus, et salvabitur (Prov. XVIII). » Homines namque ideo ad turrim confugiunt ut ab infestatione hostium liberari queant. Bene itaque nomen Domini turri comparatur in qua homines salvantur, quia sicut ait Petrus apostolus: « Non enim est aliud nomen datum sub coelo in quo oporteat salvari, nisi in nomine Domini nostri Jesu Christi (Act. IV). » Torcular autem quod in vinea supradicta constructum fuit, sacrosancti altaris figuram et imaginem nobis repraesentavit, quia sicut in torculari vel praelo uva exprimitur, et expressa in vinum dulcissimum eliquatur, sic gloriose atque mirabiliter quotidie corpus Christi et sanguis in altari consecratur et litatur. Illud vero quod sequitur in Isaia, quia 527Dexspectavit Dominus vineam suam ut faceret uvas, et ecce, fecit labruscas; dictum de haereticis et falsis Christianis intelligamus. Haereticis enim qui sacramentis ecclesiasticis 308 non consentiunt, imo perversis dogmatibus obviant et repugnant, et falsi christiani qui confitentur se nosse Deum, factis autem negant, isti procul dubio pro uvis spinas et labruscas, id est inutiles fructus et noxios Domino generant et apportant. Verumtamen quid de talibus in die judicii fieri debeat propheta in subsequentibus suae narrationis manifestat, dicens: « Nunc ergo viri Juda et habitatores Jerusalem, judicate inter me et vineam meam, id est de vinea mea (Isa. V). » 528AViros itaque Juda et habitatores Jerusalem, specialiter apostolos et caeteros sanctos intelligere debemus, quos sine dubio illius coelestis Jerusalem quae sursum est mater nostra, cives et habitatores esse scimus. Quibus itaque remota omni ambiguitate Dominus in Evangelio suo repromittit dicens: « Vos qui secuti estis me, sedebitis super sedes duodecim. judicantes duodecim tribus Israel (Matth. XIX). » Et Paulus apostolus: « Nescitis, inquit, quoniam sancti de mundo judicabunt? (I Cor. VI.) » Et alio in loco de omnibus sanctis scriptum est: « Judicabunt sancti nationes, et dominabuntur populis (Sap. III). » De istis videlicet apostolis et caeteris sanctis coram majestate Dei in aeterna jam praedestinatione sublimatis, Zacharias propheta gloriam eorum contemplatus 528Bprophetavit, dicens: « Isti sunt duo filii olei splendoris qui assistant Dominatori universae terrae (Zachar. IV). » Per binarium quippe numerum, ideo propheta apostolos caeterosque sanctos designavit, quia duo sunt praecepta charitatis, quorum observatio et impletio sanctos et amicos Dei efficit. Quis autem sit ille qui per oleum designetur, superius jam praelibavimus, cum illa sententia de Isaia a nobis exponeretur. Sciendum praeterea quod in Zacharia propheta amplius aliquid dicitur quam in Isaia. Deus namque omnipotens, quia ipse et vere misericors et vera misericordia est, in Isaia simpliciter oleum appellatur, in Zacharia vero cum additamento, splendoris oleum nominatur. Nec immerito Deus Pater 528Comnipotens dicitur esse oleum splendoris, quia ejus Unigenitus filius ab Apostolo vocatur splendor et figura substantiae ipsius (Hebr. I). Splendor est igitur Pater, splendor est Filius, splendor est nihilominus Spiritus sanctus, non tamen tres splendores sunt, sed splendor unus. Siquidem simili modo in catholica fide credimus et pronuntiamus, Deus est Pater, Deus est Filius, Deus est Spiritus sanctus; nec tamen tres dii sunt, sed unus Deus. Quia igitur, sicut superius diximus, eleos Graece misericors vel misericordia Latine dicitur, consequenter per oleum ipse qui est misericors, et Pater misericordiarum Deus intelligitur. Si ergo per oleum accipimus Deum Patrem, datur nobis intelligi ut ejus Filium interpretemur olei splendorem. Oleum igitur splendoris, 528Det splendor olei ingenitus genitor est, et unigenitus Genitoris Dei.

RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING.

Goldsmiths, who are wont to work in gold and silver or in precious stones, when they fashion some precious vessel between their hands — there are perhaps some men who praise and marvel at that vessel which they behold, and are delighted with the wondrous variety of the craftsman's work; but the craftsmen nevertheless still set beneath the hammers the vessel which they have in their hands, thin it with files, adorn it with gems; and although to the men standing by that vessel already seems on every side to be perfect, to the craftsmen it seems to be less polished and perfect. And so in that manner, when we treated above that saying 525A— namely, "Thy name is oil poured out" — although what we have said may perhaps please some, yet still, by the helping grace of the Holy Spirit, we can add something to the earlier treatment which may not displease the hearing of readers and hearers alike. "Thy name is oil poured out; therefore the maidens have loved thee (Cant. I)." This is the voice of holy Church, which praises the clemency of her Bridegroom, and his ineffable charity, in this two-membered utterance. Nor is it any wonder if the Bride praises the clemency of her Bridegroom, since the Bridegroom himself also does not keep silent about the affection and tenderness which he has had toward his Bride. For thus he himself says through Jeremiah the prophet: « With an everlasting charity have I loved thee, therefore have I drawn thee, taking pity (cap. XXXI). Concerning this ineffable charity 525Bof Christ, indeed, wherewith he himself loved holy Church, John the apostle says: Herein is the charity of God perfected in us, not because we have loved him, but because he first loved us (I Joan. IV). » [Vulg. I Joan. IV, 10 non quasi nos dilexerimus Deum, "not as though we had loved God"] Moreover it is pleasing to consider whether in the Old Testament or in the prophets anything said about oil can be found which agrees with the name of the Bridegroom and can becomingly be fitted to the mysteries of the Bride, that is, of holy Church. For in Exodus the Lord says to Moses: « Command the children of Israel that they bring thee the purest oil from the olive trees, and pounded with a pestle, that a lamp may burn always before me in the tabernacle of the testimony (cap XXVII). » Isaiah the prophet also, making mention of oil, says: « A vineyard was made for my beloved 525Con a horn, the son of oil; and he hedged it in, and picked the stones out of it, and planted it a choice vine, and built a tower in the midst of it, set up a winepress in it, and looked that it should bring forth grapes, and it brought forth wild grapes (cap. V). » But in Zechariah the prophet it is thus written of oil: « These are the two sons of the oil of splendour, who stand before the ruler of the whole earth (cap. IV). » [Vulg. Zach. IV, 14 isti sunt duo filii olei qui assistunt Dominatori universae terrae — without splendoris, "of splendour"] For since in the earlier treatment, aided by the help of the divinity of Christ and somewhat made fruitful by the dew of spiritual understanding, we discoursed of the seven natures of oil — not indeed as is worthy, but according to the small measure of our wit — and assigned those same natures to the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit, which are in Christ in their fullness; it now remains that we speak something further about oil under the allegorical sense, which may agree both with Christ and with holy Church, 525Dand may morally suit each and every one of the faithful. Let us see, therefore, what it is that Isaiah the excellent prophet says: « A vineyard, he says, was made for my beloved on a horn, the son of oil. » O thou, Lord Jesus Christ, who art the son of the highest oil, unveil the spiritual eyes of my heart, that I may be able to consider the wondrous mysteries which are hidden from eternity in thy holy and most blessed law. Eleos indeed in Greek is called in Latin misericordia, mercy. For the Lord says in the Gospel: « Be merciful, as your Father also is merciful (Luc. VI). » David the prophet also, refreshed by the superabundant grace of the Holy Spirit, speaks thus of God: « My God, my mercy (Psal. LVIII). » God therefore almighty, ineffably loving and 307 especially clement, who in Isaiah is called oil, 526Ais himself doubtless called merciful in the Gospel and in his Only-begotten Son, and is proclaimed mercy by David the prophet. To the most beloved Son, therefore, of this ineffable and incomparable oil a certain most dear vineyard was made on a most excellent horn, that is, in the humanity which he took from our sinful mass; which humanity so surpassed the remaining mass, from which it was taken, by the power of the divinity that took it, as a horn is wont to surpass that flesh out of which, in rising, it is seen to proceed. But concerning this horn, that is, concerning the incarnation of the Lord Jesus, Zacharias, filled with the grace of the Holy Spirit, said, « Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, because he hath visited and wrought the redemption of his people (Luc. I). » And as though someone were asking of him what mercy the God of Israel 526Bhad done to his people, he straightway added, saying: « He hath raised up a horn of salvation in the house of David his servant (ibid.). » [Vulg. Luc. I, 69 erexit cornu salutis nobis, "a horn of salvation for us"] If we should wish to interpret that horn otherwise than the truth of the matter holds, truth itself, propped upon authority, and authority strengthened by faithful truth, would contradict us. For it is manifest that a royal Virgin of David's stock was chosen, from whom and in whom that horn — that is, our humanity — was taken up by the Word of the Father; and if this had not been done, the human race, which had perished, could in no wise be saved. And this is the horn of salvation which has been raised up for us, namely the Word made flesh for our salvation, which dwelt among us (Joan. I). On this horn, therefore, is the vineyard most dear to the beloved Son of oil; that is, through the humanity which 526Che took, the Son of almighty God acquired holy Church for himself. But that the Son himself is called beloved, his Bride bears witness in the Song of Songs: « My beloved, she says, is white and ruddy (Cant. V). » Ruddy, because of the shedding of blood on the cross; white, because of the prerogative of virginity and chastity. Again in the same little book his most dear Bride speaks thus of the same Bridegroom of hers: « My beloved, she says, shall abide between my breasts (Cant. I). » Holy Church therefore, which in Isaiah is called a vineyard, is herself called Bride in the Song of Songs. This vineyard indeed was made most dear in delights to the beloved Son of oil, from the time when he took flesh from her, since as Paul says: « No man ever hated his own flesh (Ephes. V). » And in another 526Dplace the same Paul more plainly intimates what love the Bridegroom has toward his Bride: « Husbands, he says, love your wives, as Christ loved holy Church and delivered himself up for her (Col. III). » [Vulg. Ephes. V, 25 sicut et Christus dilexit ecclesiam, et se ipsum tradidit pro ea] Whence the same Bridegroom in the Song of Songs says of his Bride: « How beautiful art thou, my love, how beautiful art thou; thy eyes are those of doves! (Cant. I.) » [Vulg. Cant. I, 14 ecce tu pulchra es, amica mea, ecce tu pulchra, oculi tui columbarum] The Bridegroom doubles the beauty of his Bride by repetition for this reason: because he foreknew her a virgin and chaste in soul and in body. And he compared her eyes to doves, because he foresaw that she had that simplicity of faith which heretics lack.
