But nevertheless, what do we judge this to be that the prophet says, that God hedged in his vineyard, that is, holy Church? holy Church is indeed called a vineyard, 527Anot only in Isaiah, but in the book of the Gospels and in the Song of Songs: in the Gospel, where the householder hired workmen into his vineyard (Matth. XX); but in the Song of Songs, where it is written: « There was a vineyard for the Peaceable One, » and, « Our vineyard hath flourished (Cant. II). » This vineyard, therefore, the Lord surrounded with a hedge, when he fortified it with the sayings of the prophets, and, lest it could be trodden down and torn asunder by heretics, instructed it with apostolic teachings as with a strong garrison. Whence it well indeed follows in the same prophet, that « he picked the stones out of it (Isa. V). » For stones are picked out of a vineyard by men for this reason, that they may be cast out of that vineyard as harmful things. And so the heretics, who in the Gospel are called cockle (Matth. XIII), are themselves called stones in Isaiah the prophet, and 527Bin the Song of Songs are described in elegant speech under the name of foxes, as it is written: « Catch us the little foxes, which destroy our vineyards (Cant. II). » But what the Prophet says: [And he planted it a choice vine], this doubtless is what the Lord says in the Gospel: « Therefore I have chosen you out of the world, that you may go and bring forth fruit, and that your fruit may remain (Joan. XV). » [Vulg. Joan. XV, 16 ego elegi vos et posui vos, ut eatis; de mundo stands at verse 19] And Paul the apostle in his Epistles: « I, he says, have planted, » and, of God: « you are the husbandry (I Cor. XIII). » But of the apostles it is said: « These, living in the flesh, planted the Church with their own blood. » The tower too, which the prophet Isaiah testifies to have been built in the midst of the vineyard, is itself the name of the Lord, of which it is said through Solomon: 527C« The name of the Lord is a most strong tower; to it the just man shall flee, and shall be saved (Prov. XVIII). » [Vulg. Prov. XVIII, 10 ad ipsum currit justus et exaltabitur, "the just runneth to it and shall be exalted"] For men flee to a tower for this reason, that they may be freed from the harassing of enemies. And so the name of the Lord is well compared to a tower in which men are saved, since as Peter the apostle says: « For there is no other name given under heaven wherein we must be saved, save in the name of our Lord Jesus Christ (Act. IV). » [Vulg. Act. IV, 12 nec enim aliud nomen est sub caelo datum hominibus, in quo oporteat nos salvos fieri, without the closing clause] But the winepress which was built in the aforesaid vineyard represented to us the figure and image of the most holy altar, since as in a winepress or press the grape is squeezed, and being squeezed is strained into most sweet wine, so gloriously and wondrously each day the body and blood of Christ is consecrated and offered upon the altar. But that which follows in Isaiah, that 527Dthe Lord looked for his vineyard to bring forth grapes, and behold, it brought forth wild grapes — let us understand it as said of heretics and false Christians. For the heretics, who do 308 not consent to the ecclesiastical sacraments, nay rather withstand and fight against them with perverse doctrines, and the false Christians who profess that they know God but by their deeds deny him — these beyond doubt beget and bring to the Lord thorns and wild grapes instead of grapes, that is, useless and harmful fruits. But nevertheless what ought to be done with such men on the day of judgment the prophet makes plain in what follows in his narrative, saying: « Now therefore, men of Judah and inhabitants of Jerusalem, judge between me and my vineyard, that is, concerning my vineyard (Isa. V). » [Vulg. Isa. V, 3 habitatores Jerusalem et vir Juda, judicate inter me et inter vineam meam] 528AAnd so by the men of Judah and the inhabitants of Jerusalem we ought to understand especially the apostles and the other saints, whom without doubt we know to be citizens and inhabitants of that heavenly Jerusalem which is above, our mother. And so to them, all ambiguity removed, the Lord promises in his Gospel, saying: « You who have followed me shall sit upon twelve seats. judging the twelve tribes of Israel (Matth. XIX). » And Paul the apostle: « Know you not, he says, that the saints shall judge the world? (I Cor. VI.) » And in another place it is written of all the saints: « The saints shall judge the nations, and shall rule over the peoples (Sap. III). » Of these apostles, namely, and of the other saints, already exalted before the majesty of God in eternal predestination, Zechariah the prophet, contemplating their glory, 528Bprophesied, saying: « These are the two sons of the oil of splendour who stand before the Ruler of the whole earth (Zachar. IV). » For by the number two the prophet signified the apostles and the other saints for this reason: because there are two precepts of charity, the observance and fulfilling of which makes men saints and friends of God. But who he is that is signified by the oil we have already touched upon above, when that saying from Isaiah was expounded by us. It is to be known, moreover, that in Zechariah the prophet something more is said than in Isaiah. For God almighty, because he himself is both truly merciful and true mercy, is in Isaiah simply called oil, but in Zechariah, with an addition, is named the oil of splendour. And not undeservedly is God the Father 528Calmighty said to be the oil of splendour, since his Only-begotten Son is called by the Apostle the splendour and figure of his substance (Hebr. I). Splendour therefore is the Father, splendour is the Son, splendour none the less is the Holy Spirit; yet they are not three splendours, but one splendour. For indeed in like manner in the catholic faith we believe and declare: God is the Father, God is the Son, God is the Holy Spirit; and yet they are not three gods, but one God. Since therefore, as we said above, eleos in Greek is called in Latin misericors or misericordia, merciful or mercy, consequently by the oil is understood he who is merciful, and God the Father of mercies. If therefore by the oil we take God the Father, it is given us to understand that we should interpret his Son as the splendour of the oil. The oil of splendour, therefore, 528Dand the splendour of the oil, is the unbegotten begetter, and the only-begotten of God the Begetter.

RECAPITULATIO A SUPERIORIBUS. QUALITER REFERRI DEBEAT AD SYNAGOGAM ISTA SENTENTIA VINEAE.

« Vinea, inquit, facta est dilecto meo in cornu filio olei (Isa V). » Quae sit autem vinea idem propheta manifestius declarat cum dicit: « Vinea Domini exercituum, domus Israel est, et viri Juda, germen delectabile ejus (ibid.). » De hac quoque vinea loquitur David propheta: « Vineam, inquiens de Aegypto transtulisti, ejecisti gentes, et plantasti, eam (Psal. LXXIX); » et caetera quae sequuntur in 529Aipso psalmo de eadem vinea quae facile possunt intelligi sine interpretatione aliqua. Haec est, inquam, illa vinea scilicet plebs Judaica, de qua Salvator in Evangelio ait: « Homo quidam paterfamilias plantavit vineam, et locavit eam agricolis. Et factum est cum appropinquasset tempus vindemiarum misit servos suos ad agricolas, ut reciperent fructum vineae ab agricolis. Agricolae autem videntes servos patrisfamilias apprehenderunt eos, et multis contumeliis affectos interfecerunt. Audiens vero paterfamilias quod accidisset, ait: Mittam unigenitum filium meum charissimum ad eos, forsitan verebuntur eum. At illi viso filio Domini sui dixerunt adinvicem: Ecce hic est haeres: Iste igitur occidatur, et sic ad nos haereditas transferetur (Luc. XX). » Quis 529Bergo iste paterfamilias rectius potest intelligi quam Deus Pater omnipotens Domini nostri Jesu Christi? Ipse enim est verus paterfamilias, qui universum mundum clementi pietate gubernat et regit, et secundum suam irreprehensibilem sapientiam Christianos, Judaeos, paganos, haereticos disponit. Hae namque sunt familiae, quarum pater iste paterfamilias esse dicitur, et quarum providentiam ipse habere non dedignatur. Providet enim idem piissimus paterfamilias Christianis, videlicet filiis suis ea conditione, ut si ejus voluerint obsequi praeceptis poenas inferni valeant devitare, et gaudia paradisi indeficientia possint invenire. Judaeorum denique curam idem paterfamilias se habere demonstrat, quia, ut ad 529Cipsum revertantur, clementi bonitate exspectat. Paganos et haereticos a sua providentia nullatenus repellit, quia licet beneficiis ipsius, sicut mali servi, ingrati existant, tamen patienti longanimitate adhuc Dominus exspectat, ut relictis haeresibus et idolis ad se redeant. Iste igitur paterfamilias vineam plantavit, quando Israeliticum 309 populum de Aegypto in manu potenti et brachio excelso eduxit, et eum in terram repromissionis expulsis alienigenis gentibus habitare fecit. Et locavit eam agricolis, quando praecepit summis sacerdotibus et scribis et Pharisaeis, ut eumdem populum secundum legem Moysi erudirent, et ab appetitu totius mali, quantum fas est mortalibus, cohiberent. Servos autem suos misit idem paterfamilias ad supradictam 529Dvineam quando prophetas destinavit ad erudiendam eamdem Israeliticam gentem. Agricolae vero cognoscentes servos Domini sui, nonnullos ex ipsis contumeliis affecerunt, aliquibus mortem immeritam intulerunt. Si enim sacrae Scripturae paginis studiosius intenti esse volumus, paucos admodum electorum reperire poterimus, qui ab impiis aut contumeliis non sunt affecti, aut crudelissimae morti injuste traditi. Manifestum vero est quod impiissimi Judaei Isaiam, et Jeremiam crudeli morte damnaverunt, reliquos prophetas fere omnes multis contumeliis et convitiis dehonestaverunt. Filius autem patrisfamilias qui ab agricolis interficitur Dominus Jesus Christus est, qui a summis sacerdotibus et scribis et pharisaeis crucifigitur. Sed jam ad superiorem 530Asententiam redeamus, de qua sub typo Synagogae loqui disposueramus. « Vinea, inquit Isaias, facta est dilecto meo, in cornu filio olei. Quoniam quidem de vinea aliquantulum, licet minus sufficienter, diximus, restat nunc ut ubi Deus Filius dilectus appellatur, insinuemus. In Evangelio siquidem scriptum est quia descendit Spiritus sanctus super Dominum Jesum in columbae specie, et vox Patris audita est, dicens: Hic est filius meus dilectus in quo mihi complacuit, ipsum audite (Luc. IX). » De isto namque dilecto ipso charissimo loquitur Sponsa in eodem de quo loquimur libro: « Dilectus, inquit, meus mihi, et ego illi, qui pascitur in liliis, donec aspiret dies, et inclinentur umbrae (Cant. II). » Ac si dicat: Dilectus meus mihi foederabitur fide, et ergo 530Bfoederabor illi fide et operatione, qui pascitur in liliis, hoc est, delectatur operibus castis, piis et justis, donec aspiret dies, et umbrae inclinentur, id est donec appareat dies judicii, et peccatorum imagines ad nihilum redigantur. Huic ergo dilecto vinea contraria in cornu exstitit, id est valde Synagoga contradixit humanitati quam Christus assumpsit; in tantum ut nunquam in eum vellet credere, quem quasi hominem solummodo et non Deum videbat visui humano subjacere. Vinea, inquam, ista facta est nimis amara huic dilecto, quando Synagoga Dominum Jesum in humanitate qua indutus erat potavit felle et aceto. Recordari debes, qui haec audis, quia superius dixi per cornu debere intelligi Filii Dei 530Chumanitatem, et ex auctoritate sacri eloquii demonstravi satis rationem sufficientem. Quare autem Filius Dei vocetur per Isaiam filius olei, brevi ratione replicare debemus, quia hoc jam superius satis idonee perdocuimus. Oleos enim Graece, misericordia Latine dicitur. Quia igitur Pater misericors et misericordia est, constat pro certo Christum esse Filium Dei olei, quoniam ipse est Filius misericordis Dei. Hic igitur dilectus filius olei, id est Christus Filius Dei suam vineam, scilicet Synagogam sepivit, quando eam patriarcharum exemplis et institutis edocuit, et prophetarum libris munivit, ne videlicet alium Dominum praeter ipsum admitteret, et ne singularis ille ferus de quo Psalmista conqueritur (Psal. LXXIX), scilicet diabolus, illam devastaret. 530DIdcirco enim vinea sepitur ut Domino ipsius vineae fructus ejus illaesus conservetur, et hostibus, et animalibus claustra ipsius non facile reserentur. Quod autem dicitur: « Elegit Dominus lapides ex illa (Isa. V); » constat quia post electionem apostolorum expulsa sit ipsa Synagoga a Deo et abjecta. De qua utique abjectione bene in Isaia scriptum reperitur, ubi Dominus de Sobna Scriba et praeposito templi loquitur: « Ecce, inquit Isaias, o tu Sobna; ecce, inquit, Dominus expellet te de statione tua, et asportari te faciet sicut asportatur gallus gallinaceus (Isa. XXII). » Simili quoque modo de abjectione Synagogae sub persona Jeconiae regis scriptum est: « Si fuerit Jeconias rex Juda sicut annulus aureus in manu mea, dicit Dominus, inde evellam eum et projiciam 531A(Jer. XXII). » Quasi enim Sponsa expulsa est de statione sua, quando Synagoga ab illa pristini sui status gloria ob meritum suae iniquitatis est dejecta, et quasi gallus gallinaceus eadem Synagoga asportata est, quando propter infructuositatem et imperfectionem suam ab exteriori intellectu et Judaica superstitione remota est. Gallum quippe gallinaceum novimus esse animal castratum et infructuosum, cui bene antiqua lex comparatur, quae sicut dicit beatus Paulus, nihil ad perfectum adduxit, unde merito sequitur in Isaia propheta de eodem Sobna: « Quid tu hic, aut quasi quis hic? (Isa. XXII). » Sine dubio enim, quid tu agis hic, illi dicere solemus cujus opus minime approbamus. Et quasi quis, non revera ut aliquis perfectus locum aliquem obtinet, 531Bsed tamen opus dignum meritis sui officii vel loci non exercet. Possumus sane illam electionem lapidum, de qua superius praefati sumus referre ad reges et prophetas quos a Deo in Synagoga electos esse non dubitamus. Quod autem dicit propheta Isaias, quia Dominus illam vineam electam plantavit; manifeste datur intelligi quoniam quidem reges et prophetae totaque gens Israelitica de stirpe Abraham, 310 qui electus est a Deo originem duxit. Enimvero multum ista plantatio degeneravit ab illa gloriosa et paterna vita, de cujus radice videbatur propaginem ducere. Quam utique degenerationem Dominus in Evangelio, benignissima improperatione Judaeis exprobrabat, dicens: « Si semen Abrahae 531Cestis, opera Abrahae facite, et utquid vultis me interficere? hoc Abraham non fecit (Joan. VIII). » Admodum namque Judaei degeneraverant ab illa gloria sanguinis Abrahae patriarchae patris sui. Abraham enim Deum dilexit, honoravit et adoravit; gens vero Israelitica Deum odio habuit, dehonestavit, et in cruce crudelissima damnavit. Turris autem quae in medio vineae per prophetam a Domino aedificata fuisse dicitur, significat templum antiquis temporibus, pro dignitate sui famosissimum, quod nullus ignorat in medio gentis Judaicae, praecepto Dei esse constructum. Torcular vero quod in supra memorata vinea exstitit, altare nobis insinuat quod gens Israelitica in medio sui in magna veneratione habebat. Quod nimirum altare bene comparatur torculari, 531Dquia sicut in torculari multae uvae expressae in vini liquorem rediguntur, sic diversae multaeque victimae quae in altari positae cremabantur, ad unum sacrificium, per significationem, id est ad Christum referebantur. Quod autem dicit propheta, quia exspectavit Dominus vineam suam, ut faceret uvas, et fecit labruscas; manifestis indiciis denuntiat, quoniam quidem ista sententia ad Judaeos maxime pertineat. Nonne ipsi Judaei pro uvis labruscas attulerunt, quando Creatorem suum ante faciem Pilati negaverunt? (Joan. XIX.) Quod utique beatus Petrus eis improperat, dicens: « Vos autem virum homicidam donare vobis petiistis, auctorem vero 532Avitae morti tradidistis, quem Deus suscitavit a mortuis (Act. III). » Nonne Judaei sceleratissimi labruscas Domino fecerunt pro uvis, quando dixerunt Pilato: « Crucifige eum; » Et: « Si hunc dimittis, non es amicus Caesaris (Joan. XIX). » Propterea illud quod Isaias dicit in subseqnentibus: « Nunc, viri Juda et habitatores Jerusalem, judicate inter me et vineam meam (Isa. V), » pertinere ad generalem resurrectionem nullus ignorat, in qua sancti apostoli istam vineam judicabunt et condemnabunt. Hoc denique Dominus in Evangelio ipsis Judaeis sibi detrahentibus, testatur cum dicit: « Si ego, ut dicitis, in principe daemoniorum ejicio daemones, filii vestri in quo ejiciunt? (Matth. XII.) » Ideo ipsi vestri judices erunt. Quod etiam iste Isaias propheta declarat, 532Bcum de adventu Domini et sanctorum apostolorum ad diem judicii prophetavit, dicens: « Dominus ad judicium cum senioribus populi sui veniet (Isa. III). » Salomon quoque Christum, et sanctam Ecclesiam, et sanctos apostolos, et diem judicii brevi sermone curavit comprehendere cum dicit: « Nobilis in portis vir sanctae Ecclesiae, cum sederit cum senatoribus populi sui (Prov. XXXI). » Apostoli itaque qui in Evangelio duodecim judices, et filii Judaeorum secundum carnem appellantur, ipsi nimirum in Isaia viri Juda et habitatores Jerusalem senesque populi vocantur, et a Salomone senatores populi nominantur. Isti sunt nimirum duo filii olei splendoris quos Zacharias propheta (Cap. IV) in spiritu 532Cprophetiae dicit assistere Dominatori universae terrae. Quod ideo per binarium numerum descripsit, quia eos prophetico spiritu divinitatem et humanitatem Christi praedicare praevidit. Porro illud quod idem propheta dicit: « Isti sunt qui assistunt Dominatori universae terrae, » non solum de apostolis, verum etiam de omnibus sanctis dictum esse intelligamus. Unde Domiuus ipse in Evangelio suo ait: « Pater volo ut ubi ego sum, illic sit et minister meus (Joan. XII). » Quod etiam beatus Augustinus doctor egregius, et catholicae fidei defensor fidelissimus testatur cum dicit: « Sciamus pro certo, quia post ascensionem Domini ad coelos, omnes sanctorum animae cum Christo sunt, et exeuntes de corpore ad Christum vadunt, sicut scriptum est: Justorum animae 532Din manu Dei sunt (Sap. III), exspectantes resurrectionem corporis sui, ut integra et perpetua beatitudine cum Christo pariter perfruantur. Similiter quoque et peccatorum animae in inferno sunt positae, sub timore exspectantes resurrectionem sui corporis, ut cum diabolo convertantur ad poenam aeternam.

RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING. IN WHAT MANNER THIS SAYING OF THE VINEYARD OUGHT TO BE REFERRED TO THE SYNAGOGUE.

« A vineyard, he says, was made for my beloved on a horn, the son of oil (Isa V). » But what the vineyard is the same prophet declares more plainly when he says: « The vineyard of the Lord of hosts is the house of Israel, and the men of Judah his delightful plant (ibid.). » Of this vineyard David the prophet also speaks: « Thou hast brought a vineyard, saying, out of Egypt; thou hast cast out the nations, and planted, it (Psal. LXXIX); » and the rest which follows in 529Athat psalm concerning the same vineyard, which can easily be understood without any interpretation. This, I say, is that vineyard, namely the Jewish people, of which the Saviour says in the Gospel: « A certain man, a householder, planted a vineyard, and let it out to husbandmen. And it came to pass, when the time of the vintage drew near, that he sent his servants to the husbandmen, that they might receive the fruit of the vineyard from the husbandmen. But the husbandmen, seeing the servants of the householder, laid hold on them, and, having afflicted them with many insults, killed them. But the householder, hearing what had happened, said: I will send my only-begotten, most dear son to them; perhaps they will reverence him. But they, seeing the son of their Lord, said one to another: Behold, here is the heir: let him therefore be killed, and so the inheritance will be transferred to us (Luc. XX). » [Vulg. Luc. XX, 9 homo plantavit vineam et locavit eam colonis; and XX, 14 hic est heres, occidamus illum, ut nostra fiat hereditas] Who 529Bthen can more rightly be understood as this householder than God the Father almighty of our Lord Jesus Christ? For he is the true householder, who governs and rules the whole world with clement tenderness, and according to his irreproachable wisdom disposes Christians, Jews, pagans, heretics. For these are the households, of which that householder is said to be father, and whose providence he does not disdain to have. For that same most loving householder provides for the Christians, that is, for his own sons, on this condition, that if they will obey his precepts they may be able to escape the punishments of hell, and may be able to find the unfailing joys of paradise. And that same householder shows that he has care of the Jews, since he waits with clement goodness that they may return to 529Chimself. Pagans and heretics he in no wise drives from his providence, since although, like bad servants, they are ungrateful for his benefits, yet the Lord still waits with patient long-suffering that, leaving their heresies and their idols, they may come back to him. This householder therefore planted a vineyard when he led the Israelite 309 people out of Egypt with a mighty hand and an outstretched arm, and made them dwell in the land of promise, the foreign nations having been driven out. And he let it out to husbandmen when he charged the high priests and the scribes and the Pharisees to instruct that same people according to the law of Moses, and to restrain them from the appetite of all evil, so far as is lawful to mortals. And that same householder sent his servants to the aforesaid 529Dvineyard when he appointed the prophets to instruct that same Israelite nation. But the husbandmen, recognizing the servants of their Lord, afflicted several of them with insults, and upon some inflicted an undeserved death. For if we are willing to be more diligently intent upon the pages of Holy Scripture, we shall be able to find very few indeed of the elect who have not either been afflicted with insults by the impious, or unjustly delivered over to a most cruel death. And it is manifest that the most impious Jews condemned Isaiah and Jeremiah to a cruel death, and dishonoured almost all the remaining prophets with many insults and revilings. But the son of the householder who is killed by the husbandmen is the Lord Jesus Christ, who is crucified by the high priests and the scribes and the Pharisees. But let us now return to the foregoing 530Asaying, of which we had purposed to speak under the figure of the Synagogue. « A vineyard, says Isaiah, was made for my beloved, on a horn, the son of oil. Since indeed we have said somewhat, though less than sufficiently, about the vineyard, it now remains that we set forth where the Son of God is called beloved. For in the Gospel it is written that the Holy Spirit came down upon the Lord Jesus in the likeness of a dove, and the voice of the Father was heard, saying: This is my beloved son, in whom I am well pleased; hear ye him (Luc. IX). » [Vulg. Luc. IX, 35 hic est Filius meus electus, ipsum audite, "my chosen son"] For of this beloved, this most dear one, the Bride speaks in the very book of which we are speaking: « My beloved, she says, to me, and I to him, who feedeth among the lilies, until the day break, and the shadows decline (Cant. II). » As if she said: My beloved shall be joined to me by faith, and therefore 530BI shall be joined to him by faith and by work, who feedeth among the lilies, that is, is delighted with chaste and loving and just works, until the day break, and the shadows decline — that is, until the day of judgment appear, and the images of sins be brought to nothing. To this beloved, therefore, the vineyard on the horn showed itself contrary, that is, the Synagogue exceedingly contradicted the humanity which Christ took; so far that it would never believe in him whom it saw lying open to human sight as a man only, and not as God. This vineyard, I say, was made exceedingly bitter to this beloved, when the Synagogue gave the Lord Jesus gall and vinegar to drink in the humanity wherewith he was clothed. Thou who hearest these things oughtest to remember that I said above that by the horn is to be understood the humanity of the Son of God, 530Cand that from the authority of sacred speech I showed a sufficient reason. But why the Son of God is called by Isaiah the son of oil we ought to repeat in a brief reason, since we have already taught this above sufficiently and fitly. For Oleos in Greek is called misericordia, mercy, in Latin. Since therefore the Father is merciful and is mercy, it stands for certain that Christ is the Son of the God of oil, since he is the Son of the merciful God. This beloved son of oil, therefore, that is, Christ the Son of God, hedged in his vineyard, that is, the Synagogue, when he taught it with the examples and institutions of the patriarchs and fortified it with the books of the prophets, lest, namely, it should admit any other Lord beside himself, and lest that singular wild beast of which the Psalmist complains (Psal. LXXIX), namely the devil, should lay it waste. 530DFor a vineyard is hedged in for this reason, that its fruit may be kept unharmed for the lord of that vineyard, and that its barriers may not be easily unbarred to enemies and to beasts. But as to what is said: « The Lord picked the stones out of it (Isa. V); » it stands that after the election of the apostles that Synagogue was driven out by God and cast off. And of this casting-off it is well found written in Isaiah, where the Lord speaks of Sobna the scribe and provost of the temple: « Behold, says Isaiah, O thou Sobna; behold, he says, the Lord will drive thee out of thy station, and will cause thee to be carried away as a cock is carried away (Isa. XXII). » [Vulg. Isa. XXII, 17 carries no expulsion clause here; the words about the station stand at verse 19 as et expellam te de statione tua] In like manner also it is written of the casting-off of the Synagogue under the person of king Jechonias: « If Jechonias king of Judah were as a ring of gold on my hand, saith the Lord, thence would I pluck him away and cast him off 531A(Jer. XXII). » [Vulg. Jer. XXII, 24 Jechonias filius Joachim rex Juda anulus in manu dextera mea, inde avellam eum] For she was as it were a Bride driven out of her station, when the Synagogue was cast down from that glory of her former state for the desert of her iniquity; and that same Synagogue was carried away as a cock, when, because of her unfruitfulness and imperfection, she was removed from outward understanding and from Jewish superstition. For we know that a cock is a beast castrated and unfruitful, to which the ancient law is well compared, which, as blessed Paul says, brought nothing to perfection; whence it deservedly follows in Isaiah the prophet concerning the same Sobna: « What dost thou here, or as it were who here? (Isa. XXII). » For without doubt we are wont to say "what doest thou here" to him whose work we by no means approve. And "as it were who," not that he truly holds some place as a perfect man, 531Bbut yet does not carry out work worthy of the merits of his office or of his place. We can indeed refer that election of the stones, of which we spoke above, to the kings and prophets whom we do not doubt to have been chosen by God in the Synagogue. But as to what the prophet Isaiah says, that the Lord planted that vineyard a choice vine, it is plainly given us to understand that indeed the kings and prophets and the whole Israelite nation drew their origin from the stock of Abraham, 310 who was chosen by God. But indeed that planting much degenerated from that glorious and fatherly life, from whose root it seemed to draw its shoot. Which degeneration the Lord in the Gospel reproached to the Jews with a most kindly upbraiding, saying: « If you are the seed of Abraham, 531Cdo the works of Abraham; and why do you wish to kill me? this Abraham did not do (Joan. VIII). » [Vulg. Joan. VIII, 39–40 si filii Abrahae estis, "if you are the sons of Abraham"; and nunc autem quaeritis me interficere, "but now you seek to kill me"] For the Jews had greatly degenerated from that glory of the blood of Abraham the patriarch, their father. For Abraham loved, honoured and adored God; but the Israelite nation hated God, dishonoured him, and condemned him on a most cruel cross. But the tower which is said by the prophet to have been built by the Lord in the midst of the vineyard signifies the temple, most famous in ancient times for its dignity, which no one is ignorant was built in the midst of the Jewish nation by the precept of God. But the winepress which stood in the aforementioned vineyard intimates to us the altar which the Israelite nation held in great veneration in the midst of itself. Which altar indeed is well compared to a winepress, 531Dsince as in a winepress many grapes are squeezed and reduced into the liquor of wine, so the divers and many victims which, being placed on the altar, were burned, were referred by their signification to one sacrifice, that is, to Christ. But as to what the prophet says, that the Lord looked for his vineyard to bring forth grapes, and it brought forth wild grapes: he announces by manifest tokens that this saying belongs above all to the Jews. Did not the Jews themselves bring wild grapes instead of grapes, when they denied their Creator before the face of Pilate? (Joan. XIX.) Which indeed blessed Peter upbraids to them, saying: « But you asked that a murderer should be given to you, and delivered over to death the author 532Aof life, whom God raised from the dead (Act. III). » [Vulg. Act. III, 15 auctorem vero vitae interfecistis, "but you killed the author of life"] Did not the most wicked Jews make wild grapes for the Lord instead of grapes, when they said to Pilate: « Crucify him; » And: « If thou release this man, thou art no friend of Caesar (Joan. XIX). » Therefore that which Isaiah says in what subseqnentibus: « Now, men of Judah and inhabitants of Jerusalem, judge between me and my vineyard (Isa. V), » no one is ignorant belongs to the general resurrection, in which the holy apostles shall judge and condemn that vineyard. This, in fine, the Lord in the Gospel testifies to the Jews themselves as they disparaged him, when he says: « If I, as you say, cast out devils by the prince of devils, by whom do your sons cast them out? (Matth. XII.) » [Vulg. Matth. XII, 27 si ego in Beelzebub ejicio daemones] Therefore they themselves shall be your judges. Which this same prophet Isaiah also declares, 532Bwhen he prophesied of the coming of the Lord and of the holy apostles to the day of judgment, saying: « The Lord will come to judgment with the elders of his people (Isa. III). » [Vulg. Isa. III, 14 Dominus ad judicium veniet cum senibus populi sui et principibus ejus] Solomon too took care to comprise in a brief speech Christ, and holy Church, and the holy apostles, and the day of judgment, when he says: « Noble in the gates is the man of holy Church, when he shall sit with the senators of his people (Prov. XXXI). » [Vulg. Prov. XXXI, 23 nobilis in portis vir ejus, quando sederit cum senatoribus terrae, "her husband… with the senators of the land"] And so the apostles, who in the Gospel are called twelve judges and sons of the Jews according to the flesh, are themselves doubtless called in Isaiah the men of Judah and the inhabitants of Jerusalem and the elders of the people, and are named by Solomon the senators of the people. These indeed are the two sons of the oil of splendour whom Zechariah the prophet (Cap. IV) says in the spirit 532Cof prophecy to stand before the Ruler of the whole earth. Which he described by the number two for this reason, that he foresaw in the prophetic spirit that they would preach the divinity and the humanity of Christ. Further, that which the same prophet says: « These are they who stand before the Ruler of the whole earth, » let us understand to have been said not only of the apostles, but also of all the saints. Whence the Domiuus himself in his Gospel says: « Father, I will that where I am, there also may my minister be (Joan. XII). » [Vulg. Joan. XII, 26 ubi sum ego, illic et minister meus erit, "where I am, there also shall my minister be"] Which blessed Augustine also, the excellent doctor and most faithful defender of the catholic faith, testifies when he says: « Let us know for certain that after the ascension of the Lord to the heavens all the souls of the saints are with Christ, and going out from the body they go to Christ, as it is written: The souls of the just 532Dare in the hand of God (Sap. III), awaiting the resurrection of their body, that they may enjoy together with Christ an entire and perpetual beatitude. In like manner also the souls of sinners are set in hell, awaiting under fear the resurrection of their body, that with the devil they may be turned to eternal punishment.

RECAPITULATIO A SUPERIORIBUS SECUNDUM MORALITATEM.

Quamvis grave sit admodum, atque periculosum, ut homo imperitus et male morigeratus disputet de bonis moribus hominum, tamen nequaquam sopitis sensibus negligere debemus, quod semper et sine intervallo benedicere et laudare Deum jubemur, sicut 533APsalmista ait: « Benedicam Dominum in omni tempore, semper laus ejus in ore meo (Psal. XXXIII). » Enim vero si tandiu volumus exspectare loqui de bono, donec simus boni; vel si tandiu de perfectione tacemus donec perfecti simus. Et si rursum usque adeo silemus a laudibus Dei, quoadusque laudare Deum digni fuerimus, patet profecto quia nusquam Deum laudabimus, quoniam sicut necesse est, nullatenus in hac vita unquam boni et perfecti atque laudibus Dei erimus digni. Non ergo deficiat nobis bona voluntas, licet desit efficax potestas, sicque tandem poterimus 311 Deo ampliante adipisci, ut simus boni et perfecti, laudibusque Dei idonei. Sed jam nunc discutiamus sententiam istam sub morali interpretatione scilicet: « Oleum effusum nomen tuum, ideo adolescentulae 533Bdilexerunt te (Cant. I). » Beatus ille homo licet mortalis sit, cujus nomen ob meritum bonae vitae tantam praerogativam jam obtinuit, ut illi veraciter dici possit: « Oleum effusum nomen tuum, » ideo adolescentulae dilexerunt te. Sane talis homo valde quaerendus est, dumque inventus fuerit, non mediocriter diligendus est. Qui enim in saeculo bene et religiose vivit, et de substantia sua pro Deo largas facit eleemosynas, et ea quae sibi non vult fieri, alteri non facit, et qui in omnibus operibus suis quaerit ut Deus laudetur, et qui talem se exhibet ut ab omnibus hominibus pro sanctitate sua veneretur, isti nimirum dici potest: « Oleum effusum nomen tuum, » ideo adolescentulae dilexerunt te, id est fideles animae. Simili modo qui ad conversionem vadit 533Cet in claustro bene, et sancte, et religiose conversatur, et qui bona fama irreprehensibilis vitae, alios animat et attrahit, ut ad Dominum convertantur, et insuper qui columbina pace et fraterna dilectione animis fratrum suorum amabilem se reddit, adeo, ut ab omnibus pura mentis intentione diligatur, huic sine dubio dici debet: « Oleum effusum nomen tuum, ideo adolescentulae, » id est fratrum animae, « dilexerunt te. » Praeterea hoc quod in prima recapitulatione superius posuimus, quia praecepit Dominus Moysi, ut diceret filiis Israel ut afferrent oleum de arboribus olivarum, si nos sumus veri Israelitae, in quibus dolus non est, et nobis similiter praecipit noster spiritualis Moyses (Exod. 533DXXVII), videlicet Dominus Jesus Christus, ut nos afferamus ei oleum de arboribus olivarum, id est ut nos sequamur et imitemur exempla, et actiones sanctorum Patrum, qui per opera misericordiae Deo dignoscuntur priscis temporibus placuisse; verumtamen quia obscuritas in divina pagina non est bona, imo valde minus capacibus est noxia, dignum arbitror de arboribus olivarum et de oleo aliquantulum disserere latius, quatenus clariori sensu respondeat quod dicere volumus. Arbores ergo olivarum sancti Patres nostri sunt, de quibus dicit beatus Paulus: Isti sunt viri misericordiae, quorum justitiae oblivionem ante Deum non acceperunt. Istae siquidem sunt arbores olivarum de quibus in Evangelio Dominus dicit: « Non potest arbor bona fructus malos 534Afacere (Luc. VI). » Oleum harum arborum est omne opus bonum, pium, modestum, castum, religiosum, et sanctum. Quod autem per oleum, opus bonum intelligi debeat, Evangelium secundum Matthaeum manifestissime declarat: « Simile est inquit, regnum coelorum decem virginibus, et caetera quae ibi sequuntur (Matth. XXV). » Cui sententiae Salomon concordare satis videtur, qui inter caetera verba sua sic loquitur: « Omni tempore vestimenta tua sint candida, et oleum de capite tuo non deficiat (Eccle. IX). » Vestimenta igitur nostra, corporis nostri sunt membra, cuibus nostra anima induitur, dum in hoc corpusculo secundum Dei dispositionem demoratur. Quae utique membra semper debent candida esse, quia non debent alicui peccato 534Bsubjacere. Sic enim dicit Isaias propheta: « Vestimentum, inquit, mistum sanguine erit in combustionem ignis (Isa. IX). » Denique et Apostolus dicit: « Non regnet peccatum in vestro mortali corpore, ut obediatis ejus concupiscentiis (Rom. VI). » Et iterum: « Sicut, inquit, exhibuistis membra vestra servire immunditiae ad iniquitatem, ita nunc exhibere membra vestra servire justitiae in sanctificationem (ibid.). » Oleum autem nunquam deficit de capite nostro, quando mens nostra semper pio opere abundat, et bono desiderio. Et amplius, si fieri posset, vult assidue facere, quam unquam valeat ad perfectum perducere, unde Psalmista dicit: « Concupivit anima mea desiderare justificationes tuas in omni tempore (Psal. CXVIII). » Oleum ergo 534Cde arboribus olivarum Deo offerimus, cum per opera pietatis et justitiae ipsi placere studemus, per quae quondam patres nostros placuisse non dubitamus. Praeterea hoc quod oleum illud quod Deo offerebatur purissimum exstitit, non sine causa hoc ita Moyses expressit, quod usque ad nostram notitiam pervenire praescivit. Potera namque idem propheta purum vel purius oleum dicere, sed maluit purissimum ponere, ut videlicet nobis tacite insinuaret, qualiter opus nostrum examinare deberemus, quo ipsi Deo, qui est summa puritas, placere intendimus. Oleum enim tunc est purum quando in exteriori exhibitione, peccato caret opus nostrum. Tunc vero purius efficitur, cum nec in locutione nostra 534Dpeccatum aliquod repetitur. Si autem mens nostra quando ad bonum opus peragendum se accingit, vanam gloriam intus devitare valet profecto oleum est purissimum quod Deo in sacrificio sui cordis offeret. Sane quod idem oleum refertur fuisse pilo tusum, minime sub negligentia arbitror esse praetereundum. Tunsio enim pili, significat tribulationes et pressuras hujus mundi. Quid ergo per oleum pilo diverberatum nobis insinuat, nisi quia illorum patrum actiones et exempla debemus imitari, quos per multas tribulationes juxta Apostolum (Hebr. XI) scimus introisse in regnum Dei? Quod autem praecipitur ut ardeat semper lucerna coram Deo in tabernaculo testimonii (Exod. XXVII), hoc significat nimirum, quia intentio 312 cordis nostri nunquam 535Adebet removeri a consideratione et contemplatione majestatis Dei, sicut illius cor a Deo nunquam recedebat, qui dicebat: « Meditatio cordis mei in conspectu tuo semper (Psal. XVIII). » Lucerna quippe corporis tui, ut ait Dominus in Evangelio (Marc. VI), est oculus tuus. Quem utique oculum sancti Patres nostri interpretati sunt animi intentionem, vel sincerum mentis affectum. Tabernaculum vero testimonii, corpus nostrum non immerito potest intelligi, unde beatus Petrus dicit: « Scio, fratres, quod velox sit depositio tabernaculi mei, secundum quod Dominus Jesus Christus dignatus est revelare mihi (II Petr. I). » In hoc ergo tabernaculo, id est in corpore nostro, debet semper ardere lucerna coram Domino, quia simplex et pura 535Bmentis intentio assidue debet dominari corpori nostro, et nunquam debemus pati aliquid dominari nostrae menti, quod sciamus displicere in conspectu judicis nostri. Vel lucerna relucet assidue in tabernaculo nostro, cum fervor charitatis nunquam tepescit in corde nostro. Sic quippe ista lucerna charitatis in corde Pauli renitebat fulgore mirae claritatis, ita ut idem Paulus diceret: « Charitas Dei diffusa est in cordibus nostris per inhabitantem Spiritum, qui datus est nobis (Rom. V). » Vel lucerna sine defectu claritatis fulget in tabernaculo testimonii, cum cor nostrum per infusionem Spiritus sancti assidue cogitat, quae sunt Dei. Sicut ergo capitulum in libro Exodi secundum moralitatem 535Cdiscussimus: sic decens est, ut illam sententiam de Isaia assumptam moraliter disseramus. « Vinea, inquit, facta est dilecto meo in cornu filio olei. » O quam beata est illa anima, quae veraciter et pie operando efficitur Dei vinea! Sed jam nunc consideremus quid sit vinea, et unde constet vinea, et ejus terrenam operationem transferamus in moralem et spiritualem aedificationem. Vinea siquidem ex vitibus constare videtur, et vitis dicitur, eo quod vi se teneat in loco ubi plantatur. Quicunque ergo vitis seu vinea esse desiderat, necesse est, ut propositum sanctitatis et religionis quod pro Deo arripuit, nullatenus dimittat, prosperitatibus non dissolvatur, adversitatibus non frangatur, sciens quia regnum coelorum vim patitur et violenti rapiunt illud 535D(Matth. XI). Sed et hoc quod vitis contra consuetudinem aliarum arborum, humorem, quem de terra trahit per medullam ad fastigia suorum ramorum transmittit, nequaquam sine admiratione ingentis sacramenti considerandum est. Caeterae enim arbores humorem inter corticem et robur sui ligni a terra suscipiunt, et eumdem humorem ramusculis ex se procedentibus, ut fructificare queant, infundunt. Quid ergo significat hoc quod vitis in medulla, id est, in interiori parte sui humorem concipit, quem postea per ramos exteriores ubertim diffundit? Nimirum uniuscujusque fidelis viri perfectam animam, cujus corpus etsi coram hominibus apparet publico, tamen in praesentia Dei manet in occulto, quia magis placere Deo studet interius, quam hominibus 536Avel saeculo exterius, unde ait Psalmista: « Omnis gloria ejus filiae regis ab intus (Psal. XLIV). » Hinc etiam Paulus dicit: « Gloria nostra haec est testimonium conscientiae nostrae (II Cor. I). » Potest adhuc hujusmodi sacramentum in vite considerari, quae contra morem aliarum arborum, succum trajicit in medulla interiori, quo paulatim postmodum rigat ramos exteriores naturali ortu ex se prodeuntes. Sancti namque viri, charismata Spiritus sancti, quae occulta Dei largitione in arcano suscipiunt cordis sui, religiosis hominibus per boni operis exhibitionem et coelestis verbi praedicationem infundunt, et more bonae vitis quod in eis invisibiliter agitur, exterius visibiliter demonstratur. Est praeterea aliud, quod de vite rationabili auctoritate valet 536Bdici, quodque a prudenti solertia illustrium clericorum in divinis eloquiis reperiri potest. Est enim haec arbor, scilicet vitis humilis in sua statura, et quandiu vivit foliis adornata, floribus odorifera, gemmatis fructibus omnium oculis grata. Si vero abscissa fuerit quanto prius exstitit charior, pretiosior, acceptior atque amabilior, tanto postea exstat vilior, degenerior atque inutilior. Quod utique Dominus per prophetam Ezechielem manifestat, cum dicit: « Fili hominis, quid fiet de ligno vitis, quod pretiosum est prae caeteris lignis silvarum? (Ezech. XV.) » Nunquid si abscisum fuerit fiet de eo quidquam operis? Nunquid fabricatur de eo paxillus ut dependeat in eo quodcunque vas? Ad nihil vero utile est, nisi 536Cut in ignem mittatur. Et quid ista significarent manifestius aperitur, cum de Filiis Israel in subsequentibus sermo habitus invenitur. Ipsi vero filii Israel quandiu mandatis Dei obsecuti sunt, et a cultura ejus non recesserunt, quasi bona vitis flore et fructu in decore suo perstiterunt. Postquam autem ejus praeceptis contradicere coeperunt, et insuper ei qui auctor est vitae, crucis supplicium intulerunt, quasi vitis inutilis et amputata igni aeterno deputati sunt. Similiter quoque et Christiani qui sacramentis ecclesiasticis imbuti sunt, qui fide et opere, ab Ecclesiae doctoribus instructi sunt, quandiu infra gremium ejusdem Ecclesiae fidem suam operibus bonis exercent, tandiu velut vitis fructifera in vinea Dei virore fidei vigent. Si vero, 536Dquod absit! ad aliquam haeresim labantur, relicta gratia cui jampridem traditi erant, pravis dogmatibus spontanei inficiantur, nonne isti merito 313 suae iniquitatis se tradunt ultricibus flammis velut vitis amputata et inutilis? Quibus procul dubio elegantissime illud congruit, quod Moyses propheta in suo cantico dicit: « De vinea Sodomorum vinea eorum, usque ad illum locum, ubi ex persona Dei ait: Mea est ultio, et ego retribuam eis, subauditur, in die judicii quod merentur (Deut. XXXII). » Sed quid dicam de conversis et religiosis viris, qui sicut Dominus in Evangelio declarat (Luc. VIII), ad tempus credunt, et in tempore tentationis recedunt? Nonnullos enim magnae religionis et satis ardui propositi novi viros, 537Aquos utinam non novissem, qui, quandiu licet eis in claustris suam propriam voluntatem exercere, vel per claustri officinas, seu et per universum mundum, et per parentes vagari et discurrere, tandiu sub specie qualiscunque religionis infra monasterii septa, nomine tenus retinentur, quamvis, ut superius dixi, universum per orbem perdita mente, et animo volatili evagantur. Quibus nimirum bene convenit illud quod Dominus per prophetam dicit: « Factus est Ephraim quasi columba seducta non habens cor; alieni comederunt robur ejus, et ipse nescivit (Osee. VII). » Alieni namque sunt daemones: et vitia repugnantia, cogitationesque malae, et concupiscentiae carnales, quae nos a Deo alienant, si in nobis per nostram ignaviam principatum obtineant. 537BDe his siquidem alienis David conqueritur, dicens: « Quoniam quidem alieni insurrexerunt adversum me, et fortes quaesierunt animam meam (Psal. LIII). » Salomon quoque ait: « Ne des alienis honorem tuum (Prov. V). » Et quasi nos quaereremus a Salomone qui essent isti alieni, illico subjunxit: « et annos tuos crudeli (ibid.), » scilicet, diabolo, de quo Dominus testatur: « Quia ipse homicida erat ab initio (Joan. VIII). » Qui enim supradictis alienis, id est daemonibus et vitiis concupiscentiisque male deditus fuerit, dum vivit in stadio hujus praesentis vitae, ipse post hanc vitam procul dubio tradetur in potestatem Satanae, id est hujus crudelis. Iste namque est ille crudelis qui in Isaia (cap. XIX) scribitur homines persequi crudeliter.

537CHi igitur religiosi, si tamen dicendi sunt religiosi qui ita se habent sicut modo depinximus, vel etiam, quod pudet dicere, adhuc pejus, si aliquando a magistris suis pro salute sua corriguntur, aut si ut in claustro suo sedeant, et ordinem suum teneant, constringuntur, protinus abjecto timore Dei et relicto proposito religionis et ordinis sui, sub obtentu arctioris religionis de claustro suo inordinate prosilientes, diversas partes orbis terrarum girovagi peragrantur, vel, proh dolor! quod infamius est, ad saeculum revertuntur. Quibus nimirum decenter illud convenit, quod beatus Petrus apostolus in sua epistola ait: « Canis reversus ad vomitum suum, et sus lota in volutabro luti (II Petr. II). » Et: 537D« Melius illis esset viam veritatis non cognovisse, quam post cognitionem ejus, retrorsum abire (ibid.). » Isti ergo conversi quandiu in sancto proposito manserunt, tandiu quasi vitis Sorech, id est electa, in vinea Dei, in charitate radicati et fundati, exstiterunt. Nunc autem retrorsum sunt alienati, in sensum reprobum conversi, et juxta prophetam Ezechiel, inutiles et degeneres penitus effecti ad nihil aliud ulterius valent, nisi ut in ignem projiciantur et ardeant. Longius ab ordine itaque nostrae narrationis digressi sumus, dum de natura vitis et vineae disputavimus, verumtamen jam idoneum est tempus ut revertamur ad id quod coeperamus. « Vinea, inquit, Isaias (cap. V), facta est charissima dilecto meo in cornu filio olei. » 538ADilectus itaque est, ad quem sponsa in Canticis canticorum legatos suos mittit, dicens: « Nuntiate dilecto meo, quia amore ejus langueo (Cant. V). » O quam felix, et quam beata est anima quae languet amore Dei! In ea sine dubio funditus jam interiit amor deceptoris hujus mundi. et ad nihilum redacta est fallax gloria fluentis saeculi. Huic siquidem dilecto sponso suo adhuc loquitur eadem sponsa sua charissima in delicis in eodem libello. « Veni, Dilecte mi, egrediamur in agrum, videamus si floruit vinea, si flores fructus claritatis et bonae operationis parturiunt (Cant. VII). » Sed certe, inquit, facta est vinea ista charissima, id est anima nostra huic dilecto filio olei scilicet omnipotentis Dei, in cornu videlicet. Omnibus liquet vel qui saltem raro sensu 538Bet intelligentia illustrantur, quia omne cornu carnem excedit de qua egreditur; et ideo non immerito per cornu omnis virtus spiritualis accipitur. Unde et Moyses propheta, ob excellentiam meritorum, et singularem praerogativam spiritualium virtutum quibus totus effloruit, solus inter mortales ex consortio collocutionis Dei cornutus apparuit; sicut scriptum est: Cum descendisset Moyses de monte Sinai, ignorabat quod cornuta esset (Exod. XXXIV), id est gloriosa et admirabilis facies ejus, ex familiaritate colloquii Dei. Sed et Dominus quidem Jesus, qui ante Moysen imo antequam mundus fieret, Deus exstitit, post Moysen vero novissimis temporibus homo mortalis huic mundo per Incarnationis mysterium apparuit, ab Habacuch propheta (Habac. 538CIII) cornua in manibus habere perhibetur, in quibus nimirum maxima ejus fortitudo fuisse dignoscitur. Sic quippe in Cantico ejusdem prophetae scriptum est: « Cornua in manibus ejus (ibid.), » scilicet Christi, et in sequentibus: « Ibi, inquit, abscondita est fortitudo ejus, ibi corruet mors in conspectu ejus (ibid.). » Praeterea si nos vellemus modo ad medium ducere, quidquid de istis spiritualibus cornibus divina eloquia solent eloqui, facilius 314 nos dies desereret quam copia dicendi. Sed neque ex toto silebimus. « Exaltabuntur, inquit Psalmista, cornua justi (Psal. LXXIV). » Quis est tam hebes et tam stolidus, qui dicere audeat quod justus cornua habeat? Sed virtutes justi cornua ejus sunt, quibus 538Dipse pugnat contra diabolum, et quibus sublimatur et exaltatur ante Deum. Dicat ergo Isaias quod vinea facta est charissima dilecto filio olei, id est anima nostra propter virtutes quas habet, diligitur et in magno honore habetur ab Unigenito Filio Dei, et hoc est quod per prophetam dicitur: « Si pretiosum a vili separaveris, quasi os meum eris (Jer. XV). » Pretiosum a vili quippe separare, est virtutes a vitiis discernere. Quis est autem filius olei? quia persaepe jam dictum est toties idem repetere valde taediosum est. Quod vero dicit Propheta, quia Dominus vineam sepivit, de nostra utique anima, quae est spiritualis vinea Dei, istam sepitionem intelligere nos convenit. O quam gloriosa et quam virtutibus praeclara est anima illa, de qua tanta 539Aest cura Deo, ut pia diligentia sepiatur ab ipso! Sic enim ipse Dominus dicit per prophetam: « Ego sepiam vias tuas, et ibis post amatores tuos, et non apprehendes eos, et tunc dices: Revertar ad virum meum pristinum, quia melius mihi erat quam modo sit (Osee II). » Praeterea huic rei paulisper immorantes considerare libet, qualiter hi qui hortos concludunt, sepes faciant. Hortus enim conclusus moraliter appellatur a Deo beatissima anima. Notum siquidem est omnibus, quia sepes ex palis et virgis fiunt, ita duntaxat ut pali inferius defixi terrae inhaereant, ex una parte, ex alia vero parte erectis capitibus sursum in coelo respiciant. Postea autem virgae ex transverso interseruntur, talique modo sepis muniens hortum Dei perficitur. Verumtamen 539Bquod rectius possumus appellare palos hujus spiritualis sepis nisi sanctos Dei, qui quamvis adhuc morentur in terra pro infirmitate carnis, assidue tamen cum Christo sunt in coelo, et ei haerent spiritalibus desideriis? unde unus ex hujusmodi spiritalibus palis in terra degens aiebat: « Nostra autem conversatio est in coelis (Philipp. III). » Et quid virgae, nisi exempla et opera bona, et catholica dogmata eorum sunt? Istis ergo palis et hujusmodi virgis sepit Deus vineam suam, quia exemplis et doctrinis sanctorum suorum quotidie excolit, docet, instruit, et monet animas fidelium suorum. Hinc est quod David dixit: « Immittet Angelum Dominus in circuitu timentium se et custodiet eos (Psal. XXXIII). » Alius quoque propheta nihilominus ex 539Cpersona Dei testatur, dicens: « Qui tangit vos, tangit pupillam oculi mei (Zach. II), » liquido constat quod inter omnia membra quae homo habet, pupilla oculi tenerior et delicatior apparet. Si ergo tantae auctoritatis sumus, ut pupillae oculi Dei aequiparemur, non debemus ejus esse beneficii ingrati, sed necesse est sic nos vivere, quatenus ab ipso mereamur sicut bona vinea custodiri et sepiri. Illud quoque quod Isaias propheta tantopere exprimit, ut asserat quod Deus ex vinea sua lapides elegerit. De nostra anima dictum non dubitemus, si tamen ut superius jam probavimus, vinea Dei spiritualiter vivendo fuerimus. Lapides enim qui in vinea Dei sunt, vitia et concupiscentiae etiam immunda desideria intelligi possunt, quae sicut 539Dlapides vineam, ita haec omnia faciunt sterilem animam nostram. Sed nunc orandus est Deus, ut ipse dignetur lapides ex ista vinea eligere et projicere, id est ab anima nostra omnia vitia quae ei displicent exstirpare; quatenus anima nostra veluti bonus palmes in ipso qui est vera vitis manens cum apostolis audiat: « Sine me nihil potestis facere, et sicut palmes non valet fructificare, nisi manserit in vite, sic nec vos nisi in me manseritis (Joan. XV). » Quod autem in sequentibus suae narrationis dicit Isaias, quia « plantavit Deus vineam suam electam, » ad nostrum corpus haec sciamus pertinere moraliter et ad nostram animam. « Ad imaginem quippe Dei factus est homo, et ad similitudinem 540Aipsius (Gen. I). » Satis nimirum ista plantatio electa exstitit, quae ad similitudinem et imaginem Dei creata fuit. Praeterea in libro Sapientiae scriptum reperitur: « Deus fecit hominem simplicem et rectum, sed ipse immiscuit se infinitis quaestionibus (Eccle. VII). Post haec, inquit Isaias, aedificavit Deus turrim in medio vineae suae. » Si igitur anima nostra vinea Dei desiderat esse, oportet illam sine dubio turrim a Deo aedificatam in medio sui habere. Turris namque quando construitur in quadrangulari forma ab imo sursum erigitur. Aedifica ergo, Domine Deus, in vinea mea turrim quadrangularem, id est funda et radicari fac in anima nostra quatuor istas virtutes, scilicet prudentiam, fortitudinem, justitiam, temperantiam, 540Bet in nostro corpore nihilominus abstinentiam, sobrietatem, castitatem, veram poenitentiam. Reliquae autem virtutes sint hujus spiritualis aedificii lapides. Coementum vero quod lapides ligat et complectitur, ipsa sit charitas sine qua nullum opus bonum esse dicitur, et in qua omne opus bonum solidatur et fundatur; sic enim dicit Paulus: « In charitate radicati et fundati estote (Ephes. III). » « Torcular, inquit Isaias, exstruxit Dominus in vinea sua. » Notum est omnibus opus quod in torculari agitur. In torculari quippe uvae exprimuntur et expressae liquefiunt, ut in vinum dulcissimum convertantur. Quamvis ergo Dominus de passione sua dicat per prophetam: « Torcular calcavi solus, et de gentibus non est vir mecum (Isa. 540CLXIII), » tamen adhuc est aliud torcular spirituale, 315 quod utinam Dominus Jesus dignetur in vinea nostri corporis exstruere. Sancta namque compunctio, et beatarum lacrymarum pro Christi amore effusio, non incongrue torcular animae appellari debet. Hoc torcular spirituale, exstructum in se habebat, illa charissima sponsa in deliciis quae in Canticis canticorum suspirans de Sponso suo dicebat: « Anima mea liquefacta est, ut Dilectus meus mihi locutus est (Cant. V). » Ostenditur ergo in hac sententia inaestimabilis atque incomparabilis affectus Sponsae erga sponsum suum, quae in tantum sincero amore diligebat illum, ut etiam tota liquefieret ad illius verbum. Verumtamen multo ampliori affectu, et 540Daltiori charitate eamdem sponsam suam idem sponsus diligit, qui pro amore illius non solum paulominus ab angelis minoratus de caelo descendit, sed etiam usque ad ignominiosae mortis supplicium pervenit. Quod equidem Jeremias propheta in suis lamentationibus protestatur, dicens: « Torcular calcavit Dominus virgini filiae Judae (Thren. I). » Ipsa nempe beatissima anima, quae in Canticis canticorum dicitur Sponsa, in Jeremia appellatur virgo filia Juda. Virgo autem nuncupatur nupta Deo anima propter fidei integritatem, et corporis quo induitur castitatem. Filia vero Juda ob humilem peccatorum confessionem et gloriosam in laudibus Dei perseverantiam. Juda quippe confessio interpretatur, quod ad delictorum confessionem et ad laudem Dei 541Arefertur. Licet adhuc multa supersint quae de torculari dici valeant, tamen ista sufficere posse arbitror modernis auditoribus qui libenter audientes brevitati student, et ne volumen longius protensum mentes aggravet taediosorum audientium. Sane nunquid adeo vereri debemus ne offendamus pigros et taediosos, ut praetermittamus agere id quod potest adjuvare et instruere, et aedificare strenuos viros, et meditatione legis Dei exercitatos? nullo modo. Videamus ergo quid est quod sequitur in Isaia propheta. Et exspectavit, inquit Isaias, vineam suam Dominus ut faceret uvas, et ecce, proh dolor! fecit labruscas. Quae nimirum sententia potius est timenda quam exponenda. « Ecce enim, ait Jacobus apostolus, agricola exspectat pretiosum fructum 541Bterrae patienter ferens, donec accipiat temporaneum et serotinum. Patientes et vos estote, et confirmate corda vestra in servitio Dei quoadusque ipse Dominus veniat (Jacob. V). » Praeterea ne prorsus indiscussum relinquamus quod superius dictum est, quia exspectavit Dominus vineam suam ut faceret uvas, et fecit labruscas, dicendum paucis verbis quoniam quidem vae illis qui a Creatore suo in tota vita sua exspectantur ut fructificent, tandemque finito stadio vitae praesentis infructuosi apparent. Generaliter quippe omnes Christiani qui fidem susceperunt, et operibus fidei imbuti sunt, si male vivunt, si extremum diem sine fidei operatione claudunt, procul dubio pro uvis, labruscas afferunt. Sed 541Cet tu quicunque conversus diceris, licet infra ambitum monasterii vel claustri esse ad tempus videaris, si antequam extremus dies tibi adveniat a 542Asancta conversione, et a servitio Dei recedis, sine dubio (si dici potest) plus quam proh dolor! labruscas Domino Deo tuo facis pro uvis. Verumtamen illi beati et felices sunt, qui versa vice pro labruscis Deo se exspectanti ad salutem pretiosas uvas afferunt. Isti sunt nimirum qui aliquando huic fallaci saeculo nimio amore inhaeserunt, sed postea se sentientes errasse, toto cordis affectu ad religionem sanctam conversi sunt, et spreta saeculi vanitate totis viribus servitio Dei se mancipaverunt. De quibus Zacharias propheta mihi videtur dicere: « Isti sunt filii olei splendoris qui assistunt Dominatori universae terrae (Zach. IV). Quia vero persaepe jam superius praefati sumus quod oleum significet misericordiam, et misericordia designet illum, de quo 542Bdicit Psalmista: Deus meus misericordia mea (Psal. LVIII), » hoc iterum replicare devitamus, ne fastidium auditoribus pariamus. Sed hoc constat pro certo quoniam quidem si Deus oleum dicitur, hi qui sunt filii olei, aeque possunt dici et filii Dei. Cui siquidem sententiae consona voce concordat beatus Joannes apostolus, dicens: « Fratres, filii Dei sumus, et nondum apparet quid erimus. Scimus quoniam cum apparuerit similes ei erimus, quia videbimus eum sicuti est (Joan. III). » Consideremus ergo quis sit ille qui loquitur. Conversus nempe erat, apostolus et Evangelista erat, mundo abrenuntiaverat, ideoque; audacter aiebat: « Filii Dei sumus. » Manifestum est igitur, quia qui perfectius 542Crenuntiat saeculo, et familiarius more apostolorum adhaeret Deo, filius Dei est, et dici veraciter potest.

541Explicit tertius Tomus.

RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING ACCORDING TO THE MORAL SENSE.

Although it is exceedingly grave and perilous that an unskilled and ill-mannered man should dispute about the good morals of men, yet we ought in no wise, with drowsy senses, to neglect that we are bidden always and without interval to bless and praise God, as 533Athe Psalmist says: « I will bless the Lord at all times, his praise shall be ever in my mouth (Psal. XXXIII). » For indeed if we wish to wait so long to speak of the good, until we are good; or if we keep silence about perfection so long until we are perfect. And if again we are silent from the praises of God up to the point at which we shall be worthy to praise God, it is plain assuredly that we shall nowhere praise God, since, as must be, we shall in no wise ever in this life be good and perfect and worthy of the praises of God. Let not, therefore, good will fail us, although effectual power be lacking; and so at length we shall be able, God enlarging us, to attain that we may be good and perfect and 311 fit for the praises of God. But now let us examine this saying under the moral interpretation, namely: « Thy name is oil poured out; therefore the maidens 533Bhave loved thee (Cant. I). » Blessed is that man, though he be mortal, whose name for the desert of a good life has already obtained so great a prerogative that it can truly be said to him: « Thy name is oil poured out, » therefore the maidens have loved thee. Assuredly such a man is greatly to be sought, and when he has been found, he is to be loved in no middling degree. For he who lives well and religiously in the world, and out of his substance gives large alms for God, and does not do to another what he does not wish to be done to himself, and who in all his works seeks that God be praised, and who so shows himself that he is venerated by all men for his holiness — to him doubtless it can be said: « Thy name is oil poured out, » therefore the maidens, that is, the faithful souls, have loved thee. In like manner he who goes to conversion 533Cand in the cloister lives well and holily and religiously, and who by the good report of a blameless life animates and draws others, that they may be converted to the Lord, and moreover who by dove-like peace and brotherly love makes himself lovable to the minds of his brethren, so far that he is loved by all with a pure intention of mind — to him without doubt it ought to be said: « Thy name is oil poured out; therefore the maidens, » that is, the souls of the brethren, « have loved thee. » Moreover, as to what we set down above in the first recapitulation, that the Lord charged Moses to tell the children of Israel to bring oil from the olive trees: if we are true Israelites, in whom there is no guile, our spiritual Moses (Exod. 533DXXVII) likewise charges us — namely the Lord Jesus Christ — that we bring him oil from the olive trees, that is, that we follow and imitate the examples and actions of the holy Fathers, who by the works of mercy are known to have pleased God in former times; but nevertheless, since obscurity in the divine page is not good, nay is exceedingly harmful to the less capable, I judge it worthy to discourse somewhat more widely about the olive trees and about the oil, so far as what we wish to say may answer to a clearer sense. The olive trees, therefore, are our holy Fathers, of whom blessed Paul says: "These are men of mercy, whose righteousnesses have not received forgetfulness before God." These indeed are the olive trees of which the Lord says in the Gospel: « A good tree cannot bring forth evil 534Afruits (Luc. VI). » [Vulg. Luc. VI, 43 non est arbor bona quae facit fructus malos; the wording here is that of Matth. VII, 18] The oil of these trees is every work good, loving, modest, chaste, religious and holy. But that by the oil a good work ought to be understood, the Gospel according to Matthew most plainly declares: « The kingdom of heaven is like, he says, to ten virgins, and the rest which follows there (Matth. XXV). » [Vulg. Matth. XXV, 1 simile erit regnum caelorum, future "shall be like"] With which saying Solomon seems well enough to agree, who among his other words speaks thus: « At all times let thy garments be white, and let oil not be lacking from thy head (Eccle. IX). » Our garments, therefore, are the members of our body, with which cuibus our soul is clothed, while it tarries in this little body according to the disposition of God. Which members ought always to be white, since they ought not to lie under any sin. 534BFor thus says Isaiah the prophet: « A garment, he says, mingled with blood shall be for the burning of fire (Isa. IX). » [Vulg. Isa. IX, 5 erit in combustionem et cibus ignis, "shall be for burning, and fuel for the fire"] And moreover the Apostle says: « Let not sin reign in your mortal body, so that you obey its concupiscences (Rom. VI). » And again: « As, he says, you presented your members to serve uncleanness unto iniquity, so now to present your members to serve justice unto sanctification (ibid.). » [Vulg. Rom. VI, 19 ita nunc exhibete membra vestra, imperative "so now present ye your members"] But oil is never lacking from our head when our mind always abounds in loving work and in good desire. And it wishes to do continually more, if it could be done, than it can ever bring to perfection; whence the Psalmist says: « My soul hath coveted to long for thy justifications at all times (Psal. CXVIII). » Oil, therefore, 534Cfrom the olive trees we offer to God when through works of piety and of justice we strive to please him, by which we do not doubt that our fathers of old pleased him. Moreover, that the oil which was offered to God was of the purest, Moses did not without cause so express, since he foreknew that it would come to our knowledge. For that same prophet could have said Potera pure or purer oil, but he chose rather to set down "purest," that he might silently intimate to us in what manner we ought to examine our work, whereby we intend to please God himself, who is the highest purity. For oil is pure then, when in its outward showing our work lacks sin. But then it is made purer, when in our speech also 534Dno sin is repeated. And if our mind, when it girds itself to perform a good work, is able inwardly to shun vain glory, then assuredly it is the purest oil which it will offer to God in the sacrifice of its own heart. And that the same oil is said to have been pounded with a pestle, I judge in no way to be passed over out of negligence. For the pounding of the pestle signifies the tribulations and pressures of this world. What then does the oil beaten with a pestle intimate to us, but that we ought to imitate the actions and examples of those fathers whom we know, according to the Apostle (Hebr. XI), to have entered into the kingdom of God through many tribulations? But that it is commanded that a lamp burn always before God in the tabernacle of the testimony (Exod. XXVII), this doubtless signifies that the intention 312 of our heart ought never 535Ato be withdrawn from the consideration and contemplation of the majesty of God, as the heart of him never withdrew from God who said: « The meditation of my heart is ever in thy sight (Psal. XVIII). » For the lamp of thy body, as the Lord says in the Gospel (Marc. VI), is thine eye. Which eye our holy Fathers have interpreted as the intention of the mind, or the sincere affection of the mind. And by the tabernacle of the testimony our body can not undeservedly be understood, whence blessed Peter says: « I know, brethren, that the laying aside of my tabernacle is swift, according as the Lord Jesus Christ has vouchsafed to reveal to me (II Petr. I). » [Vulg. II Petr. I, 14 certus quod velox est depositio tabernaculi mei… significavit mihi, "being sure… hath signified to me"] In this tabernacle, therefore, that is, in our body, a lamp ought always to burn before the Lord, since a simple and pure 535Bintention of the mind ought unremittingly to have dominion over our body, and we ought never to suffer anything to have dominion over our mind which we know to be displeasing in the sight of our judge. Or, the lamp shines unremittingly in our tabernacle when the fervour of charity never grows lukewarm in our heart. For thus that lamp of charity shone again in the heart of Paul with the brightness of a wondrous clearness, so that the same Paul said: « The charity of God is poured forth in our hearts by the indwelling Spirit, who is given to us (Rom. V). » [Vulg. Rom. V, 5 per Spiritum Sanctum, qui datus est nobis, "by the Holy Spirit"] Or, the lamp shines without failing of brightness in the tabernacle of the testimony, when our heart, through the infusion of the Holy Spirit, unremittingly thinks upon the things which are of God. As therefore we have examined the chapter in the book of Exodus according to the moral sense, 535Cso it is fitting that we discourse morally upon that saying taken from Isaiah. « A vineyard, he says, was made for my beloved on a horn, the son of oil. » O how blessed is that soul which, by working truly and lovingly, is made the vineyard of God! But now let us consider what a vineyard is, and of what a vineyard consists, and let us transfer its earthly working into moral and spiritual building-up. A vineyard indeed is seen to consist of vines, and it is called vitis because by force it holds itself in the place where it is planted. Whosoever therefore desires to be a vine or a vineyard, it is needful that he in no wise let go the purpose of holiness and religion which he took up for God, that he be not dissolved by prosperity, nor broken by adversities, knowing that the kingdom of heaven suffers violence and the violent bear it away 535D(Matth. XI). But this too, that the vine, against the custom of other trees, transmits through its pith to the tops of its branches the moisture which it draws from the earth, is by no means to be considered without wonder at a mighty mystery. For other trees take up the moisture from the earth between the bark and the strength of their wood, and pour that same moisture into the little branches proceeding from themselves, that they may be able to bear fruit. What then does this signify, that the vine conceives its moisture in the pith, that is, in its inner part, which afterward it diffuses abundantly through the outer branches? Doubtless the perfect soul of each faithful man, whose body, though it appear publicly before men, yet in the presence of God remains in secret, since he strives to please God more inwardly than men 536Aor the world outwardly; whence the Psalmist says: « All the glory of the king's daughter is from within (Psal. XLIV). » Hence Paul also says: « This is our glory, the testimony of our conscience (II Cor. I). » A mystery of this kind can further be considered in the vine, which, against the manner of other trees, carries its sap in the inner pith, wherewith little by little it afterward waters the outer branches that spring from it by natural birth. For holy men pour into religious men, through the showing forth of good work and the preaching of the heavenly word, the charisms of the Holy Spirit which they receive by God's hidden bounty in the secret place of their heart; and after the manner of a good vine, what is wrought in them invisibly is shown forth visibly without. There is moreover another thing which can be said of the vine 536Bwith reasonable authority, and which can be found in the divine sayings by the prudent skill of illustrious clerics. For this tree, namely the vine, is lowly in its stature, and, so long as it lives, adorned with leaves, fragrant with flowers, pleasing to all eyes with its budded fruits. But if it has been cut off, by however much it was before more dear, more precious, more acceptable and more lovable, by so much afterward it stands more vile, more degenerate and more useless. Which indeed the Lord makes plain through the prophet Ezekiel, when he says: « Son of man, what shall be done with the wood of the vine, which is precious above all the other woods of the forests? (Ezech. XV.) » [Vulg. Ezech. XV, 2 quid fiet ligno vitis ex omnibus lignis nemorum quae sunt inter ligna silvarum, without pretiosum, "precious"] Shall anything of work be made of it, if it has been cut off? Shall a peg be fashioned of it, that any vessel may hang upon it? It is useful for nothing, save 536Cthat it be cast into the fire. And what these things signify is more plainly opened when a discourse is found concerning the children of Israel in what follows. But the children of Israel themselves, so long as they obeyed the commandments of God, and did not depart from his worship, stood fast in their comeliness like a good vine with flower and fruit. But after they began to contradict his precepts, and moreover inflicted the punishment of the cross upon him who is the author of life, they were assigned to eternal fire like a useless and lopped-off vine. In like manner also the Christians who have been imbued with the ecclesiastical sacraments, who have been instructed in faith and work by the doctors of the Church, so long as within the bosom of that same Church they exercise their faith by good works, so long do they flourish, like a fruit-bearing vine in the vineyard of God, with the greenness of faith. But if, 536Dwhich God forbid! they slip into some heresy, and, the grace to which they were long ago handed over being abandoned, of their own accord are tainted with depraved doctrines: do not these deservedly, for their 313 iniquity, hand themselves over to avenging flames like a lopped-off and useless vine? To whom beyond doubt that most elegantly agrees which Moses the prophet says in his canticle: « Their vineyard is of the vineyard of Sodom, as far as that place where he says in the person of God: Vengeance is mine, and I will repay them — it is understood, on the day of judgment, which they deserve (Deut. XXXII). » But what shall I say of converts and religious men who, as the Lord declares in the Gospel (Luc. VIII), believe for a time, and in the time of temptation fall away? For I have known not a few men of great religion and of a purpose lofty enough, 537Awhom would that I had not known, who, so long as it is allowed them in their cloisters to exercise their own will, or to wander and run about through the workshops of the cloister, and through the whole world, and among their kinsfolk, so long are they kept within the enclosures of the monastery under the appearance of religion of some sort, in name only, although, as I said above, with a lost mind and a flighty spirit they stray through the whole world. To whom indeed that well applies which the Lord says through the prophet: « Ephraim is become as a dove seduced, having no heart; strangers have devoured his strength, and he knew it not (Osee. VII). » For the strangers are the demons, and the vices that fight against us, and evil thoughts, and carnal concupiscences, which estrange us from God, if by our own sloth they obtain the chief place in us. 537BOf these strangers indeed David complains, saying: « Since indeed strangers have risen up against me, and the strong have sought after my soul (Psal. LIII). » Solomon too says: « Give not thy honour to strangers (Prov. V). » And as though we were asking of Solomon who these strangers were, he straightway added: « and thy years to the cruel one (ibid.), » namely, the devil, of whom the Lord testifies: « Because he was a murderer from the beginning (Joan. VIII). » For he who has been evilly given over to the aforesaid strangers, that is, to the demons and to the vices and concupiscences, while he lives in the race-course of this present life, he beyond doubt after this life shall be delivered into the power of Satan, that is, of this cruel one. For this is that cruel one who in Isaiah (cap. XIX) is written to persecute men cruelly.

537CThese religious, then — if indeed they are to be called religious who bear themselves as we have just now depicted, or even, which is a shame to say, still worse — if at any time they are corrected by their masters for their own salvation, or if they are constrained to sit in their own cloister and to keep their own order, straightway, the fear of God being cast off and the purpose of their religion and of their order forsaken, leaping out of their cloister unruly under the pretext of a stricter religion, they range as gyrovagues through the diverse parts of the round world, or — alas the grief! — what is more infamous, they return to the world. To whom assuredly that fittingly belongs which the blessed apostle Peter says in his epistle: « The dog returned to his own vomit, and the sow that was washed to her wallowing in the mire (II Petr. II). » And: 537D« It were better for them not to have known the way of truth, than after the knowledge of it to go backward (ibid.). » [Vulg. II Petr. 2:21 viam justitiae, "the way of justice"] These converts therefore, so long as they abode in the holy purpose, so long did they stand as it were a vine of Sorech, that is, a chosen vine, in the vineyard of God, rooted and founded in charity. But now they are estranged backward, turned into a reprobate sense, and, according to the prophet Ezechiel, made utterly useless and degenerate, they avail for nothing further, save that they be cast into the fire and burn. Farther, therefore, from the order of our narration have we digressed, while we disputed of the nature of the vine and of the vineyard; nevertheless it is now a fitting time that we return to that which we had begun. « A vineyard, says Isaias (cap. V), was made most dear to my beloved, on a horn, the son of oil. » [Vulg. Isa. 5:1 Vinea facta est dilecto meo in cornu filio olei, without charissima] 538AThe beloved, therefore, is he to whom the bride in the Song of Songs sends her legates, saying: « Tell my beloved that I languish for love of him (Cant. V). » O how happy, and how blessed is the soul that languishes with the love of God! In it, without doubt, the love of the deceiver of this world has now utterly perished. And the deceitful glory of the flowing age has been brought to nothing. To this her beloved bridegroom that same most dear bride still speaks amid delights in the same little book. « Come, my Beloved, let us go forth into the field, let us see if the vineyard hath flourished, if the flowers bring forth the fruits of brightness and of good working (Cant. VII). » But surely, he says, this vineyard was made most dear, that is, our soul, to this beloved son of oil, namely of almighty God, on a horn, to wit. It is plain to all, or at least to such as are illumined with a rare sense 538Band understanding, that every horn exceeds the flesh out of which it issues; and therefore not undeservedly is every spiritual virtue taken by the horn. Whence also Moses the prophet, for the excellence of his merits and the singular prerogative of the spiritual virtues wherein he wholly flourished, alone among mortals appeared horned from the fellowship of speaking with God; as it is written: When Moses had come down from Mount Sinai, he knew not that it was horned (Exod. XXXIV), that is, that his face was glorious and admirable, from the familiarity of converse with God. But the Lord Jesus also, who before Moses — nay, before the world was made — existed as God, yet after Moses in the last times appeared to this world as mortal man through the mystery of the Incarnation, is declared by the prophet Habacuc (Habac. 538CIII) to have horns in his hands, in which assuredly his greatest strength is discerned to have been.
