V). » Posuerunt ergo Judaei Christum custodem in vineis gentium, quando nolentes credere in eum, a cordibus suis expulerunt illum. Et tunc Christus vineam suam non custodivit, quando eos exigente perfidia sua dereliquit. Unde et ipse in Evangelio dicit: « Ecce relinquetur vobis domus vestra deserta (Luc. XIII). »

RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING, IN WHAT MANNER THIS SENTENCE, NAMELY, « I AM BLACK, BUT COMELY, » IS REFERRED TO THE PERSON OF CHRIST.

This sentence, namely I am black, but comely, can reasonably, by a good understanding, be referred to the flesh of Christ hanging upon the cross 329, and as it were saying: O you, daughters of Jerusalem, that is, O you, faithful souls, black indeed I am on account of the pain of the imminent passion and the most rigorous 565Bdeath, but comely and beautiful shall I be a little afterward, on account of the glorious resurrection and the wonderful ascension. Whence Paul says: « We see Jesus, on account of the suffering of death, crowned with glory and honor (Hebr. II). » As the tents of Cedar, that is, just as once his neighbors persecuted Cedar, so also the Jews, my friends and my kinsmen, stood against me, and, crying out: « Crucify him (Luc. XXIII; Joan. XIX), » lifted up their voice against me. Which also the prophet Jeremias had prophesied long before in the person of Christ, saying thus: « My inheritance is become to me as a lion in the forest, it hath given out its voice against me (Jer. XII). » Of the lion it is related that he goes round with roaring the forest 565Cin which he abides, and this he does for this reason, that no means of going out may lie open to the animals remaining within the forest, so that afterwards he may the more easily bring death upon whichever animal he will. Which can fittingly and suitably enough be applied to the Jewish people, who, like a lion going round the whole forest of this world, says: « You see that we prevail nothing, behold the whole world goes after him. If we let him alone thus, all will believe in him (Joan. XII). » [Vulg. Joan. 12:19 ecce mundus totus post eum abiit, "behold the whole world is gone after him"; the second sentence stands at Joan. 11:48] Which Jewish people also gave out its voice against Christ, when it cried to Pilate: « If thou release this man, thou art not Caesar's friend (Joan. XIX), » and why it said this it at once subjoined: « We have a law, and according to the law he ought to die. (ibid.). » By Cedar therefore, who is interpreted darkness, [we ought to understand] the darkness of death; but by the skin of Solomon we ought 565Dto understand the glory of the resurrection. Let the flesh of Christ hanging on the cross then say: I am black as the tents of Cedar, but I am comely as the skin of Solomon, that is, they are able to seem to bring upon me the darkness of death, but the glory of the resurrection they are not able to take away. Do not consider me that I am swarthy, because the sun hath discolored me, that is: Do not look upon me in that calamity and misery in which I now hang for you upon the cross, but look upon me as already made bright by the resurrection, and coming to judgment in my majesty. Because the sun hath discolored me, that is, because the sun of justice, who dwells within me, both willed me thus to be discolored through death, and after the resurrection will make me bright with incomparable glory. Whence the same Son says in the Gospel: « Glorify me, Father, with thyself, with the brightness which 566AI had before the world was made (Joan. XVII). » [Vulg. Joan. 17:5 claritate quam habui priusquam mundus esset apud te, "the glory which I had with thee before the world was"; Migne omits apud te] The sons of my mother have fought against me. The mother of Christ, according to the flesh, was the Synagogue, because it too drew its origin through the patriarchs and the kings, and through blessed Mary. But in what manner the Jews, who are the sons of his mother, that is, of the Synagogue, fought against him in the passion, the Psalmist testifies, saying: « The sons of men, their teeth are weapons and arrows, and their tongue a sharp sword (Psal. LVI). » They have set me keeper in the vineyards, my vineyard I have not kept. It is manifest that the faithless Jews persecuted Christ both living and dead, so far that with vain solicitude they even kept watch over his sepulchre; and the more they cast him out from themselves through their incrudelitatem, the more they sent him over to the Gentiles, who were to believe 566B. « Now the vineyard of the Lord of hosts, as Isaias says, is the house of Israel (Isa. V). » Therefore the Jews set Christ as keeper in the vineyards of the Gentiles, when, being unwilling to believe in him, they drove him out from their hearts. And then Christ did not keep his own vineyard, when he forsook them, their own faithlessness requiring it. Whence he himself also says in the Gospel: « Behold your house shall be left to you desolate (Luc. XIII). » [Vulg. Luc. 13:35 relinquitur, present, "is left"]

RECAPITULATIO A SUPERIORIBUS QUOMODO ISTA SENTENTIA, SCILICET, « NIGRA SUM, SED FORMOSA, » DEBEAT REFERRI ALLEGORICE AD NOSTRAM NATURAM.

Puto ergo quod nihil delinquimus, si istam sententiam: Nigra sum, sed formosa, ad nostram naturam 566Callegorice referimus. Spiritualis enim spiritualiter intelligit omnia. Dicat ergo nostra natura, non in eo statu existens in quo a summo Creatore est condita, sed in eo potius in quo remansit a diabolo seducta: Nigra sum propter inobedientiam et peccatum primi Adae patris mei, sed formosa futura sum propter obedientiam et justificationem secundi Adae, scilicet Christi redemptoris mei. Unde Paulus dicit: « Sicut per inobedientiam unius hominis peccatores constituti sunt multi, sic per obedientiam unius justi constituentur multi (Rom. V). » Sicut tabernacula Cedar, hoc est sicut Cedar quondam factus est instabilis propter persecutionem quam patiebatur a vicinis suis, sic ego amodo instabilis ero et nunquam in eodem statu permanebo propter 566Dsuperbam spem inanis gloriae quam mihi diabolus falso spopondit, quando ad credendum sibi mihi persuasit, dicens: « Si de fructu vetito comederitis, aperientur oculi vestri, et eritis sicut dii (Gen. III). » Sicut pellis Salomonis. Ecclesia catholica pro certo tenet et credit, quia beata Maria, quae semper virgo inviolata permansit, de nostra corrupta natura et peccatrice massa originem traxit. Quae beata Virgo non solum pellis, sed etiam tabernaculum Salomonis, id est veri pacifici Christi facta est, quando idem Christus pro 330 salute generis humani de ea nasci dignatus est. Dicat ergo natura nostra; Per primum Adam peccatorem nigra facta sum, sed per secundum Adam meum redemptorem formosa 567Afutura sum. Nigra sum, quia diabolo magis quam Deo credidi, sed formosa futura sum, quia de me carnem accipiet Filius Dei, et me reducet ad contemplandam divinae majestatis gloriam, ad quam de eo statu in quo creata eram, sine mortis dolore transiissem, nisi a diabolo spe seducta inani miserabiliter cecidissem. Nolite me considerare quod fusca sum, id est nolite me contemplari talem, qualis suadente antiquo hoste effecta sum, sed potius talem contemplamini, qualis a summo Creatore creata sum. Quia decoloravit me sol. Si per solem tentationem accipimus, verum et probatissimum sensum habemus. Dicit enim Dominus in Evangelio de seminibus supra petram seminatis, id est de infidelibus Christianis et perversis haereticis, quia: 567B« Sole orto aruerunt (Luc. VIII), » hoc est orta tentatione in fide et operatione defecerunt. Denique in libro Psalmorum scriptum reperitur: « Per diem sol non uret te (Psal. CXX), » id est in prosperitate tentatio nulla decipiet te. Dicat ergo nostra natura: quia sol me decoloravit, hoc est tentatio illata a diabolo colorem immortalitatis et innocentiae mihi abstulit, et colore mortis, et nigredine peccati me decoloravit. Filii matris meae pugnaverunt contra me. Mater nostrae et angelicae naturae clementissima gratia est Creatoris Dei, a qua mirabiliter sunt creati homines et angeli, sicut dicitur in Genesi. « In principio creavit Deus coelum et terram (Gen. I), » id est in seipso qui est principium omnium creaturarum creavit Deus angelicam et terrenam 567Cnaturam. A qua videlicet angelica natura illi angeli per superbiam recedentes apostataverunt, qui postea daemones effecti contra nostram naturam in paradiso mediante serpente pugnaverunt. De quibus in hoc loco eadem natura nostra merito conqueritur dicens: Filii matris meae pugnaverunt contra me, ac si dicat: Illi qui mecum a summo Creatore misericorditer creati sunt, ipsi me de paradiso, in quo posita eram, per serpentis suggestionem crudeliter expulerunt. Posuerunt me custodem in vineis; vineam meam non custodivi. Possumus istam sententiam secundum illum sermonem interpretari de quo scribitur in Genesi: « Plantaverat autem Dominus paradisum voluptatis a principio, in quo posuit hominem quem 567Dformaverat, ut operaretur et custodiret illud (Gen. II). » Quasi enim in vineis nostra natura custos posita fuit, quando Deus in paradiso diversi generis arboribus consito primum hominem misit, ut videlicet cogitando et loquendo quod Deo placitum esset, operaretur, et seipsam custodiret, ne a diabolo deciperetur. Quasi vero vineis paradisi nostra natura praelata fuit, quando a Domino audivit: « Ex omni ligno paradisi comede, de ligno vero scientiae boni et mali noli comedere (ibid.), » verumtamen quia natura nostra hanc obedientiam minime servavit sed diabolo per inobedientiam se substravit, idcirco et vineas paradisi quibus praelata fuerat, perdidit, et seipsam, hoc est vineam suam, male custodivit.

RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING HOW THIS SENTENCE, NAMELY, « I AM BLACK, BUT COMELY, » OUGHT TO BE REFERRED ALLEGORICALLY TO OUR NATURE.

I think therefore that we do no wrong, if we refer this sentence: I am black, but comely, to our nature 566Callegorically. For the spiritual man understands all things spiritually. Let our nature then say — not existing in that state in which it was founded by the highest Creator, but rather in that in which it remained, seduced by the devil: I am black on account of the disobedience and the sin of the first Adam my father, but I shall be comely on account of the obedience and the justification of the second Adam, that is, of Christ my redeemer. Whence Paul says: « As by the disobedience of one man many were made sinners, so by the obedience of one just man many shall be constituted (Rom. V). » [Vulg. Rom. 5:19 per unius oboeditionem iusti constituentur multi, "by the obedience of one, many shall be made just" — there iusti is the predicate of multi, not a genitive with unius] As the tents of Cedar, that is, as Cedar was once made unstable on account of the persecution which he suffered from his neighbors, so from this time on I shall be unstable and shall never remain in the same state, on account of 566Dthe proud hope of empty glory which the devil falsely promised me, when he persuaded me to believe him, saying: « If you eat of the forbidden fruit, your eyes shall be opened, and you shall be as gods (Gen. III). » [Vulg. Gen. 3:5 in quocumque die comederitis ex eo, "in what day soever you shall eat thereof"; the Vulgate has nothing answering to de fructu vetito, "of the forbidden fruit"] As the skin of Solomon. The Catholic Church holds and believes for certain that blessed Mary, who ever remained an inviolate virgin, drew her origin from our corrupted nature and sinful lump. Which blessed Virgin was made not only the skin, but even the tabernacle of Solomon, that is, of the true peaceable one, Christ, when the same Christ deigned for 330 the salvation of the human race to be born of her. Let our nature then say; Through the first Adam, a sinner, I have been made black, but through the second Adam, my redeemer, I shall be 567Acomely. I am black, because I believed the devil rather than God, but I shall be comely, because the Son of God will take flesh of me, and will lead me back to the contemplating of the glory of the divine majesty — to which, from that state in which I had been created, I should have passed over without the pain of death, had I not, seduced by the devil with an empty hope, miserably fallen. Do not consider me that I am swarthy, that is, do not contemplate me such as I was made at the persuasion of the ancient enemy, but rather contemplate me such as I was created by the highest Creator. Because the sun hath discolored me. If by the sun we take temptation, we have a true and most approved sense. For the Lord says in the Gospel concerning the seeds sown upon the rock, that is, concerning unbelieving Christians and perverse heretics, that: 567B« When the sun was risen they withered away (Luc. VIII), » that is, when temptation had arisen they failed in faith and in work. [Vulg. Luc. 8:6 natum aruit, "having sprung up it withered"; sole orto belongs to Matth. 13:6, where the verb is aestuaverunt, "they were scorched"] Finally in the book of Psalms it is found written: « By day the sun shall not burn thee (Psal. CXX), » that is, in prosperity no temptation shall deceive thee. Let our nature then say: because the sun hath discolored me, that is, the temptation brought in by the devil took from me the color of immortality and of innocence, and discolored me with the color of death and with the blackness of sin. The sons of my mother have fought against me. The mother of our nature and of the angelic nature is the most clement grace of God the Creator, by which men and angels were wonderfully created, as is said in Genesis. « In the beginning God created heaven and earth (Gen. I), » that is, in himself, who is the beginning of all creatures, God created the angelic and the earthly 567Cnature. From which angelic nature, namely, those angels, withdrawing through pride, apostatized, who afterwards, made demons, fought against our nature in paradise by the mediation of the serpent. Of whom in this place our same nature rightly complains, saying: The sons of my mother have fought against me, as if she should say: They who together with me were mercifully created by the highest Creator, they themselves cruelly drove me out of paradise, in which I had been set, through the suggestion of the serpent. They have set me keeper in the vineyards; my vineyard I have not kept. We can interpret this sentence according to that word of which it is written in Genesis: « Now the Lord had planted a paradise of pleasure from the beginning, in which he placed the man whom 567Dhe had formed, that he might work and keep it (Gen. II). » For our nature was set as it were a keeper in the vineyards, when God sent the first man into paradise, planted with trees of divers kinds, that namely, by thinking and by speaking what should be pleasing to God, it might work and might keep itself, lest it be deceived by the devil. And our nature was as it were set over the vineyards of paradise, when it heard from the Lord: « Of every tree of paradise eat thou, but of the tree of the knowledge of good and evil do not eat (ibid.), » yet nevertheless, because our nature by no means kept this obedience but laid itself under the devil through disobedience, therefore it both lost the vineyards of paradise over which it had been set, and kept ill its own self, that is, its own vineyard.

RECAPITULATIO A SUPERIORIBUS

RECAPITULATION FROM THE FOREGOING

SECUNDUM MORALITATEM.

  • 568A

Igitur quoniam Deo auxiliante triplicem hujus sententiae allegoriam disseruimus, restat nunc ut eodem adjuvante, moralitatem, quae in ea latet, in lucem proferamus, et nobismetipsis, id est animae nostrae, rationabiliter coaptemus. Dicat ergo etiam anima nostra tentationibus diversis et innumerabilibus fatigata, spinis et aculeis, calamitatibus et miseriis suae carnis vallata, putredine et corruptione suae carnis vel corporis aggravata: nigra sum, quia « caro concupiscit adversus spiritum (Galat. V) » et indesinenter me corpus meum trahit ad peccatum (Rom. VI); nigra sum, inquit, quia video aliam legem in membris meis repugnantem legi mentis meae et 568Bcaptivum me ducentem in legem peccati. Et modo subjunxit qualiter nigra sit, scilicet, sicut tabernacula Cedar. Hoc est pigra et instabilis sum in bonum, prona vero et labilis sum ad malum. De isto autem Cedar conqueritur Psalmista cum dicit: « Heu mihi! quia habitavi cum habitantibus Cedar (Psal. CXL). » id est cum his qui tenebras diligunt, et instabiles mente et corpore existunt. Unde Dominus ad Ezechielem loquitur dicens: « Fili hominis, inter scorpiones habitas (Ezech. II). » Filium autem hominis prophetam suum ideo Dominus appellavit, ne idem propheta inde superbiret, et ad vanam gloriam laberetur, quod ei Deus suus loqueretur. Scorpio autem blandum et mitem vultum habet, sed retro vulnere arcuato cauda percutere solet. Qui sunt ergo isti 568Cscorpiones, nisi seductores homines, et daemones, vitia carnis, nostrae concupiscentiae, et voluptates? Ista enim omnia quasi scorpiones blandum et suavissimum vultum nobis ostendunt, quando ad peccatum delectando nos attrahunt; sed postea retro quasi cauda percutiunt, et occidunt, quia pro eo quod eis ad peccandum assensum praebuimus, in extremo die judicii cum his omnibus irremediabiliter inferni tormenta sustinebimus. Et hoc est nimirum scorpiones cauda ferire et occidere. Quos igitur Ezechiel scorpiones vocat, illos David in Psalmis, 331 et Salomon in Canticis Cedar appellat. Sed et tu quicunque in Ecclesia Dei stans oras vel psallis, quotiescunque mente et animo per universum 568Dmundum vagaris, et immundis et tetris turpibusque intus cogitationibus devastaris, scito te habitare cum habitantibus Cedar. Sed et hoc quod sequitur in eodem versu, valde timendum est, scilicet: « Multum incola fuit anima mea (Psal. CXL), » id est valde diu delectata est anima mea cum habitantibus Cedar, hoc est cum vitiis et concupiscentiis, cum voluptatibus et immundis desideriis, quae omnia Cedar, id est tenebrae sunt, quia hominem tenebrosum faciunt. Unde Paulus dicit: « Fuistis aliquando tenebrae, » id est peccatores; « nunc autem lux, » id est justi, in Domino (Ephes. V). » Sed formosa sum sicut pellis Salomonis. Paulo superius similitudinem dedit, videlicet tabernacula Cedar, quibus se assimilavit cum dicit: Nigra sum sicut 569Atabernacula Cedar. Modo vero comparationem reddit, scilicet, pellem Salomonis, cui se per formositatem comparavit dicens: Sed formosa sum sicut pellis Salomonis. Per quam pellem Salomonis diximus superius totum tabernaculum designari, quod de pellibus constructum fuit. Sed necesse est ut aliquid de eo secundum moralitatem loquamur: « Tabernaculum fuit constructum de pellibus rubricatis hyacinthinis et de ciliciis (Exod. XXVI). Per pelles rubricatas intelligimus martyres, qui sanguinem suum pro Deo fuderunt, per hyacinthinas vero pelles, accipimus confessores, qui pro amore coelestis patriae toto vitae suae cursu laboraverunt. Per cilicia vero, peccatores accipimus. Si ergo anima nostra vult esse tabernaculum Dei, habeat in se charitatem 569Bmartyrum, fidem confessorum, spem peccatorum. Possideat enim necesse est adhuc inter adversa et prospera fortitudinem et patientiam martyrum. In bonis operibus perseverantiam, et longanimitatem confessorum, poenitentiam et confessionem humilem peccatorum. Praeterea bonum est animae nostrae, ut ipsa sit spiritualiter arca Testamenti, quae erat in tabernaculo Dei. Arca enim de lignis imputribilibus in quadrum erat constituta, optimo et pretioso auro deaurata, super duos vectes in quibus portabatur, stabilita (Exod. XXV). Si ergo anima nostra exempla sanctorum (qui sunt ligna imputribilia) imitata fuerit, et quatuor Evangelia (quae per quadraturam arcae significantur) memoriter retinuerit, et sapientiae spirituali quae per aurum designatur, operam dederit, 569Cet Vetus et Novum Testamentum, quae per duos vectes figuraliter, spiritualiter intellexerit; tunc anima nostra arca spiritualis in tabernaculo Dei erit. In tabernaculo denique urna continens coeleste manna fuit, et duae tabulae testamenti, et virga Aaron quae fronduit (III Reg. VIII; Hebr. IX). » Ista ergo nos oportet omnia spiritualiter possidere, ut videlicet habeamus urnam continentem manna coeleste, id est ut credamus Christum et verum Deum, et verum hominem esse. Per urnam enim humanitas, per manna vero coeleste ejus divinitas intelligitur. Sed et nihilominus duae tabulae Testamenti necessariae nobis sunt, ut postquam de Christo quod verus sit Deus et verus homo, perfecte crediderimus, de Patre 569Det Spiritu sancto, qui per duas tabulas designantur, sanum sensum et catholicum teneamus, credendo quia Pater et Filius et Spiritus sanctus unus sit Deus. Cum ergo sanctam Trinitatem, id est Patrem et Filium et Spiritum sanctum, unum Deum confessi fuerimus, necessarium puto ut de humanitate Christi, quam in beata Maria sumpsit, quae per virgam Aaron demonstratur, catholice sentiamus. Ut videlicet indubitanter credamus, quia sicut eadem virga sine cultura humani auxilii floruit et fructum fecit, sic beata Virgo Maria absque contactu hominis Filium Dei de Spiritu sancto concepit et peperit. Si ergo anima nostra tabernaculum Dei spirituale fuerit, si intra se arcam, urnam, tabulas, virgam Aaron spiritualiter continuerit; tunc veraciter dicere poterit: 570AFormosa sum sicut pellis Salomonis. Nolite me considerare quod fusca sum, quia decoloravit me sol. Quando pro amore Dei et pro intentione coelestis patriae Evangelium auditoribus nostris praedicamus, si forte ex ipsa nostra locutione vanam gloriam incurrimus, vel ideo, quia bene loquimur, vel quia libenter a populo audimur, tunc dicere debet anima nostra vanae gloriae contagio fuscata, nolite me considerare quod fusca sum, id est nolite me despicere quia peccavi, quoniam pro amore Christi inanis gloriae lapsum incurri: non quod ei placeat, si in ejus opere deliqui, sed quia nisi pro amore ejus praedicationis officium assumerem, nullatenus ad hanc peccati maculam devenissem. Vel certe, nolite me considerare quod fusca sum, id est nolite me arbitrari 570Besse peccatricem in mea natura, quia a summo Deo sine peccati macula sum creata: et quod peccatrix modo in praesentia appareo, non naturaliter hoc ex me possideo, sed quia hoc soli justitiae, id est Deo placuit qui me ad istam carnem peccatricem secundum suam justam voluntatem transmisit, ex cujus inhabitatione mea natura peccatum contraxit, quae prius sine peccati labe a bono Creatore creata fuit. Sicut enim vinum bonum cum in vasculum corruptum mittitur, statim corrumpitur, non quod ex natura sua hoc habeat ut corrumpatur, sed participatione vasculi corrupti in quo missum est, corrumpitur: sic anima nostra non naturaliter 332 est peccatrix et corrupta, sed participatione corporis corrupti in quo est posita, cum sit naturaliter incorrupta, 570Cefficitur accidentaliter corrupta. Sane quomodo hoc eveniat nullus scit, sed quia ita Deo placet, ab initio et placuit, qui in sua sancta dispositione ab aeterno omnia irreprehensibiliter ordinavit. Et hoc est quod sequitur, quia decoloravit me sol. Filii matris meae pugnaverunt contra me. Filii matris animae nostrae possunt allegorice appellari quinque sensus carnis, scilicet visus, auditus, odoratus, gustus et tactus, a quibus humana caro regitur et gubernatur, sicut materia filiis suis sustentatur et adjuvatur. Qui filii carnis, quomodo quotidiano conflictu adversus animam praelientur, experimento satis probabili possumus indicare. Nonne visus pugnat adversus animam quando oculus videt mulierem ad 570Dconcupiscendam eam? Unde Jeremias ait: « Oculi mei depraedati sunt animam meam (Thren. III). » Vere etenim infelices oculi miseram animam depraedantur et oppugnant, cum illicita quae exterius vident, intus illi annuntiant et simplicem castitatem, qua Deo anima nostra familiariter conjungitur, illi auferre moliuntur. Pugnam vero quam auditus noster contra animam indesinenter exercet, facile quilibet ostendere potest. Quidquid enim audimus quod peccatum est, et a bono verae religionis discordat, hoc totum sine dubio animam nostram vulnerat. Quid igitur de odoratu referam qui omnia quae exteriori flatu trahit ad se intus, mittit ad animam? Dumque eum exterioribus delectationibus inficit, quasi vulneribus inflictis illam debilitat et occidit. Os vero 571Anostrum quomodo mentiendo animam interficiat, Salomon interrogatus respondeat, sic enim ipse dicit: « Os quod mentitur, occidit animam (Sap. I). » Quod profecto David testatur, dicens in libro Psalmorum: « Perdes omnes qui loquuntur mendacium (Psal. V). » Et Dominus in Evangelio: « Ex ore tuo, inquit, justificaberis, et ex ore tuo condemnaberis (Matth. XII). » Ergo juxta sententiam Domini, os nostrum loquendo, et comedendo illicita, nos condemnat. Si vero comedimus quae nobis necessaria sunt, et loquimur quae Deo placita sunt, tunc os nostrum nos justificat. Quibus vero telis tactus oppugnet animam, noverunt milites Christi, qui quotidie praeliantur spiritualia praelia Domini. Unde et divina Scriptura testatur: « Sicut qui tangit picem 571Bab ipsa inquinatur, sic qui tangit uxorem proximi sui, immundus prorsus efficitur (Eccli. XIII). » Dicat ergo anima nostra: Filii matris meae pugnaverunt contra me, id est quinque sensus qui in carne regnant, indesinenti bello me impugnant, et quia dum in carne sum, uti me oportet eorum solatio, nullatenus valeo transire, vel solam diei horam absque illorum praelio. Posuerunt me custodem in vineis, vineam neam non custodivi. Manifestum est et nullus religiose vivens dubitat, quia quanto diligentius unusquisque alterius actionibus custodiendis invigilat, tanto minus sollicite aliquando suas actiones observat. Quod autem nostrae actiones vineae appellantur, beatus Gregorius in suis Scripturis testatur. 571CDicat ergo anima nostra: Posuerunt me custodem in vineis, vineam meam non custodivi. Quod est dicere: Dum proximorum meorum actiones custodire et emendare cupio, meas proprias actiones minus studiose procuro. Siquidem et illi qui temporales hujus saeculi vineas custodiunt, multoties ventorum flantium et imbrium irruentium pericula incurrunt, nec timent pro earumdem vinearum observatione proprii corporis jacturam sustinere, dum diligunt praemia temporalia, quae a dominis vinearum arbitrantur se posse recipere. Sic namque, sic faciunt praelati sanctae Ecclesiae. Cum enim per praedicationem colligendis animabus inserviunt, minus quam necesse esset, interdum sui ipsius sollicitudinem gerunt, et nonnunquam pro ipsa sua praedicatione a persecutoribus 571Dmortem corporis libenter suscipiunt, quia aeterna praemia quae a Deo exspectant, ante oculos suos proponunt, quod utique apostoli et martyres fecerunt.

ACCORDING TO THE MORALITY.

  • 568A

Since therefore, God helping, we have set forth the threefold allegory of this sentence, it now remains that, with the same one aiding us, we bring to light the morality which lies hidden in it, and reasonably fit it to our very selves, that is, to our soul. Let our soul also then say — wearied with divers and innumerable temptations, walled about with the thorns and the stings, the calamities and the miseries of its flesh, weighed down with the rottenness and the corruption of its flesh or body: I am black, because « the flesh lusteth against the spirit (Galat. V) » and unceasingly my body drags me to sin (Rom. VI); I am black, she says, because I see another law in my members fighting against the law of my mind and 568Bleading me captive into the law of sin. And now she has subjoined in what manner she is black, namely, as the tents of Cedar. That is, I am sluggish and unstable toward the good, but prone and slippery toward the evil. But of this Cedar the Psalmist complains when he says: « Woe is me! that I have dwelt with the inhabitants of Cedar (Psal. CXL). » [Vulg. Psal. CXIX, 5 habitationibus Cedar, "the dwellings of Cedar"] that is, with those who love darkness, and are unstable in mind and in body. Whence the Lord speaks to Ezechiel, saying: « Son of man, thou dwellest among scorpions (Ezech. II). » But the Lord called his prophet "son of man" for this reason, lest the same prophet should be made proud thereat, and slip into vain glory, because his God spoke to him. Now the scorpion has a bland and mild countenance, but behind it is wont to strike with its tail in an arched wound. Who then are these 568Cscorpions, if not seducing men, and demons, the vices of the flesh, our concupiscences, and pleasures? For all these things, like scorpions, show us a bland and most sweet countenance, when by delighting us they draw us to sin; but afterwards from behind they strike as with a tail, and they kill, because, for that we gave them our assent unto sinning, on the last day of judgment we shall with all these irremediably endure the torments of hell. And this doubtless is for scorpions to strike with the tail and to kill. Those therefore whom Ezechiel calls scorpions, those David in the Psalms, 331 and Solomon in the Canticles calls Cedar. But thou also, whoever thou art that standing in the Church of God dost pray or sing psalms, as often as thou wanderest in mind and in spirit through the whole 568Dworld, and art laid waste within by unclean and foul and shameful thoughts, know thou that thou dwellest with the inhabitants of Cedar. But this also which follows in the same verse is greatly to be feared, namely: « My soul hath long been a sojourner (Psal. CXL), » that is, my soul has taken delight very long with the inhabitants of Cedar, that is, with vices and concupiscences, with pleasures and unclean desires, all which are Cedar, that is, darkness, because they make a man dark. Whence Paul says: « You were once darkness, » that is, sinners; « but now light, » that is, just, in the Lord (Ephes. V). » But I am comely as the skin of Solomon. A little above she gave a likeness, namely the tents of Cedar, to which she likened herself when she says: I am black as 569Athe tents of Cedar. But now she renders a comparison, namely, the skin of Solomon, to which she compared herself through comeliness, saying: But I am comely as the skin of Solomon. By which skin of Solomon we said above that the whole tabernacle is designated, which was built of skins. But it is necessary that we say somewhat of it according to the morality: « The tabernacle was built of skins dyed red, of hyacinth-colored skins, and of haircloths (Exod. XXVI). By the skins dyed red we understand the martyrs, who poured out their blood for God; and by the hyacinth-colored skins we take the confessors, who for the love of the heavenly country labored through the whole course of their life. And by the haircloths we take sinners. If therefore our soul wishes to be the tabernacle of God, let it have in itself the charity 569Bof martyrs, the faith of confessors, the hope of sinners. For it must needs possess, still amid adversity and prosperity, the fortitude and the patience of the martyrs. In good works, the perseverance and the longsuffering of the confessors; the penance and the humble confession of sinners. Moreover it is good for our soul that it be spiritually the ark of the Testament, which was in the tabernacle of God. For the ark was set up four-square of woods that cannot rot, gilded with the best and precious gold, made steady upon two bars by which it was carried (Exod. XXV). If therefore our soul shall have imitated the examples of the saints (who are the woods that cannot rot), and shall have retained by memory the four Gospels (which are signified by the squareness of the ark), and shall have given labor to the spiritual wisdom which is designated by the gold, 569Cand shall have understood spiritually both the Old and the New Testament, which [are set forth] figuratively by the two bars; then our soul will be a spiritual ark in the tabernacle of God. In the tabernacle, finally, there was an urn containing the heavenly manna, and the two tables of the testament, and the rod of Aaron which budded (III Reg. VIII; Hebr. IX). » All these things therefore it behooves us to possess spiritually, that namely we may have the urn containing the heavenly manna, that is, that we may believe Christ to be both true God and true man. For by the urn his humanity, and by the heavenly manna his divinity, is understood. But none the less are the two tables of the Testament necessary to us, that after we shall have perfectly believed concerning Christ that he is true God and true man, we may hold a sound and Catholic mind concerning the Father 569Dand the Holy Spirit, who are designated by the two tables, believing that the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit is one God. When therefore we shall have confessed the holy Trinity, that is, the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, to be one God, I think it necessary that we hold a Catholic judgment concerning the humanity of Christ, which he took in blessed Mary, who is shown forth by the rod of Aaron. That namely we may believe without doubt that, as the same rod without the tillage of human help flowered and made fruit, so the blessed Virgin Mary without the touch of man conceived and bore the Son of God of the Holy Spirit. If therefore our soul shall have been the spiritual tabernacle of God, if within itself it shall have spiritually contained the ark, the urn, the tables, the rod of Aaron; then truly will it be able to say: 570AI am comely as the skin of Solomon. Do not consider me that I am swarthy, because the sun hath discolored me. When for the love of God and for the intent of the heavenly country we preach the Gospel to our hearers, if perchance out of that very speaking of ours we incur vain glory, either because we speak well, or because we are gladly heard by the people, then our soul, made swarthy by the contagion of vain glory, ought to say, do not consider me that I am swarthy, that is, do not despise me because I have sinned, since for the love of Christ I have incurred the lapse of empty glory: not that it pleases him, if I have offended in his own work, but because, unless for love of him I were to undertake the office of preaching, I should in no way have come to this stain of sin. Or assuredly, do not consider me that I am swarthy, that is, do not judge me 570Bto be a sinner in my nature, because by the most high God I was created without the stain of sin: and that I now appear a sinner in the present, this I do not possess naturally out of myself, but because this pleased the sun of justice, that is, God, who according to his just will sent me over to this sinful flesh, from the indwelling of which my nature contracted sin, which before had been created without the blemish of sin by the good Creator. For as good wine, when it is put into a corrupt little vessel, is at once corrupted — not that from its own nature it has this, that it should be corrupted, but by the participation of the corrupt vessel into which it has been put it is corrupted — so our soul is not naturally 332 a sinner and corrupt, but by the participation of the corrupt body in which it has been placed, although it is naturally incorrupt, 570Cit is made accidentally corrupt. Truly how this comes to pass no one knows, but because it so pleases God, and pleased him from the beginning, who in his holy disposition from eternity ordained all things blamelessly. And this is what follows, because the sun hath discolored me. The sons of my mother have fought against me. The sons of the mother of our soul may allegorically be called the five senses of the flesh, namely sight, hearing, smell, taste and touch, by which the human flesh is ruled and governed, as matter is sustained and helped by its own sons. Which sons of the flesh, in what manner they war in daily conflict against the soul, we can show by an experience convincing enough. Does not sight fight against the soul when the eye sees a woman so as 570Dto lust after her? Whence Jeremias says: « My eyes have plundered my soul (Thren. III). » [Vulg. Thren. 3:51 oculus meus depraedatus est animam meam, singular, "my eye hath wasted my soul"] For truly the unhappy eyes plunder and assault the wretched soul, when the unlawful things which they see without they announce to her within, and strive to take from her the simple chastity by which our soul is familiarly joined to God. But the battle which our hearing unceasingly wages against the soul, anyone can easily show. For whatever we hear that is sin, and that disagrees from the good of true religion, all this without doubt wounds our soul. What then shall I say of the smell, which sends to the soul all the things that by an outward breath it draws to itself within? And while it infects him with outward delights, it weakens and kills her as with wounds inflicted. But how our mouth 571Aby lying slays the soul, let Solomon, being asked, answer, for thus he himself says: « The mouth that lieth killeth the soul (Sap. I). » Which David assuredly testifies, saying in the book of Psalms: « Thou wilt destroy all that speak a lie (Psal. V). » And the Lord in the Gospel: « Out of thy mouth, he says, thou shalt be justified, and out of thy mouth thou shalt be condemned (Matth. XII). » [Vulg. Matth. 12:37 ex verbis tuis, "by thy words"; the exposition that follows turns on the mouth] Therefore according to the sentence of the Lord, our mouth, by speaking and by eating unlawful things, condemns us. But if we eat the things that are necessary for us, and speak the things that are pleasing to God, then our mouth justifies us. But with what darts touch assaults the soul, the soldiers of Christ know, who daily fight the spiritual battles of the Lord. Whence Holy Scripture also testifies: « As he who touches pitch 571Bis defiled by it, so he who touches his neighbor's wife is made utterly unclean (Eccli. XIII). » [Vulg. Eccli. 13:1 qui communicaverit superbo induet superbiam, "he that hath fellowship with the proud shall put on pride"; the clause on the neighbor's wife stands at Prov. 6:29] Let our soul then say: The sons of my mother have fought against me, that is, the five senses which reign in the flesh assail me with unceasing war, and because, while I am in the flesh, I must use their solace, I am in no way able to pass over even a single hour of the day without their battle. They have set me keeper in the vineyards, the vineyard neam I have not kept. It is manifest, and no one living religiously doubts, that the more diligently each one keeps watch over the guarding of another's actions, the less carefully does he sometimes observe his own actions. And that our actions are called vineyards, blessed Gregory testifies in his writings. 571CLet our soul then say: They have set me keeper in the vineyards, my vineyard I have not kept. Which is to say: While I desire to keep and to amend the actions of my neighbors, I attend less studiously to my own proper actions. For indeed they also who keep the temporal vineyards of this world many times incur the perils of blowing winds and of rushing rains, nor do they fear, for the watching of those same vineyards, to endure the loss of their own body, while they love the temporal rewards which they judge that they can receive from the lords of the vineyards. For so, even so, do the prelates of holy Church. For when through preaching they serve for the gathering in of souls, they sometimes bear less care of their own selves than would be needful, and not seldom for that very preaching of theirs they gladly take on the death of the body from persecutors, 571Dbecause they set before their eyes the eternal rewards which they await from God — which assuredly the apostles and the martyrs did.

QUALITER ISTA SENTENTIA, « NIGRA SUM, SED FORMOSA, » ASCRIBATUR BEATAE MARIAE.

Igitur, postquam triplicem allegoriam et simplicem moralitatem hujus sententiae Deo opitulante in lucem protulimus, dignum est ut ad beatae Mariae Virginis personam stylum nostrum dirigamus, quia in ejus laudem et sanctae Ecclesiae honorem totum librum istum a Spiritu sancto editum per os Salomonis non dubitamus. Dicat ergo et ipsa beata Maria: Nigra sum, sed formosa, filiae Hierusalem, id est: ego filia coelestis Hierusalem quae sursum est mater nostra 572A(Gal. IV); nigra sum in praesenti saeculo, hoc est contemptibilis et despecta et moerore confecta, dum video filium meum invidia Judaeorum crucifixum, sanguine suo perfusum; nigra, inquam, prae tristitia praesenti sum, quia dolor anxietatis filii mei pertingit usque ad dolorem cordis mei. Unde et Simeon ad beatam Mariam dixit: « Et tuam ipsius animam pertransibit gladius (Luc. II); » quasi diceret: Dolor passionis et mortis Christi Filii tui pertinget et vulnerabit 333 intima penetralia cordis tui; sicque veram ejus genitricem te esse comprobabis, cum ei in cruce morienti intimo cordis affectu compatieris. Dicat ergo beata Maria: Nigra sum prae dolore et tristitia, quia video filium meum in cruce morientem; sed formosa sum, id est gaudeo et exsulto, quia 572Bconsidero illum a morte jam resurgentem. Sicut tabernacula Cedar nigra sum, sed sicut pellis Salomonis formosa sum. Per tabernacula, quae portando de loco ad locum mutantur, transitoria et mutatoria praesentis vitae tristitia intelligitur; per pellem vero, id est per tabernaculum Salomonis, coelestis gloria quam Deus inhabitat, designatur. Dicat ergo beata Maria: Ego quidem transitoria et temporali tristitia pro morte filii mei modo afficior, sed post paululum illi resurgenti, et ad coelos ascendenti, et in coelesti gloria regnanti, congratulabor. Nolite me considerare quod fusca sum, quia decoloravit me sol, id est nolite me despectui habere, nec contemnere, quia in tribulatione praesenti afflictam cernitis, quoniam quidem, o vos Judaei, propter filium meum, qui est sol justitiae, 572Ccontribulor, et affligor, quem vos interfecistis. Vel certe nolite me contemplari calamitate et miseria pro morte filii mei denigratam, sed considerate me jam ad dexteram ipsius collocatam, et in aeterna claritate cum ipso regnantem. Filii matris meae pugnaverunt contra me. Spiritualiter autem filii matris beatae et perpetuae Virginis Mariae, Judaei iniqui fuerunt, qui filii Synagogae exstiterunt, et sine misericordia contra eam pugnaverunt, quando ejus filium in cruce peremerunt. A dolore enim et passione quam Filius in cruce sustinuit, nullatenus immunis fuit. Praeterea credendum est quia anxietas mortis Christi in cruce morientis pervenit usque ad viscera piissimae Matris, nec poterat nisi cum magno dolore in cruce 572Dpendentem aspicere, quem et verum Deum esse, et de carne sua carnem sciebat veraciter assumpsisse. Posuerunt me custodem in vineis, vineam meam non custodivi. Exsulta et lauda, habitatio Sion (Isa. XII), » id est Ecclesia de gentibus congregata, quae a tanta custode custodiris et gubernaris. Laetare, inquam, quia Regina coelorum et Domina angelorum posita est custos tui. Tu es enim spiritualis vinea Domini Sabaoth, id est exercituum: tibi praelata est custos mundi imperatrix, quae exaltata est super choros angelorum. De qua merito dicitur, quia vineam suam non custodivit, et quod propter infidelitatem et superbiam Judaeorum Synagogam deseruit. Quam utique videbatur debuisse carnali affectu custodire, et orationum suarum auxilio fovere, quia de ea dignoscitur 573Acarnalem originem duxisse. Verumtamen quoniam ipsa Synagoga, id est perfidi Judaei illam quasi sororem suam spreverunt, et eam honorare noluerunt, justo Dei judicio, in hoc saeculo et in die judicii, ejus intercessione et auxilio carebunt. « Tu ergo Hierusalem, lauda Dominum, et lauda Deum tuum, Sion (Psal. CXLVII), » id est Ecclesia gentium, quae ideo Hierusalem et Sion spiritualiter appellaris, quia Deo, qui est pax summa, per fidem et operationem inhaerere comprobaris; et in specula, hoc est in altitudine spiritualis vitae stans, hostes tuos de longe contemplaris. Lauda, inquam, Ecclesia, Deum 574Aqui dedit tibi tantam advocatricem, videlicet suam gloriosam genitricem, quam idcirco Judaei despiciunt, quia illam fuisse suam sororem secundum carnem dicunt. Nos igitur Judaeorum perfidia et impietate depulsa a cordibus nostris, honoremus et magnificemus debita cum laude Matrem nostri Redemptoris. Hoc scientes pro certo, quia honor et laus, et gloria, et magnificentia, quam exhibemus ejus genitrici in terris, ipsum nobis benevolum faciet et in coelis, et venientem ad judicium placabilem sua majestate cum angelis et archangelis.

573Explicit sextus tomus.

HOW THIS SENTENCE, « I AM BLACK, BUT COMELY, » IS ASCRIBED TO BLESSED MARY.

Since therefore, after we have with God's aid brought to light the threefold allegory and the simple morality of this sentence, it is fitting that we direct our pen to the person of the blessed Virgin Mary, because we do not doubt that this whole book was put forth by the Holy Spirit through the mouth of Solomon unto her praise and unto the honor of holy Church. Let blessed Mary herself then also say: I am black, but comely, O daughters of Jerusalem, that is: I, a daughter of the heavenly Jerusalem, which is above, our mother 572A(Gal. IV); I am black in the present world, that is, contemptible and despised and worn out with mourning, while I see my son crucified by the envy of the Jews, drenched with his own blood; black, I say, am I by reason of present sadness, because the pain of my son's anguish reaches even to the pain of my heart. Whence Simeon also said to blessed Mary: « And thy own soul a sword shall pierce (Luc. II); » as if he should say: The pain of the passion and of the death of Christ thy Son shall reach and shall wound 333 the inmost recesses of thy heart; and so thou shalt prove thyself to be his true mother, when with the inmost affection of thy heart thou shalt suffer together with him dying upon the cross. Let blessed Mary then say: I am black by reason of grief and sadness, because I see my son dying upon the cross; but I am comely, that is, I rejoice and exult, because 572BI behold him already rising again from death. As the tents of Cedar I am black, but as the skin of Solomon I am comely. By the tents, which by carrying are shifted from place to place, is understood the transitory and changeable sadness of the present life; but by the skin, that is, by the tabernacle of Solomon, is designated the heavenly glory which God inhabits. Let blessed Mary then say: I indeed am now afflicted with a transitory and temporal sadness for the death of my son, but a little afterward I shall rejoice with him rising again, and ascending to the heavens, and reigning in the heavenly glory. Do not consider me that I am swarthy, because the sun hath discolored me, that is, do not hold me in contempt, nor despise me, because you discern me afflicted in the present tribulation, since indeed, O you Jews, it is on account of my son, who is the sun of justice, 572Cthat I am troubled and afflicted — whom you have slain. Or assuredly, do not contemplate me blackened with calamity and misery for the death of my son, but consider me as already placed at his right hand, and reigning with him in eternal brightness. The sons of my mother have fought against me. But spiritually the sons of the mother of the blessed and perpetual Virgin Mary were the wicked Jews, who stood forth as the sons of the Synagogue, and without mercy fought against her, when they slew her son upon the cross. For from the pain and the passion which the Son endured on the cross she was in no way exempt. Moreover it is to be believed that the anguish of the death of Christ dying on the cross reached even to the inward parts of his most loving Mother, nor could she look upon him hanging on the cross 572Dsave with great pain, whom she knew both to be true God, and to have truly taken flesh of her own flesh. They have set me keeper in the vineyards, my vineyard I have not kept. Rejoice and praise, O habitation of Sion (Isa. XII), » that is, the Church gathered from the Gentiles, thou who art kept and governed by so great a keeper. Be glad, I say, because the Queen of the heavens and the Lady of the angels has been set as thy keeper. For thou art the spiritual vineyard of the Lord Sabaoth, that is, of hosts: over thee is set as keeper the empress of the world, who has been exalted above the choirs of the angels. Of whom it is deservedly said that she did not keep her own vineyard, and that on account of the unbelief and the pride of the Jews she forsook the Synagogue. Which assuredly she seemed to have been bound to keep with a carnal affection, and to cherish with the help of her prayers, because she is known 573Ato have drawn her carnal origin from it. Nevertheless, since that same Synagogue, that is, the faithless Jews, spurned her as though she were their sister, and would not honor her, by the just judgment of God, both in this world and in the day of judgment, they shall lack her intercession and her help. « Thou therefore, Jerusalem, praise the Lord, and praise thy God, O Sion (Psal. CXLVII), » that is, O Church of the Gentiles, thou who art therefore spiritually called Jerusalem and Sion, because thou art proved to cleave by faith and by work to God, who is the highest peace; and standing upon the watchtower, that is, upon the height of the spiritual life, thou beholdest thine enemies afar off. Praise, I say, O Church, the God 574Awho has given thee so great an advocate, namely his own glorious mother, whom the Jews for this reason despise, because they say that she was their sister according to the flesh. Let us therefore, the faithlessness and the impiety of the Jews having been driven from our hearts, honor and magnify with due praise the Mother of our Redeemer. Knowing this for certain, that the honor and the praise, and the glory, and the magnificence which we show to his mother on earth will make him well-disposed toward us in the heavens also, and, coming to judgment in his majesty with angels and archangels, placable.

573Here ends the sixth Tome.

INCIPIT SEPTIMUS TOMUS Super hoc quod dicitur: Indica mihi quem diligit anima mea, ubi pascas, ubi cubes in meridie, ne vagari incipiam post greges sodalium tuorum (Cant. I).

573BInter duodecim lapides pretiosos, topazium caeteris lapidibus pretiosiorem qui reperire desiderat, necesse est ut omnium lapidum quantitates et colores naturaliter agnoscat, ne forte errore ductus alterum pro altero indifferenter accipiat. Sunt enim inter duodecim, duo lapides aliqua ex parte topazio similes, videlicet chrysolithus et chrysophrasus, qui videntur esse aurei coloris sicuti est topazius. Sed in hoc tamen topazius ab aliis lapidibus differt, quia rarior inventione est, et, ut dicitur, omni colore refulget. Idem vero lapis reperitur in Aethiopia et in regione Thebaida, unde et in eadem provincia 334 insula esse refertur, quae Topazion appellatur, ubi idem lapis topazius invenitur. Quod autem de Aethiopia topazius veniat, Dominus, ad beatum Job loquens, manifestat. 573CCum enim ad eumdem Job Dominus loqueretur de sapientia: « Non adaequabitur, inquit, ei topazius de Aethiopia (Job XXVI). » Qui sunt ergo duodecim lapides pretiosi, nisi duodecim apostoli supra fundamentum, quod est Christus, fundati? Quem vero alium significat topazius rarior et pretiosior caeteris lapidibus, nisi Dominum nostrum Jesum Christum, qui est « speciosus forma prae filiis hominum (Psal. XLIV), » et est humilior omnibus hominibus? Inde etiam discipulis suis ipse in Evangelio fertur dixisse: « Discite a me quia mitis sum et humilis corde (Matth. XI). » Unde bene dicitur quod topazius omni resplendeat colore, quia « in ipso omnis plenitudo divinitatis inhabitat (Coloss. II), » et ipse decoratus 573Dest ornatu omnis spiritualis virtutis, quia secundum Joannem, « Vidimus eum quasi unigenitum a Patre, plenum gratiae et veritatis (Joan. I). » Praeterea quod idem lapis topazius a seipso nomen insulae donavit in qua reperitur, ut videlicet a topazio insula Topazion appelletur, nullatenus absque ingenti sacramento factum esse creditur. Sicut enim per topazium Christum, sic et per hanc insulam, intelligimus sanctam Ecclesiam. Et quemadmodum haec 574Binsula a topazio lapide nomen est sortita, ut ab ipso Topazion appellata, ita et sancta Ecclesia a Christo angulari lapide Christiana est nuncupata, et omnes fideles Christiani delectantur nomine Christi insigniri. Sane quod nos sanctam Ecclesiam insulae comparavimus, secundum Isaiam prophetam locuti sumus, qui omnes Ecclesias per prophetiae spiritum appellat insulas dicens: « Audite, insulae, et attendite, populi de longe (Isa. XLIX). » Merito ergo Ecclesiae gentium insulis comparantur, quia sicut insulae ventorum flatibus verberantur et undarum irruentium tumultibus saepe concutiuntur, sic Ecclesiae per diversas mundi partes diffusae, magnis persecutionibus deprimuntur, et innumerabilibus tentationibus agitantur. Sed et hoc quod topazius secundum sententiam 574CDomini alloquentis, in Aethiopia reperiri dicitur, et quod sapientiae idem topazius nullatenus coaequetur, satis convenienter Domino Jesu coaptetur. Per sapientiam enim, Christi divinitas, per topazium vero digne intelligitur ejus humanitas, qui topazius de Aethiopica terra tollitur, quia humanitas ejus de nostra peccatrice et tenebrosa natura assumitur, et nequaquam ejus divinitati comparare possumus ipsius humanitatem, quia aequalis est Patri secundum divinitatem, minor vero Patre secundum humanitatem. Hoc denique idem Filius manifestat in Evangelio dicens: « Ego et Pater unum sumus (Joan. X), » quod pertinet ad ejus divinitatem; et alio loco: « Pater major me est (Joan. XIV), » 574Dquod pertinet ad ejus humanitatem. Unde et David propheta testatur in Psalmis: « Minuisti eum paulo minus ab angelis (Psal. VIII). » Paululum minus quam angeli Filius Dei est minoratus, quia cum esset Deus immortalis et invisibilis, pro nobis homo mortalis et visibilis est factus. Et quoniam pro salute generis humani jussu Patris sui tantum se humiliavit; idcirco ab eodem Patre suo nomen quod est super omne nomen, accepit, ut in nomine ejus 575Aomne genu flectatur, et ab omni creatura coeli et terrae (Philip. II), cum Patre et Spiritu sancto unus Deus adoretur. Et hoc est quod in isto versu sequitur: « Gloria et honore coronasti eum, Domine, et constituisti eum super opera manuum tuarum (Psal. VIII). »

Igitur post hanc parvam discretionem quam fecimus, jam redeamus ad id unde longius evagati sumus, et intenta cordis aure audiamus quid est quod dicit Sponsa ad Sponsum, Ecclesia ad Christum: Indica mihi quem diligit anima mea, ubi pascas, ubi cubes in meridie, etc. Ac si dicat: Topazius es, lapis pretiosus es; te quaero, te desidero, te super omnia diligo. Indica mihi te ipsum, quia inter tot lapides pretiosos te reperire non possum, et hoc est quod 575Bsequitur: Ne forte vagari incipiam per greges sodalium tuorum, quaerendo te sic, ut quondam illi vagabantur et errabant, de quibus in Evangelio Dominus dicebat: « Quem dicunt homines esse hominis Filium? Responderunt discipuli: Alii Eliam, alii Jeremiam, aut unum ex prophetis (Matth. XVI). » Isti ergo omnes incipiebant vagari post gregem sodalium, hoc est inter lapides pretiosos errabant, quaerentes topazium, et volebant accipere pro topazio chrysolithum, vel chrysophrasum, qui parte ex aliqua similes sunt ei, quia sunt sicuti ipse est coloris aurei. Audierant enim prophetas miracula fecisse sicut Christum, quod est coloris aurei esse sicut topazium. Sed Petrus Spiritu sancto revelante aliis lapidibus 575Crelictis, verum topazium agnovit quando dixit: « Tu es Christus Filius Dei vivi (ibid.). » Ac si diceret: Equidem prophetae inter homines magnae auctoritatis tempore suo exstiterunt, id est quasi lapides pretiosi miraculorum gloria refulserunt, sed tamen quemadmodum tu es Filius Dei, nullatenus fuerunt. Audiamus ergo, remoto saecularium causarum tumultu, orationem qua sancta Ecclesia Christum deprecatur, ut seipsum certis indiciis ei manifestare dignetur, ne forte aliquem sanctorum (quod absit!) pro eo adoret et colat, et suae simplicitatis errore seducta in idololatriam incidat. Consideremus denique quid sanctae Ecclesiae Christus respondeat, et qualiter 335 cultum solius divinitatis ab omnibus sanctis removeat, et eam soli Deo, cui jure debetur, attribuat. Sanctos 575Dquippe honorare debemus, non adorare. Deus autem solus est adorandus et honorandus; sancti vero pro amore ipsius sunt tantummodo honorandi, non adorandi. Dicat ergo Christus ad sanctam Ecclesiam: Tu me deprecaris quamvis remota omni ambiguitate te certificem ubi, id est in quo loco pascam vel accubem, et ego respondebo tibi quia in meridie pascor et accubo, id est in Patre meo requiesco et maneo, in quo mansi aequalis ei per omnia ab aeterno. Quod autem Filius in Patre et Pater in Filio ineffabiliter ab aeterno maneat, omnis auctoritas tam Novi quam Veteris Testamenti consona voce praedicat. Unde est illud quod Dominus loquitur ad Patrem de discipulis suis: « Pater, volo ut sicut tu es in me, et ego in te, ita et ipsi unum sint in 576Anobis (Joan. XVII). » Et alio in loco dicit Dominus ad Philippum: « Philippe, qui videt me, videt et Patrem meum, non credis quia ego in Patre et Pater est in me (Joan. XIV). » Alioquin propter ipsa opera credite, quia ergo auctoritate divinarum Scripturarum satis ostendimus quomodo in Patre manet Filius, id est in meridie accubet et pascat Christus: nunc restat ut a nobis ostendatur, ubi in divinis eloquiis Deus Pater meridies appelletur, quia hoc raro imo rarissime reperitur, et quanto rarius tanto pretiosius. Deus omnipotens « Pater misericordiarum, et Deus totius consolationis, qui consolatur nos in omni tribulatione nostra (II Cor. I), » cui displicent mala quae facimus, et cui placent bona quae agimus, ipse loquitur in Isaia propheta, 576Bdicens: « Quiescam in loco meo, et considerabo quasi meridiana lux (Isa. XVIII). » Quid est ergo quod dicit in loco meo quiescam? Quando laboravit ipse, ut modo quaerat requiem? Et quare se comparat meridianae luci plus quam aurae vel alteri horae diei? Quid vero est quod dicit considerabo, cum considerare illius sit qui rem aut nescit, aut non intelligit? Ipse enim scit et intelligit omnia antequam fiant. Quae est ergo requies Dei, nisi pax in corde fidelis Christiani et religiosi? Sic enim scriptum est: « In pace in idipsum dormiam et requiescam (Psal. IV). » Et in Isaia invenimus dicentem Dominum: « Haec est requies mea, refice lapsum (Isa. XXVIII). » Magnam igitur requiem Deo nostro damus, quando peccatorem lapsum ut a peccato 576Csuo convertatur, exhortamur. Et alibi dicit Scriptura: « Super quem requiescam, nisi super humilem et quietum, et trementem verba mea? » (Isa. LXVI.) Quis autem est locus Dei, nisi pax et justitia, pietas et misericordia, et omnium virtutum mater obedientia? Inde dicit David propheta: « In pace factus est locus ejus, et habitatio ejus in Sion (Psal. LXXV). » Sion quippe interpretatur specula. Specula vero res est alta, in qua si homo positus fuerit, longe valde a se respicere poterit. Per Sion ergo quae interpretatur specula, intelligitur omnium virtutum obedientia genitrix. In qua si quis usque ad ultimum diem vitae suae meruerit perseverare, sine dubio usque ad contemplandum 576Ddivinae majestatis claritatem poterit pervenire. Sic enim dicit divina Scriptura de obedientia: « Per obedientiam pervenitur usque ad occultorum Dei notitiam. » Quid autem est quod Deus Pater considerat, nisi hoc quod ipse secundum Job prophetam (cap. XXXI) omnes gressus, id est opera nostra dinumerat, ut videlicet post mortem unicuique nostrum secundum quod egerit reddat. Sic enim dicit Paulus apostolus: « Omnes nos oportet manifestari ante tribunal Christi, ut recipiat unusquisque prout gessit sive bonum, sive malum (II Cor. V), » Inde etiam dicit beatus Augustinus: Sicut in Ecclesia modo stamus, et nos invicem praesentes aspicimus, sic in die judicii ante tribunal Dei astituri sumus. Hanc enim animam quam modo 577Ahabemus, tunc habebimus, et in hoc ipso quo nunc induimur corpore, suscitandi sumus. Non enim sicut equi et boves facti sumus, ut aeterna morte pereamus, sed postquam hic vixerimus, alibi judicabimur, ut aut pro bonis operibus praemia consequamur aeterna, aut pro peccatis nostris supplicia perpetua consequamur. Unde et Propheta ait: « Semel locutus est Deus (Psal. LXI), » id est ab aeterno immobiliter statuit et praedestinavit. Quid? « Duo haec quae audivi (ibid.). » Quae sunt? Omnis « potestas Dei (ibid.) » Patris omnipotentis est. Ecce habes unum. Et tibi, Domine Jesu Christe, misericordia. Ecce habes alterum. Quia tu reddes in die judicii unicuique secundum opera sua (Apoc. XXII). Quod autem ad Filium, et non ad Patrem pertineat judicium, 577Baudi ipsum in Evangelio dicentem: « Pater non judicat quemquam, sed omne judicium dedit Filio (Joan. V). » Considerat ergo Deus Pater omnipotens in loco suo quasi meridiana lux omnia quae in hoc saeculo facimus, ut per Filium suum nobis reddat in die judicii secundum quod meruimus. Unde scriptum est: « Deus altissimus patiens redditor est (Eccle. V). » Et merito Deus dicitur Altissimus, quia nulla res ejus sublimitati comparatur. Patientem vero redditorem Deum divina Scriptura ideo appellat, quoniam qui peccatores ut convertantur patienter exspectat, non conversos tandem durius damnat. Sed jam intueamur cur Deus omnipotens meridianae claritati se comparavit, et quare 577Cipse qui est Creator omnium creaturarum, suae creaturae, id est claritati solis se aequiparavit. Neque enim inter omnes creaturas creaturam aliquam sole clariorem potuit quantum ad nos reperire. Sicut ergo sol prae omnibus elementis et luminaribus 336 hujus mundi ob dignitatem et claritatem sui eminet et refulget; sic Deus omnipotens omnibus rebus, quas ad laudem et gloriam nominis sui creavit, dignior et eminentior apparet. Et sicut sol fervente meridiano tempore diffusis ubique radiis admirabilis suae claritatis totum mundum illuminat, sic Deum omnipotentem considerata ejus omnipotentia et misericordia omnis creatura contremiscit et adorat. De qua misericordia dicit Psalmista: « Misericordia Domini plena est terra (Psal. XXXII); » et iterum: 577D« Misericordias Domini in aeternum cantabo (Psal. LXXXVIII). » Vere terra plena est Domini misericordia, quia omnis creatura quae in ea vivit, non hoc ex suo merito, sed de misericordia Dei, ut subsistat, accepit. Et bene idem Psalmista dicit, quod misericordias Domini in aeternum cantabit, quia et hoc quod hic bene operamur, et quod post mortem ad gaudia paradisi introducimur, hoc totum de misericordia Dei procedit, qui nos cum non essemus condidit, et diabolo suadente deceptos et perditos pietate sua salvavit. Qui etiam secundum Paulum apostolum (I Cor. II) neminem vult perire, sed omnes ad agnitionem sui vult venire. Sed si hoc constat quod Deus neminem perire vult, sed 578Aomnes ad cognitionem suae divinitatis pertingere, quare pereunt illi qui pereunt? Ideo utique pereunt, quia mandatis Dei obedire contemnunt. Ecce Deo largiente quantum potuimus ex verbis evangelicis ostendimus quod Filius ab aeterno in Patre maneat. Et ex Isaiae prophetae oraculis sententiam protulimus, in qua Deus Pater meridianam lucem se per comparationem appellat.

HERE BEGINS THE SEVENTH TOME Upon that which is said: Show me, thou whom my soul loveth, where thou feedest, where thou liest down at midday, lest I begin to wander after the flocks of thy companions (Cant. I).

573BAmong the twelve precious stones, whoever desires to find the topaz, more precious than the other stones, must needs know by nature the sizes and the colors of all the stones, lest perchance, led by error, he take the one for the other without distinction. For among the twelve there are two stones in some part like the topaz, namely the chrysolite and the chrysoprase, which seem to be of a golden color, as the topaz is. Yet in this the topaz differs from the other stones, that it is rarer in the finding, and, as is said, shines back with every color. That same stone, moreover, is found in Ethiopia and in the region of the Thebaid, whence also in the same province 334 an island is reported to be, which is called Topazion, where that same stone, the topaz, is found. And that the topaz comes from Ethiopia, the Lord, speaking to blessed Job, makes plain. 573CFor when the Lord was speaking to that same Job concerning wisdom: « The topaz of Ethiopia, he says, shall not be made equal to it (Job XXVI). » Who then are the twelve precious stones, if not the twelve apostles founded upon the foundation which is Christ? And what other does the topaz signify, rarer and more precious than the other stones, if not our Lord Jesus Christ, who is « beautiful in form above the sons of men (Psal. XLIV), » and is humbler than all men? Hence also he himself is reported in the Gospel to have said to his disciples: « Learn of me, for I am meek and humble of heart (Matth. XI). » Whence it is well said that the topaz shines back with every color, because « in him dwelleth all the fullness of the Godhead (Coloss. II), » [Vulg. in ipso inhabitat omnis plenitudo divinitatis corporaliter, adding "bodily"] and he himself is adorned 573Dwith the adornment of every spiritual virtue, because according to John, « We have seen him as the only-begotten of the Father, full of grace and truth (Joan. I). » [Vulg. et vidimus gloriam ejus, gloriam quasi unigeniti a Patre, "and we saw his glory, the glory as of the only-begotten of the Father"] Moreover, that the same stone, the topaz, has given from itself its name to the island in which it is found — namely, that from the topaz the island is called Topazion — is believed to have come about by no means without a mighty mystery. For as by the topaz we understand Christ, so also by this island we understand holy Church. And just as this 574Bisland obtained its name from the topaz stone, so as to be called Topazion from it, so also holy Church is named Christian from Christ the corner stone, and all the faithful Christians take delight in being marked with the name of Christ. Assuredly, in that we have compared holy Church to an island, we have spoken according to Isaiah the prophet, who by the spirit of prophecy calls all the Churches islands, saying: « Hear, ye islands, and hearken, ye peoples from afar (Isa. XLIX). » Rightly therefore are the Churches of the gentiles compared to islands, because as islands are lashed by the blasts of the winds and are often shaken by the tumults of the rushing waves, so the Churches spread abroad through the various parts of the world are pressed down by great persecutions and are driven about by innumerable temptations. But this also, that the topaz, according to the sentence 574Cof the Lord speaking, is said to be found in Ethiopia, and that the same topaz is by no means made equal to wisdom, may fitly enough be fitted to the Lord Jesus. For by wisdom Christ's divinity, and by the topaz his humanity, is worthily understood: which topaz is taken up out of the Ethiopian land, because his humanity is assumed out of our sinful and darkened nature; and we can in no wise compare his humanity to his divinity, because he is equal to the Father according to divinity, but less than the Father according to humanity. This, lastly, the same Son makes plain in the Gospel, saying: « I and the Father are one (Joan. X), » which pertains to his divinity; and in another place: « The Father is greater than I (Joan. XIV), » 574Dwhich pertains to his humanity. Whence also David the prophet bears witness in the Psalms: « Thou hast lessened him a little less than the angels (Psal. VIII). » By a very little less than the angels was the Son of God lessened, because, whereas he was God immortal and invisible, for us he was made man, mortal and visible. And since for the salvation of the human race, at the bidding of his Father, he so far humbled himself, therefore he received from that same Father of his the name which is above every name, that at his name 575Aevery knee should bow, and that by every creature of heaven and of earth (Philip. II) he should be adored as one God with the Father and the Holy Spirit. And this is what follows in that verse: « With glory and honor thou hast crowned him, O Lord, and hast set him over the works of thy hands (Psal. VIII). » [Vulg. has no Domine in Psal. VIII, 6]

Therefore, after this little distinction which we have made, let us now return to that from which we have strayed rather far, and with the intent ear of the heart let us hear what it is that the Bride says to the Bridegroom, the Church to Christ: Show me, thou whom my soul loveth, where thou feedest, where thou liest down at midday, etc. As if she should say: Thou art the topaz, thou art the precious stone; thee I seek, thee I desire, thee above all things I love. Show me thine own self, because among so many precious stones I cannot find thee; and this is what 575Bfollows: Lest perchance I begin to wander through the flocks of thy companions, [Vulg. and the lemma above, ne vagari incipiam post greges sodalium tuorum, "lest I begin to wander after the flocks"] seeking thee thus, as once those men wandered and erred of whom in the Gospel the Lord was saying: « Whom do men say the Son of man to be? The disciples answered: Some Elias, some Jeremias, or one of the prophets (Matth. XVI). » [Vulg. At illi dixerunt: Alii Joannem Baptistam, alii autem Eliam, alii vero Jeremiam, naming John the Baptist first] All these then were beginning to wander after the flock of the companions, that is, they were erring among the precious stones, seeking the topaz, and they wished to take for the topaz the chrysolite or the chrysoprase, which in some part are like it, because they are, as it is, of a golden color. For they had heard that the prophets had wrought miracles as Christ did, which is to be of a golden color as the topaz is. But Peter, the Holy Spirit revealing it, the other stones 575Cbeing left aside, acknowledged the true topaz when he said: « Thou art Christ the Son of the living God (ibid.). » As if he should say: The prophets indeed stood forth among men of great authority in their time, that is, like precious stones they shone with the glory of miracles; but nevertheless, in the manner in which thou art the Son of God, they in no wise were. Let us hear then, the tumult of worldly affairs put away, the prayer by which holy Church beseeches Christ that he would deign to manifest himself to her by sure tokens, lest perchance she should adore and worship some one of the saints (which God forbid!) in his stead, and, seduced by the error of her own simplicity, should fall into idolatry. Let us consider lastly what Christ answers to holy Church, and how 335 he removes the worship of the divinity alone from all the saints, and attributes it to God alone, to whom by right it is owed. For the saints 575Dwe ought to honor, not to adore. But God alone is to be adored and to be honored; the saints indeed, for love of him, are only to be honored, not to be adored. Let Christ therefore say to holy Church: Thou beseechest me that, although with all ambiguity removed I should assure thee where — that is, in what place — I feed or lie down, and I will answer thee that at midday I am fed and lie down, that is, in my Father I rest and abide, in whom I have abode equal to him in all things from eternity. And that the Son abides in the Father and the Father in the Son ineffably from eternity, every authority of the New as well as of the Old Testament proclaims with concordant voice. Whence is that which the Lord speaks to the Father concerning his disciples: « Father, I will that as thou art in me, and I in thee, so they also may be one in 576Aus (Joan. XVII). » [Vulg. Joan. XVII, 21, ut omnes unum sint, sicut tu, Pater, in me, et ego in te, ut et ipsi in nobis unum sint, without volo] And in another place the Lord says to Philip: « Philip, he that seeth me seeth my Father also; thou believest not that I am in the Father and the Father is in me (Joan. XIV). » [Vulg. Joan. XIV, 9–10, videt et Patrem (without meum), and Non creditis quia ego in Patre, et Pater in me est? — plural, and a question] Otherwise believe for the very works' sake, since therefore we have sufficiently shown by the authority of the Holy Scriptures how the Son abides in the Father, that is, how Christ lies down and feeds at midday: it now remains that it be shown by us where in the divine oracles God the Father is called midday, because this is rarely, nay most rarely, found, and the more rarely, by so much the more preciously. God almighty, « the Father of mercies, and the God of all consolation, who comforteth us in all our tribulation (II Cor. I), » to whom the evils displease which we do, and to whom the goods please which we perform, himself speaks in Isaiah the prophet, 576Bsaying: « I will rest in my place, and I will consider as the midday light (Isa. XVIII). » [Vulg. Isa. XVIII, 4, Quiescam, et considerabo in loco meo, sicut meridiana lux clara est] What then is it that he says, In my place I will rest? When did he himself labor, that he should now seek rest? And why does he compare himself to the midday light rather than to the breeze or to another hour of the day? And what is it that he says, I will consider, since to consider belongs to him who either knows not a thing or understands it not? For he himself knows and understands all things before they come to be. What then is the rest of God, if not peace in the heart of a faithful and religious Christian? For thus it is written: « In peace in the selfsame I will sleep and I will rest (Psal. IV). » And in Isaiah we find the Lord saying: « This is my rest, refresh thou the fallen (Isa. XXVIII). » [Vulg. Isa. XXVIII, 12, reficite lassum, "refresh ye the weary" — plural imperative, and lassum "weary" for lapsum "fallen"] Great rest therefore do we give to our God, when we exhort the fallen sinner to be turned from his 576Csin. And elsewhere Scripture says: « Upon whom shall I rest, if not upon the humble and the quiet, and him that trembleth at my words? » (Isa. LXVI.) [Vulg. Isa. LXVI, 2, ad quem autem respiciam, nisi ad pauperculum et contritum spiritu, et trementem sermones meos, "to whom shall I have regard, but to the poor little one and contrite of spirit"] And what is the place of God, if not peace and justice, piety and mercy, and obedience the mother of all virtues? Hence David the prophet says: « In peace is his place made, and his habitation in Sion (Psal. LXXV). » For Sion is interpreted specula. Now a watchtower is a lofty thing, upon which, if a man be set, he will be able to look very far away from himself. By Sion therefore, which is interpreted specula, is understood obedience, the mother of all virtues. In which, if any man deserve to persevere unto the last day of his life, without doubt he will be able to come even to the contemplating 576Dof the brightness of the divine majesty. For thus Holy Scripture speaks concerning obedience: « Through obedience one comes even to the knowledge of the hidden things of God. » And what is it that God the Father considers, if not this, that he himself, according to Job the prophet (cap. XXXI), numbers all our steps, that is, our works, so that, namely, after death he may render to each one of us according to what he has done. For thus says Paul the apostle: « We must all be made manifest before the tribunal of Christ, that every one may receive according as he hath done, whether it be good or evil (II Cor. V), » [Vulg. II Cor. V, 10, ut referat unusquisque propria corporis, prout gessit, "that every one may render the proper things of the body"] Hence also blessed Augustine says: "As we now stand in the Church, and behold one another present, so on the day of judgment we are to stand before the tribunal of God. For this soul which we now 577Ahave, then we shall have, and in this very body wherewith we are now clothed we are to be raised up. For we were not made like horses and oxen, that we should perish by eternal death, but after we have lived here, we shall be judged elsewhere, that either for good works we may obtain eternal rewards, or for our sins we may obtain perpetual punishments." Whence also the Prophet says: « God hath spoken once (Psal. LXI), » that is, from eternity he unchangeably established and predestined it. What? « These two things which I have heard (ibid.). » What are they? All « the power of God (ibid.) » is the almighty Father's. Behold, thou hast the one. And to thee, O Lord Jesus Christ, mercy. Behold, thou hast the other. For thou wilt render in the day of judgment to every man according to his works (Apoc. XXII). And that judgment pertains to the Son, and not to the Father, 577Bhear him saying in the Gospel: « The Father judgeth not any man, but hath given all judgment to the Son (Joan. V). » God the almighty Father therefore considers in his place, as the midday light, all the things which we do in this world, that through his Son he may render to us in the day of judgment according to what we have deserved. Whence it is written: « God most high is a patient renderer (Eccle. V). » And rightly is God called Most High, because no thing is compared to his loftiness. And Holy Scripture calls God a patient renderer for this reason, that he who patiently awaits sinners, that they may be converted, at length the more harshly condemns those not converted. But now let us behold why God almighty compared himself to the midday brightness, and why 577Che who is the Creator of all creatures made himself equal to his own creature, that is, to the brightness of the sun. For neither among all creatures could he find, so far as concerns us, any creature brighter than the sun. As therefore the sun, above all the elements and luminaries 336 of this world, stands eminent and shines back by reason of its dignity and its brightness, so God almighty appears more worthy and more eminent than all the things which he created unto the praise and glory of his name. And as the sun, in the burning midday season, with the rays of its wondrous brightness poured out everywhere, illumines the whole world, so every creature, his omnipotence and mercy being considered, trembles before God almighty and adores him. Of which mercy the Psalmist says: « The earth is full of the mercy of the Lord (Psal. XXXII); » and again: 577D« The mercies of the Lord I will sing for ever (Psal. LXXXVIII). » Truly the earth is full of the Lord's mercy, because every creature which lives in it has received this — that it may subsist — not from its own merit, but from the mercy of God. And well does the same Psalmist say that he will sing the mercies of the Lord for ever, because both this, that we work well here, and that after death we are brought into the joys of paradise, all this proceeds from the mercy of God, who founded us when we were not, and by his own tenderness saved us when we had been deceived and lost by the devil's persuasion. Who also, according to Paul the apostle (I Cor. II), wills that no man perish, but wills that all come to the acknowledgment of himself. But if this stands firm, that God wills no man to perish, but 578Awills all to attain to the knowledge of his divinity, why do they perish who perish? Assuredly they perish for this, that they scorn to obey the commandments of God. Behold, by God's bounty we have shown, as much as we could, from the words of the Gospel, that the Son abides from eternity in the Father. And from the oracles of Isaiah the prophet we have brought forth a sentence in which God the Father calls himself, by comparison, the midday light.

CONCLUSIO SENTENTIAE.

Igitur pro certo constat et nullus inde dubitat quia, si Pater est meridies, et si Filius manet in Patre, sine dubio manet in meridie.
